# exercise_selection
_2315 claims from 433 videos, 2020-01-08 → 2025-03-15; merged into 2230 distinct claims. Script output (reduce.py), not edited by hand._

Line format: `- [Nv Y1-Y2 types] claim {numbers by month} cites` — Nv = distinct videos that said it; types P principle, R prescription, O opinion, A anecdote, D data; `?` = low-confidence caption; `also:` = differently-worded variants merged into it.

## [general] compound / muscle / program / need (362)
- [2v 2020-2021 OP] Good mornings are an underrated squat builder; a box squat with the safety squat bar works well for the deadlift {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1194}} {{sFMQj4Lflk0@503}}
- [1v 2020 O] Squat, bench and deadlift are not necessarily the best lifts, but used smartly they are a solid base for any bodybuilding program and most bodybuilders should do them. {{IR-YS-TGGx0@62}}
- [1v 2020 P] Only four movements go to 15 reps: hip thrusts, wide-grip shrugs, Yates rows and Bradford press, because they use light weight for the movement and need extra reps to achieve the desired contraction. {2020-01 {"reps": 15}} {{t_RZT-AkjhE@444}}
- [1v 2020 P] Branch out across movement patterns instead of doing variations of a single lift (e.g. if your compound is weighted push-ups, add close-grip bench and overhead press rather than more push-up variants): it avoids injury, keeps you growing and keeps training fresh. {{4FY2Ct22qXU@313}}
- [1v 2020 P] Each template has a 'competition' and a 'volume' version; the volume day doesn't have more volume or less weight, it just swaps in variations of the main lift so the body can rest. {{cHYOYTgiRlM@124}}
- [1v 2020 P] His first arm day continues with Arnold curls, dumbbell bench flyes, two-arm behind-the-head dumbbell extensions and wide-grip chin-ups for his small lats, then close-grip preacher curls, finger curls and pullovers. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@378}}
- [1v 2020 P] Heavy movements for the rest of the body can go on arm day (rows, close-grip bench, weighted chin-ups, pin presses) if you use a harder variation that needs less weight or a reduced range of motion. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@631}}
- [1v 2020 P] His second arm day continues with overhead pin presses for triceps and delts, barbell wrist curls and heavy seated preacher curls. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@505}}
- [1v 2020 P] Avoid full-range compound lifts like the bench press or overhead press on arm day: they train the arms but are too tiring. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@568}}
- [1v 2020 O] You don't need to camp at the cable machine on arm day; dumbbell movements work fine. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@126}}
- [1v 2020 P?] His second arm day opens with heavy EZ-bar curls, heavy side delt raises, dumbbell extensions, wide neutral-grip pull-ups for the lats, and snatch-grip Pendlay rows for the upper back. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@442}}
- [1v 2020 P] Snatch-grip deadlifts emphasize the lower back and quads, so the SSB box squat covers glutes and hamstrings and the rows and rack pulls cover traps and lats. {{7C5gtGrWYM0@378}}
- [1v 2020 P?] He adds towel-grip work to the row/stiff-leg superset for 4 sets, then snatch-grip shrugs, wide-grip pull-ups and leg raises. {2020-01 {"sets": 4}} {{7C5gtGrWYM0@188}}
- [1v 2020 O] Wisconsin method practitioners drift toward unorthodox lifts because the usable lift pool runs dry and the method depends on the excitement of a fresh lift. {{RaGX9rPQr4k@1483}}
- [1v 2020 P] The Smith machine's real use: advanced lifters targeting specific muscles with heavy weight without worrying about stabilization. {{TrU2m-4yZT0@442}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pick a few compounds that activate the most muscle, move the most weight and warm up for the volume that follows; NH uses squat, bench and deadlift but any compound works. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@126}}
- [1v 2020 O?] He suspects heavy compounds boost hormone production and his ability to add muscle more than lighter lifts like overhead press, but admits it's bro science. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@440}}
- [1v 2020 P] Floor pulls don't suit the method because you can rest as long as you want with the bar on the floor, defeating the purpose; same for any lift where you set the weight down. {{uTHCzF7Exck@256}}
- [1v 2020 O] Machines and cables are for advanced lifters who know what they're doing; novices find them attractive because they're guided but should 'jump in the sea and swim' with free-weight compounds. {{OATtf3kPtdI@187}}
- [1v 2020 P] There is no such thing as a must-do exercise for bodybuilders: do whatever targets the right muscle; only powerlifters must abide by competition lifts. {{Eh0fnXIyDMc@188}}
- [1v 2020 P] Avoid extremes: don't get stuck in the mentality of only barbell movements or only the big three; do what works, not ideology. {{OATtf3kPtdI@1438}}
- [1v 2020 O] What he enjoys about bodybuilding is the freedom: no mandatory lifts or standards, as long as it fits your goals. {{y_lPAdWi4sU@193}}
- [1v 2020 P] A complete physique needs a complete program; bench, squat, deadlift, OHP, weighted pull-ups and rows give good overall development but you still need isolation movements, which the lifting community unfairly hates. {{qdPmmahWTVA@437}}
- [1v 2020 O] Minimalism is the enemy of a good physique; anyone claiming complete results from incomplete methods is lying or missing something. {{qdPmmahWTVA@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't be dogmatic or fanatic about lifts; apart from a few dangerous exceptions, every lift can be useful when applied properly, and you are the engineer who must experiment. {{qdPmmahWTVA@500}}
- [1v 2020 O] Bench, squat and deadlift are not the only or the best compound movements; he likes them because they suit his body type. {{qdPmmahWTVA@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] The big five (adding rows and weighted pull-ups) still leaves the triceps long head, biceps and forearms undertrained. {{qdPmmahWTVA@376}}
- [1v 2020 O] Saying there are no must-do exercises but presenting the bench as king is contradictory; something being better doesn't mean it's the only thing you should do. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@690}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pick exercises with a good stretch-to-risk ratio: ones that stretch fibres differently while staying safe, rather than heavy loads in deep stretch that risk tearing the muscle. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@1449}}
- [1v 2020 O] He won't comment on claims that fat grips improve gains on presses since he has no experience with that. {{m-DWvBP_ncU@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Band exercises such as curls, triceps extensions and face pulls diversify training, feel great on the joints, give a good pump and help stay injury-free. {{8s56W2sjc2g@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] People without integrity find scientific-sounding reasons to justify personal bias against exercises they can't do. {{sQSM1JiaN80@506}}
- [1v 2020 O] Compound-only training is a shotgun rather than a sniper: many muscles get worked, but how much each is worked is unknown, which is bad for physique details. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@126}}
- [1v 2020 P] In compounds, the dominant muscle takes over and drains the growth from the exercise; isolation lets you put all your effort on one muscle. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@252}}
- [1v 2020 P] Isolation movements are less taxing because you move less weight, so you can slow down and focus on how the movement works the muscle. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@315}}
- [1v 2020 O] Many advocates of compounds-only training have glaring imbalances and undeveloped body parts, which shows why isolation matters. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] Running a hypertrophy program with no isolation movements is a big mistake; they're key even for smaller 'show muscles'. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@378}}
- [1v 2020 O] Advising box squats to lifters with too much thoracic rounding in the back squat is a mistake. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@376}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do both: compounds for strength, compound variations (still great for hypertrophy), and isolation for the body parts that need it. {{QwoBxHI7NBU@312}}
- [1v 2020 P] Because compounds are done heavy and intense, you focus on strength rather than feeling the target muscle work. {{QwoBxHI7NBU@62}}
- [1v 2020 P] Compound lifts make it hard to know which muscle is worked because leverages and proportions change activation: some people feel mostly chest on bench, others only triceps or front delts. {{QwoBxHI7NBU@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] Minimalists need to hear that isolation isn't only for arms and shoulders; other muscles important for strength aren't developed enough by compounds. {{QwoBxHI7NBU@249}}
- [1v 2020 P] Activation ratios on compounds are broadly similar between individuals, but differences can still be significant enough to affect gains. {{QwoBxHI7NBU@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] He dislikes the trap bar, but concedes novices who don't care about the conventional deadlift can use it. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@755}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats and trap bar deadlifts aren't better for recreational lifters, just easier; a recreational lifter serious about bodybuilding should do something else. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@254}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you sumo deadlift, pair it with close-stance high-bar squats without a box rather than wide-stance box squats. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@696}}
- [1v 2020 P] You can keep things exciting by changing the equipment rather than the lift, e.g. thick-bar floor press or a different deadlift variation, but then you must recalibrate volume, weight and intensity. {{TwuT80wJql8@311}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't have a favourite lift or method: favouritism is emotional, blinds you to whether it's good for you, and makes people treat critics of their lift as enemies. {{KadH0ZYObS0@312}}
- [1v 2020 O] He has a personal bias against unilateral movements, but admits they help bring up a weak side or imbalances. {{eFhwWirR70o@190}}
- [1v 2020 P] When rotating lifts, match the weight. A technical new lift such as a specialty bar should be added alongside the old one and only replace it once it's heavy enough. {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@317}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't be greedy in programming: wanting 45 lifts leads to a messy catalogue of lifts going in and out, or picked at random on the day. {2020-05 {"lifts": 45}} {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@572}}
- [1v 2020 O] He has never met anyone who built an impressive natural physique mainly with cables, and sees a correlation between heavy cable users and people who don't get big; he uses cables as finishing work for angles and tension. {{CFlRdyLiWOo@561}}
- [1v 2020 O] Method 3, the most common: written rotations triggered by failure or by time (e.g. every 6 weeks). It's rigid and many have made great gains with it, though he deviates from it. {2020-05 {"weeks": 6}} {{wJTax5ziJUY@249}}
- [1v 2020 O] Method 1, total freedom (pick the body part and method on the day), isn't advised for long-term progress, but the right kind of person can still make excellent gains with it. {{wJTax5ziJUY@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Method 2: keep a list of 15-20 lifts per body part and choose on the day which ones to do for the planned body parts. {2020-05 {"lifts": "15-20 per body part"}} {{wJTax5ziJUY@125}}
- [1v 2020 P] In his rotation whole sets come in and out of a workout, sometimes skipped two weeks running, but specificity means a variation is trained elsewhere so he never detrains. {{wJTax5ziJUY@375}}
- [1v 2020 P] The goal of a rotation is to tire the muscle and grow, not to relearn the lift. {{wJTax5ziJUY@375}}
- [1v 2020 O] He doesn't use rep ranges for calisthenics and always does as many reps as possible, since calisthenics doesn't burn you out; all-out pull-ups won't hurt lifting the same or next day. {{mViZdjZIv6s@514}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dumbbell options for nucleus overload include tricep extensions, curls, pullovers for the lats and shrugs {{4rjywMLXt98@757}}
- [1v 2020 P] Moving a lift in and out of the workout is a useful variation hack, but you will not be efficient on a lift you rarely practise {{GClcGq2tIDU@627}}
- [1v 2020 P] Applying very high exercise selection to every muscle creates mayhem: you can't practice each lift enough within one or two weeks, technique suffers and tonnage becomes unstable as you keep relearning lifts. {2020-05 {"weeks": "1-2"}} {{uRWgz3nhqds@314}}
- [1v 2020 P] Your ability to rotate lifts while keeping tonnage constant depends on smart exercise selection. {{uRWgz3nhqds@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] Too large a range (e.g. 55 chest lifts) is confusing, prevents training each movement often enough and is detrimental; too much minimalism is also bad, so strike a balance. {{uRWgz3nhqds@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pick your favorite movements that work for you, milk them with strength work, and use specific variations of them to add tonnage. {{uRWgz3nhqds@377}}
- [1v 2020 P] Strength work alone is fine early on, but later accessories and variations give you more freedom to manipulate volume and tonnage and avoid plateaus {{-6i7xt92Khg@252}}
- [1v 2020 P] Many of his strategies are long-term investments; a newly added lift starts light and costs tonnage, but later accumulates more and adds flexibility {{I1i2wF_BoCY@64}}
- [1v 2020 O] Shocking the muscle is counterproductive; rotating in unfamiliar lifts keeps lowering tonnage while you relearn and makes you a technician of new tricks rather than a master {{KTuKLbl0TOE@249}}
- [1v 2020 P] Exercises that are never replicated in the program progress slowly; keep them if you love them, but do not make the exception the rule {{KTuKLbl0TOE@440}}
- [1v 2020 P] Never put a lift in your program that has no close neighbours; occasional odd exercises are fine as exceptions {{KTuKLbl0TOE@311}}
- [1v 2020 P] Small modifications to a lift are enough to avoid overuse and accommodation; you do not need to jump from barbell to dumbbell bench {{KTuKLbl0TOE@375}}
- [1v 2020 O] Using 5x5 on your main lifts in a minimalist program without variations shoots you in the foot twice {{k3O630Nz4B8@1197}}
- [1v 2020 P] For NO to work you must connect well with the exercise and be able to repeat it again and again; stretch-heavy, easy-to-recover movements are ideal. {{2edX06SQDnU@188}}
- [1v 2020 O] He doesn't promote aggressive lifting rotations because constant switching keeps you outside the specificity window needed for carryover, making progression harder. {{ML5AQa9THHI@377}}
- [1v 2020 O] The more interesting stimulus-to-fatigue question is at the level of individual lifts, e.g. claims that some lifts cause too much fatigue to be worth it {{boPKiKE26Rw@318}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bench press and overhead press should not be rotated with each other: they aren't similar enough in specificity, and their large tonnage difference makes tonnage stability impossible, especially on a 5x5. {2020-06 {"reps": 5, "sets": 5}} {{hi-kNbZnXz0@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Supinated grip stretches the biceps most, neutral the triceps, pronated the lats, though all get some stimulus {{aoVexKzTOt0@204}}
- [1v 2020 P] Use MMC to quickly judge whether an exercise works instead of waiting four to six months to compare progress and measurements {2020-06 {"weeks": "16-26"}} {{Hn3oGdUUhic@128}}
- [1v 2020 A] He keeps more exercises lined up per day than most people and rotates lifts in and out without ever fully removing them, choosing on the day based on the week's tonnage target and the muscles he wants to hit. {{o3RTXWAia4A@65}}
- [1v 2020 O] The objection that deadlift tonnage is dispersed across too many muscles misses the point: compounds disperse tonnage quickly, fire up the body for what follows and show where the rest of the tonnage should go. {{zCo31HKOsIY@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Accessories and variations done in lower intensity windows are where he sculpts the body and targets details: the bodybuilding part of training, which suffers without strength work before it. {{zCo31HKOsIY@253}}
- [1v 2020 P] Synergy is an overarching principle; carryover and specificity sit beneath it as sub-products, letting you think about a program globally so every aspect works together. {{8I6IPBr2_z0@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Fluctuations should come from small incremental additions of variations or volume/intensity tweaks; never overhaul the whole program or pull lifts in and out weekly. {{FtwMg4iM5kA@382}}
- [1v 2020 A] He has always relied on specificity and lifts feeding one another to shock his muscles, and that was all he ever needed. {{FtwMg4iM5kA@382}}
- [1v 2020 O] Olympic lifts build less muscle than typical compounds because they lack a controlled eccentric and are highly technical {{uND20Zam2jI@189}}
- [1v 2020 O] There is no room to modify it toward hypertrophy; it should be reserved for people making powerlifting their main pursuit {{YcHkXwsIOI8@1282}}
- [1v 2020 O] What he opposes is YouTube and forum culture of doing random movements with no plan, ignoring specificity, and claiming variety surprises the body into growing {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@159}}
- [1v 2020 P] People with too high an exercise selection end up in limbo; logging a year of rotation shows marginal or regressed strength on lifts they return to {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@633}}
- [1v 2020 P] Choose a limited set of variations and get variety from what you do with them; people who do everything are good at nothing {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@735}}
- [1v 2020 O] Arnold's view was that a muscle stops responding to the same thing done over and over, which is why he wanted to shock it {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@92}}
- [1v 2020 P] Your joints should not adapt to you; you adapt to your body: some people cannot pull conventional without excess lumbar shear or squat with certain foot positions without knee stress, and leverages cannot be changed {{iCWUcBy7Vxs@187}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bodybuilders and recreational lifters should not do supra-maximal box squats or 1RM good mornings as Westside strength athletes do {{du7JYCA2pa4@744}}
- [1v 2020 O] The belt squat loads the posterior chain while the trap bar deadlift loads the quads more and is not a full hip hinge, whatever EMG data says {{du7JYCA2pa4@186}}
- [1v 2020 P] You want high movement pattern selection with medium to low exercise selection; Starting Strength is low on both {{et6AylDr1uk@64}}
- [1v 2020 P] Compounds diffuse tonnage, save time, pre-fatigue and warm up; isolation movements are special agents delivering targeted tonnage {{x3YHLNqftr4@189}}
- [1v 2020 P] Perfect isolation is not required; other muscles helping is more bang for your buck {{NJd_zD9OeQ4@192}}
- [1v 2020 P] The tryout period teaches you the right angle and grip and whether the lift suits you, since not every lift suits everyone {{nHt2c7PIGME@377}}
- [1v 2020 P] Always try new things, starting light, to see how your body responds {{7lwWsxNXDmo@502}}
- [1v 2020 P] Machines shouldn't be used for low reps: mechanical failure ruins targeting, it's impractical and dangerous {{2ag0uM9zhs4@532}}
- [1v 2020 P] Constantly rotating styles costs practice and makes it hard to build the program around the main lift; put variations in a week 1/week 2 rotation instead {{RgGkGiaGqks@629}}
- [1v 2020 P] Once you've found your style, stick to one squat style (high or low bar) and one deadlift style {{RgGkGiaGqks@566}}
- [1v 2020 O] Partials isolate regions better while full ROM gives more total tonnage {{de4yF1k6kzQ@565}}
- [1v 2020 O] Block pulls often provide more total range of motion than some lifters' full-range deadlifts anyway, so who cares - do what works for you. {{rIngKQqTJMs@1002}}
- [1v 2020 P] Check everything you do for synergy and specificity with the rest of your training; if it doesn't fit, rethink it {{-GYV9s5Ad8s@443}}
- [1v 2020 P] Find each lift's limiting factor and remove it; that's why straps are so good {{-GYV9s5Ad8s@505}}
- [1v 2020 P] Spectrum: front squat on the thoracic extension side, back squat in the middle, SSB on the lumbar side encouraging torso angle shift {{I7vk4Kk5neA@440}}
- [1v 2020 P] Front squat feels like being pulled forward by the shoulders; SSB feels like a hand pressing down on your upper back to fold you {{I7vk4Kk5neA@943}}
- [1v 2020 P] The SSB beats you differently from a straight bar: it actively tries to fold you in half, a different strength curve {{I7vk4Kk5neA@253}}
- [1v 2020 P] Most of your lifts should involve a negative; a majority of positive-only lifts is a mistake {{L5gcFxTG_cU@251}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you need variety you can program it intelligently, e.g. a two-week rotating template, without hurting gains {{HVD5j7hOobI@100}}
- [1v 2020 O] Most of the 'functional training' discourse is stupid: every movement that reinforces a good pattern and strengthens muscles carries over to daily life {{R_Ls6aPU6d0@128}}
- [1v 2020 O] Squat, deadlift and bench are functional training; bench reinforces tucking the shoulders back instead of overusing the rotator cuff {{R_Ls6aPU6d0@840}}
- [1v 2020 P] Gym movement patterns replicate natural human movements; the squat was not invented, resistance was simply added to knee flexion {{R_Ls6aPU6d0@100}}
- [1v 2020 P] Stacking exercises that pre-fatigue the same muscle must be intentional; otherwise it damages progression {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@502}}
- [1v 2020 P] Unilateral movements allow better focus and mind-muscle connection than bilateral ones {{01jOBsrQCPg@440}}
- [1v 2020 A] NH got his best results from compound movements, which made the bodybuilder 'no compounds' approach seem suspicious {{VdMhm70N_6o@40}}
- [1v 2020 O] This disqualifies straight-leg good mornings and squat variations that keep the hip from ever hinging {{u9OoRQOC3G0@350}}
- [1v 2020 P] Muscles that are prime movers in heavy compounds (quads, glutes, chest) don't need isolation; most others do {{MIHPylz3f7Q@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] The trap bar deadlift is a fine pull from the floor but reduces the hip hinge, so pair it with a more hinge-focused lift; it suits athletes who do not want to invest in deadlift technique {{eobG2o3Ww4M@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] Cleans need so much technique and warm-up that they need a specific justification; there are better hypertrophy tools {{sCMGXCXKVjs@690}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pick any exercise for each pattern (dip, seated press, Viking press), but keep choices specific so lifts feed each other; a random mix kills the split's synergy {{1f4aaqgOfXk@320}}
- [1v 2020 P] Isolation for forearms, calves and neck is ranked low since compounds working most muscles come first, but ignoring isolation leaves you looking terrible {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1557}}
- [1v 2020 P] Apply speed back-offs only to main compounds: bench, squat, deadlift, OHP, possibly weighted pull-ups; not rows, not paused variations {{Rz4uzjM068M@879}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dynamic bodyweight movements can be good for hypertrophy if programmed properly {{dfnn-YdXerg@226}}
- [1v 2020 P] Minimalism can be effective when properly put into action {{Lj0m82JUtkM@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Assistance templates that rotate, giving otherwise similar days different exercises and rep schemes, are a smart programming idea {{hT4zgNcMOBY@0}}
- [1v 2020 A] NH could do ring pull-ups and ring rows the first time he tried; anyone with a bodyweight background learns the basics fast {{h-QiKaysok0@188}}
- [1v 2020 P] Choose strength lifts that branch into many variations: bench feeds all bench variations, a triceps extension carries over little {{A-asa7QMiG0@779}}
- [1v 2020 O] NH is not dogmatic about the big three; curls, overhead press or heavy triceps extensions can be strength work {{A-asa7QMiG0@316}}
- [1v 2020 P] Low-technicality hinges like good mornings and RDLs can be practiced less strictly; main lifts like squat and deadlift need regular practice {{6sFaB3rAY1A@694}}
- [1v 2020 P] A rotation is a temporary substitution, not a replacement; a lift must be practiced every 10-12 days or it doesn't belong in the program {2020-11 {"frequency": "every 10-12 days"}} {{6sFaB3rAY1A@632}}
- [1v 2020 A] About 85% of the lifts in his current program were there when he created it; he rarely replaces anything {2020-11 {"percent": "85%"}} {{6sFaB3rAY1A@632}}
- [1v 2020 P] As a novice, keep a lifting variation list of every lift you have practised, ranked by how much you've done it; this is your data bank for programming {{gWdAfpJIXbc@330}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep a separate list of lifts you want to try; introduce new lifts very lightly and accept a tuning phase of up to six months {2020-11 {"weeks": "6 months"}} {{gWdAfpJIXbc@790}}
- [1v 2020 O] Having to swap a lift out means you didn't do your homework when building your lift list {{gWdAfpJIXbc@689}}
- [1v 2020 P] Compounds are attractive because they drive tonnage best, not because they're mandatory {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@773}}
- [1v 2020 P] Squats and deadlifts are self-sufficient and can grow from themselves; bench needs more variation and flies won't carry over to it {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@902}}
- [1v 2020 O] He's not a fan of rotating exercises every 8-12 weeks because it looks like block training, but it's fine for lunges {2020-11 {"weeks": "8-12"}} {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@902}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you test a deadlift max, you need a day dedicated to deadlifting, e.g. open the second lower day with it {{9jAjNyxeiXc@689}}
- [1v 2020 P] High-rated compounds should appear mostly in conventional form, with more variations and frequency, since they drive tonnage; isolation can be trained less often {{6z43GClpmK8@380}}
- [1v 2020 O] No technical lift is necessary; the middle ground suffices unless a lift really inspires you {{6z43GClpmK8@570}}
- [1v 2020 P] Include very technical lifts only sparingly; you can't maintain 15 of them {{6z43GClpmK8@128}}
- [1v 2020 P] You can emphasize heads of a muscle via joint angles, but compounds resist being turned into isolations; do compounds safely, heavy and frequently, then hammer lagging parts with isolation volume {{ZScs2L9rero@2780}}
- [1v 2020 P] A lift's rep tendency depends on its danger and its ability to resist muscular and structural failure {{twgzqTg4nug@507}}
- [1v 2020 P] A knee-flexion lower day cannot be only knee flexion; like never having only horizontal presses, it creates imbalance {{hYFO2eKn-m4@790}}
- [1v 2020 P] Using variations loses some specificity but gains raw progression and injury prevention {{hYFO2eKn-m4@499}}
- [1v 2020 P] Plan a day by first listing the body parts to hit, then choosing the lifts {{ehxjCB_MdKc@189}}
- [1v 2020 P] Never schedule a day without knowing what each exercise is for; program like a recipe, not by throwing ingredients in the pan {{ehxjCB_MdKc@815}}
- [1v 2020 O] You don't have to do conventional deadlifts or mixed grip to prove manhood; some people aren't built for conventional {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@499}}
- [1v 2020 P] Above-knee rack pulls are not a hip hinge anymore and carry over poorly to the deadlift {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@1127}}
- [1v 2021 P] Squats plus stiff-leg deadlifts can be rough on the lower back; rotate in Romanian deadlifts {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@490}}
- [1v 2021 P] A program needs some stretch-emphasis movements such as Romanian deadlifts or dumbbell rows {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@535}}
- [1v 2021 P] For pure hypertrophy he cannot recommend such training; his backyard calisthenics routine is a better realistic option {{ivrGNRW0rwE@376}}
- [1v 2021 P] Lifts near the top should be the most specific to the king lift because they must feed it most {{dyeeKOQJhrM@150}}
- [1v 2021 P] Map every lift you do onto the tree; if you understand it you control it, otherwise it controls you {{dyeeKOQJhrM@439}}
- [1v 2021 P] Only include lifts you have done regularly for at least three months and that agree with you: no painful lifts and no YouTube novelties {2021-02 {"months": 3}} {{rlWIogS2Qho@129}}
- [1v 2021 P] The exercise list is the backbone of the program; without it you cannot program well {{rlWIogS2Qho@64}}
- [1v 2021 O] Low range-of-motion good mornings with huge loads will not train the spinal erectors {{U1zVxzMVM2E@2717}}
- [1v 2021 O] Arm days built only from cable isolation are not how you grow {{K1GVimJowoM@445}}
- [1v 2021 P] Step two: add isolation on top of the compounds, not instead of them; this stage can last a very long time {{K1GVimJowoM@200}}
- [1v 2021 P] Endless exercise variation slows growth because you constantly cycle lifts and never progress; it appeals to people who hate routine, and novices grow anyway until they stall hard {{EP1Qw9aEKOU@193}}
- [1v 2021 P] Paused squats and Romanian deadlifts done often are building blocks for work capacity; RDLs do so without overtaxing strength work {{BBqiphYfqMA@530}}
- [1v 2021 A] NH trains high-bar squat, standard bench and close-stance conventional deadlift because they fit his build, not because everyone should {{S0qBQppUtTQ@720}}
- [1v 2021 P] Tall lifters may suit dips, low-bar squats or sumo instead; the program should adapt to the lifter, not vice versa {{S0qBQppUtTQ@740}}
- [1v 2021 P] Compound movements are the hardest to modify because they carry the most tonnage and open the session {{o05wgvQtSMc@629}}
- [1v 2021 P] If a lift keeps hurting after a fair try, replace it; ten other lifts do the same job, and bodybuilders aren't obliged to do any particular lift {{sFMQj4Lflk0@756}}
- [1v 2021 O] Pauses on overhead press, curls, skullcrushers and pull-ups are less useful: no real technical or confidence benefit and many better variations exist {{MP01uQbcoLc@1241}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pause reps aren't just for powerlifters; bodybuilding should take any tool from any discipline that builds size {{MP01uQbcoLc@62}}
- [1v 2021 P] Paused squats and deadlifts tax the cardiovascular system, so paused deadlifts may need lower reps around 4 {2021-05 {"reps": 4}} {{MP01uQbcoLc@1553}}
- [1v 2021 P] Two identical days in a program are unnecessary; rotate in variations such as dumbbell bench, preacher curls or pause squats {{HH7kCVkRyJc@563}}
- [1v 2021 P] To modify GreySkull, add bodyweight pull-ups on another day, ab isolation at the end of sessions, shrugs after deadlifts (3-4 sets), and lateral raises {2021-05 {"sets": "3-4"}} {{HH7kCVkRyJc@685}}
- [1v 2021 O] Worst bodyweight moves for hypertrophy: muscle-ups, front levers, one-arm push-ups, dragon flags and planche, because they are skill tricks {{HrUPp573ki0@2162}}
- [1v 2021 P] Harder lever variations such as planche push-ups mostly add technical difficulty, not muscular difficulty {{HrUPp573ki0@369}}
- [1v 2021 P] Calisthenics means any movement whose base pattern uses bodyweight, so an unloaded squat or lunge counts as much as a pull-up {{HrUPp573ki0@62}}
- [1v 2021 P] Weighted and bodyweight calisthenics carry over to each other strongly, but the loaded versions do more for strength {{HrUPp573ki0@179}}
- [1v 2021 P] Use variations like jokers, only with a good reason such as breaking a stall or fixing technique (for example pause squats for the bottom position) {{7n8RMhsxEB4@250}}
- [1v 2021 P] A variation implies a main lift it varies from, so you need a specificity tree before you can talk about variations {{7n8RMhsxEB4@62}}
- [1v 2021 P] Putting in many hours means nothing if what you do in the gym is bogus {{YMURbaXNNZM@124}}
- [1v 2021 P] Upper body days should include two presses: one horizontal and one vertical {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@634}}
- [1v 2021 P] Shunning isolation is a minimalist trait inherited from strength athletes; curls after squats do not hurt squat recovery, so do both {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@1703}}
- [1v 2021 P] The deadlift must open the workout, never be third; 3x5 squats plus 3x5 deadlifts in one novice session is too much and an injury risk {2021-06 {"reps": 5, "sets": 3}} {{ZkQZPnTVKpg@501}}
- [1v 2021 O] He hates unilateral work after countless lunge laps in football, but lunges are good for unloading the lower back and suit people who want slimmer, athletic legs {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@2630}}
- [1v 2021 P] Compounds put the most muscle on your frame; do not run a program of only machines and isolation, nor only heavy low-rep compounds {{8ELQ2XZgpls@545}}
- [1v 2021 P] Isolation work is as important as compounds but hits smaller muscles; keep a balance {{8ELQ2XZgpls@1147}}
- [1v 2021 P] Orthodox, conventional training works; do not reinvent the wheel unless a movement causes pain {{8ELQ2XZgpls@1319}}
- [1v 2021 P] Exercise selection dictates how your physique turns out, so it is paramount {{8ELQ2XZgpls@1891}}
- [1v 2021 P] Summary: bodybuilders do conventional plus variations; tall lifters sumo plus conventional variations; beginners trap bar plus conventional variations; athletes trap bar, Jefferson or hack {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2202}}
- [1v 2021 P] When testing a new lift, start with low volume, low intensity and low weight and build up slowly {{lG5SZbRdOjA@250}}
- [1v 2021 P] Tall lifters who struggled with conventional should try sumo; pulling sumo often beats pulling conventional rarely and getting hurt {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1004}}
- [1v 2021 P] Styles that deny the body its best mechanical leverages force more muscular activation; mechanical disadvantage equals more muscle demand {{lG5SZbRdOjA@502}}
- [1v 2021 P] Mechanics means the skeleton's levers; lifts that improve leverage demand less muscular activation, so you trade activation for longevity {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1259}}
- [1v 2021 O] Many lifters overrate the hypertrophy of Jefferson and hack deadlifts because they don't realise how much easier those lifts are {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2329}}
- [1v 2021 O] The trap bar's neutral grip and centred load make it the most natural pull, like carrying groceries or standing up from a chair {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1386}}
- [1v 2021 O] The hack deadlift is the easiest deadlift form since the load is behind you; strongmen lift huge weights this way {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1889}}
- [1v 2021 P] Doing an exercise frequently for a long time and progressing matters more than the exercise's inherent quality {{lG5SZbRdOjA@752}}
- [1v 2021 P] Tall lifters who lose position in the bottom inches of conventional should try pulling from blocks {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2140}}
- [1v 2021 O] Sumo is closer to a squat and targets different qualities; it is not cheating {{lG5SZbRdOjA@815}}
- [1v 2021 P] Athletes can pull in a sport-specific stance but must use submaximal loads {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2266}}
- [1v 2021 P] A minimalist leg day frees room for small muscles: calves, neck, traps, forearms, rear delts and abs belong on leg day {{yvhW-zyhpsM@754}}
- [1v 2021 P] Avoid squats followed by snatch-grip deadlifts; open with the snatch-grip deadlift then do a pause squat; he also dislikes straight squat and deadlift on the same day {{P89o8FBSWDI@2335}}
- [1v 2021 O] Copying sport movements with weights, like throwing uppercuts holding dumbbells, does not build sport-specific power; boxers who do it are training their cardio {{cNGPCn9Uupc@700}}
- [1v 2021 P] Keep the technical difficulty of lifts low for athletes: no Olympic cleans or advanced ring work, because the sport already demands all their skill-learning time {{cNGPCn9Uupc@1130}}
- [1v 2021 P] Hybrid templates add arm, neck, trap and leg isolation work (curls, hammer curls, neck curls, shrugs, skull crushers, Bulgarian split squats) to the Purist base {{cNGPCn9Uupc@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Racket sports template uses Swiss-bar or neutral-grip DB bench 3x6-10, reverse lunges 3x12-15, finger curls 3x6-10, and trap-bar or Jefferson deadlifts 1-3x5 {2021-09 {"reps": "5-15", "sets": "1-3"}} {{cNGPCn9Uupc@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] On lower days Alsruhe uses an explosive antagonist like kettlebell swings rather than an RDL before squats, which is smart because lower-body muscles work together and fatigue each other {{OWbmOI3cuNs@1155}}
- [1v 2021 O] Pairing each main lift with its antagonist is not as necessary for preventing imbalances as people think; benching one day and rowing another works too {{OWbmOI3cuNs@753}}
- [1v 2021 P] In his version the main mover comes first and the antagonist goes in a separate set, the core superset stays, and isolation replaces conditioning {{OWbmOI3cuNs@1155}}
- [1v 2021 P] For hypertrophy, replace the conditioning slot with isolation work {{OWbmOI3cuNs@1029}}
- [1v 2021 P] Look at what athletes do, keep only what matters for aesthetics and throw the rest away {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1630}}
- [1v 2021 O] The hybrid program is tougher on conditioning and recovery but not necessarily better for hypertrophy {{jAnfGqpsK38@1441}}
- [1v 2021 P] Saturday: RDLs 4x8-12 and weighted pull-ups 4x3-5, farmer's carries 3xAMRAP for traps, unweighted reverse rows and banded face pulls for blood flow {{jAnfGqpsK38@1124}}
- [1v 2021 O] Calisthenics and weight options are interchangeable; there are many ways to reach the same physique {{bqcRtC3bbK8@681}}
- [1v 2022 P] If stabilization were all that mattered we would all train on rings; more stabilization demand is not automatically better and stabilizers must not be overtaxed {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@520}}
- [1v 2022 P] The vast majority of training should be free weights; add a machine that suits your biomechanics after the main free-weight movement to sneak in extra tonnage without taxing tendons {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@2007}}
- [1v 2022 O] Older lifters in their 50s-60s tend to favor machines because they are easier on the joints and because their idols used them {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@188}}
- [1v 2022 P] Machine-only training is still a mistake because tendons need to accumulate fatigue to get stronger too {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@869}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machine-only programs made people bad at programming because the machine thinks for them {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@979}}
- [1v 2022 P] Compare two exercises over three months and keep whichever gives more progress, regardless of mind-muscle connection {2022-01 {"weeks": 12}} {{9y2s6kyFkPc@1564}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rotating out an exercise as soon as you plateau teaches you to quit and resets you to newbie neurological gains; rotate weekly by plan or autoregulate instead {{9y2s6kyFkPc@1752}}
- [1v 2022 O] Overhead press, bench, squat and deadlift are fine for hypertrophy; old-school bodybuilders grew with them and most of NH's programs are built on them {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2003}}
- [1v 2022 P] Program Atlas stones as a hip hinge in hybrid strongman training and account for lower back fatigue {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2885}}
- [1v 2022 O] Loading fight movements (dumbbell uppercuts, punching with bands) is misguided; lifting should train muscles in patterns your coach does not already use {{v-F3QHSM2hg@626}}
- [1v 2022 P] Fighters should build purposeful muscles only: every exercise must train a muscle used in the sport, so most isolation work is cut {{v-F3QHSM2hg@437}}
- [1v 2022 P] Unused muscle is a burden for a fighter, needing blood, oxygen and energy; every pound must have a use {{v-F3QHSM2hg@1625}}
- [1v 2022 P] His fighter programs are final versions but meant to be modified; once you understand the spirit and purpose of a program, any change in line with it makes sense {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@55}}
- [1v 2022 P] Training a press builds muscle usable beyond pressing, such as rotating an opponent: carryover is weaker but muscle is muscle {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1139}}
- [1v 2022 P] Even in pull-dominant sports keep training presses for the antagonist muscles, as long as pulling volume is not sacrificed {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1418}}
- [1v 2022 P] The majority of a program's tonnage must come from compounds: they give high intensity ratios and save time {{wLx5BRx8AaU@903}}
- [1v 2022 O] Choosing exercises by what hurts most leads to moves that don't fit your anthropometry and to chasing the pump {{wLx5BRx8AaU@680}}
- [1v 2022 P] Never deadlift as your fourth exercise; open the day with it {{wLx5BRx8AaU@1230}}
- [1v 2022 O] Competition specificity like paused bench, conventional deadlift and ass-to-grass squats makes no sense for someone who doesn't compete {{GNJkAcgUJ_0@6023}}
- [1v 2022 O] Powerlifting-style minimalism strips out exactly what matters for a balanced physique {{GNJkAcgUJ_0@6086}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rotations should never be scheduled (e.g. every two weeks) nor triggered just by a plateau; use them when the muscle needs it {2022-02 {"frequency": "not every 2 wks"}} {{BJXFeQWGUuY@2078}}
- [1v 2022 O] The carcass carries, weighted-vest hikes and sled drags he promotes are about 5% of his training and not very effective for hypertrophy {2022-02 {"percent": "5%"}} {{5ANLeP4QvX8@1574}}
- [1v 2022 P] You cannot sell the basics: compound plus isolation movements are the best way for a natural to train but have no marketing appeal {{5ANLeP4QvX8@1637}}
- [1v 2022 P] True isolation is a myth since muscles work together, but movements do prioritize some muscles and deprioritize others, which is what makes programming possible {{dVssxcEh1UQ@560}}
- [1v 2022 P] A lift that hit every muscle equally would be the worst lift because it couldn't be programmed or matched to recovery {{dVssxcEh1UQ@614}}
- [1v 2022 P] Space heavy, taxing movements; replace heavy compounds with pattern-matched variations that cost the CNS less and give more stimulus per fatigue; use low reps sparingly and train with reps in reserve {{NPlGK0-7h0A@1875}}
- [1v 2022 O] Lifters obsessed with CNS management neglect small muscles and isolation and overfocus on compounds, which paradoxically is what fatigues the CNS {{NPlGK0-7h0A@1751}}
- [1v 2022 P] Be generous with isolation because it taxes the CNS less and recovers faster {{NPlGK0-7h0A@1937}}
- [1v 2022 P] A minimalist cares about six or seven main lifts; he has about 25, so each week off neglects many more muscles and patterns {2022-03 {"lifts": 25}} {{PuIp9cGKMHo@891}}
- [1v 2022 P] Use isolation movements only for NO; compounds cause diffuse fatigue, risk injury through Bulgarian-style overload and become limited by cardio {{LNggSj0WBd8@1699}}
- [1v 2022 A] Sandow trained with strongman lifts and his own dumbbell system; NH plans to turn it into a nucleus-overload circuit template {{KtoAImbVZF4@1138}}
- [1v 2022 P] Remove exercises you do not enjoy, because you end up skipping them anyway and they take the place of something you would actually do; he found one he had not done in 3 months {2022-05 {"weeks": 12}} {{BW-32tYoNxw@125}}
- [1v 2022 P] Don't sandbag your bread-and-butter lift to survive 15 accessories afterwards; put your energy into the lifts you want to improve {{kKX0AUrXzIw@2010}}
- [1v 2022 P] Bodybuilding is using any means necessary to grow; the bodybuilder is an anti-specialist and anti-technique, and hypertrophy is itself a function {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@1449}}
- [1v 2022 P] The worst muscle-building advice is to build a muscle by replicating the performance of someone who has it, which assumes strength equals size {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@125}}
- [1v 2022 P] Time-to-stimulus ratio matters as much as fatigue-to-stimulus; choose exercises that give the most results for the least time {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@821}}
- [1v 2022 O] Bodybuilders are artists, not athletes: they borrow from sports, take only what's needed and reduce it to the minimum {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@1579}}
- [1v 2022 O] By the same logic you'd become a pro cyclist for quads, a swimmer for shoulders and a ballerina for calves {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@946}}
- [1v 2022 A] NH uses some ring movements in his own training but will never become a gymnast {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@1579}}
- [1v 2022 P] Never alternate sets of two heavy lower-body lifts such as conventional deadlifts and hack squats {{QfHfxLYRu88@2010}}
- [1v 2022 P] Combine rings for the upper body with barbells for the lower body; be utilitarian, not dogmatic {{QfHfxLYRu88@3580}}
- [1v 2022 O] Yoke walks are of little use for hypertrophy: heavy loading but no flexion or weighted stretch {{QfHfxLYRu88@441}}
- [1v 2022 P] If functional training really copied daily movements it would be things like lifting bags or opening car doors; instead it picks movements like crawling and rope climbing that people rarely do {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@127}}
- [1v 2022 P] An office worker would gain more from strengthening the lower back and fixing posture; a blue-collar worker's job is already functional, so plain strength training suits them {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@1128}}
- [1v 2022 P] Calisthenics moves aren't more natural than weighted ones; any motion the body can do (deadlift, curl) is natural {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@3146}}
- [1v 2022 P] Muscle and strength built on a dropped lift carry over to a replacement better suited to you {{qrBLykvU_EE@816}}
- [1v 2022 A] He is stubborn and has kept doing lifts that didn't suit him for years; clinging to them is the sunk cost fallacy {{qrBLykvU_EE@816}}
- [1v 2022 P] Put exercises that cause pain or don't suit your build in an 'exercise graveyard' and stop doing them {{qrBLykvU_EE@753}}
- [1v 2022 A] He partly needs many mirror-day exercises simply to avoid boredom with lifts that work for him {{itnjfi6RYtY@1568}}
- [1v 2022 P] You fix a lift pattern rather than throwing the lift away because some people do it badly {{EGCKBEWh8ng@650}}
- [1v 2022 O] A generation following isolation-cable advice will be a generation of small lifters; build big muscles with heavy compounds {{EGCKBEWh8ng@1377}}
- [1v 2022 O] All training is functional for something, just as every philosophy is good for some goal; the real question is which goal {{-PsAWpoj_ho@2163}}
- [1v 2022 O] Constantly switching lifts to chase soreness keeps you readapting neurologically {{yUecX6qONXg@990}}
- [1v 2022 P] On a leg day only heavy knee flexion or hip hinges justify opening the session; keep isolation for the end {{gizylGr0ebo@877}}
- [1v 2022 O] Alsruhe's dislike of isolation is preference, not logic; strongman carries will not fully build arms or traps {{B17sX2hT-5w@322}}
- [1v 2022 P] Treat accessories as priorities, as Basement Bodybuilding says; giant sets exist to give isolation work its place {{nqzVqvMvQ1s@944}}
- [1v 2022 P] After honestly trying a lift and finding it doesn't work for you, you can drop it into your exercise graveyard; the problem is group-think declaring lifts bad {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1392}}
- [1v 2022 P] Deadlifts and RDLs are each inferior alone but strong combined; minimalist routines fail {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1392}}
- [1v 2022 P] Presses are awkward with a rope; do pressing compounds with a barbell {{BayeNJ0NUTE@628}}
- [1v 2022 P] Squat, bench and deadlift are movement patterns; condemning them condemns all their variations (Larsen press, close-grip bench, RDLs, stiff-leg deadlifts, front squats), leaving only machines {{nmgidpn3DNM@1329}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machines let you lift much more load than the free-weight equivalent because the machine does part of the work, so per unit of load they give less of both fatigue and stimulus {{nmgidpn3DNM@885}}
- [1v 2022 P] Gurus swung the pendulum from too much big three to the opposite extreme; the balance is a majority of compound movements completed with machines, cables and isolation {{nmgidpn3DNM@1453}}
- [1v 2022 P] (Guest Basement Bodybuilding) Optimal on paper is useless if you do not enjoy it; but only doing what you are good at leaves weaknesses {{E1er3oQ3IeM@502}}
- [1v 2022 P] Training mostly compound presses builds an already-dominant chest and shoulders and worsens imbalances {{JgAoGDmM4n4@749}}
- [1v 2022 P] An 'or' in his programs means pick one exercise or rotate both across weeks (week A / week B), never both in the same workout {{wB-ydKSfQ5k@255}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pyramid of priority: the first lines of each day (heavy compounds) give the most results; skip the last line's sets if short on time {{wB-ydKSfQ5k@1583}}
- [1v 2022 P] Olympic lifts are concentric-heavy and technique-driven, with a fast descent into the catch, which minimizes the lengthening phase and makes them less optimal for hypertrophy than squats or bench {{FC8x-cwd4DQ@192}}
- [1v 2022 D] Chinese lifters do dedicated bodybuilding work (pull-ups, raises, dips) besides their main lifts; China has won 38 weightlifting golds, over double the USA {{FC8x-cwd4DQ@390}}
- [1v 2022 P] Upper body exercise selection should look like a laundry list, lower body like a Post-it, because lower-body lifts bring more volume and systemic fatigue {{0pKnDucWcDc@251}}
- [1v 2022 P] Seeing time-tested exercises rather than novelty movements in a program is a good sign {{0pKnDucWcDc@251}}
- [1v 2022 P] Calisthenics athletes do lift weights: their own body is the load {{6YWa3qOu6BY@65}}
- [1v 2023 P] Avoid lifting ADHD: rotating many variations for novelty satisfies the brain and kills progression; stick to two variations {{HXoY7xzHlWU@1198}}
- [1v 2023 P] A muscle without a lift targeting it will not grow; on squat/deadlift/row programs the posterior chain outgrows the upper back {{elk4uYvjXos@631}}
- [1v 2023 P] Powerlifting gurus reject exercise variety, but bodybuilding requires developing the whole body with varied lifts {{elk4uYvjXos@127}}
- [1v 2023 P] Exercise choices can be rotated week A/week B (e.g. RDLs one week, block pulls the next) {{upmJaBt1WHk@632}}
- [1v 2023 O] The 'no isolation, just spam chin-ups and bench' advice mostly comes from short lifters for whom it works; it doesn't transfer to taller people {{Z2JQ5n0dbkY@569}}
- [1v 2023 P] Avoid getting very strong on deadlifts, squats, bench and dips, avoid building the traps and obliques, and train legs little or not at all for otter mode {{HOgwZO70z_M@376}}
- [1v 2023 P] Machine- and cable-heavy programs allow much more workload with less joint stress than free-weight-only ones {{aJ1e41y8Na4@1763}}
- [1v 2023 O] Offering sensible alternatives for each exercise is a strength in a program {{aJ1e41y8Na4@693}}
- [1v 2023 P] Since physique reflects the movement patterns you train, you can get similar results by replicating them with resistance training without the technical demands {{rFfLUloDHeg@805}}
- [1v 2023 P] Typical strength programs tack isolation on at the end, where it gets skipped or done tired; that is not hypertrophy programming {{d1eLAd5WvIA@313}}
- [1v 2023 P] After heavy compounds, move to lower-fatigue isolation like weighted sissy squats and Nordics; that last 20% makes the physique {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@1135}}
- [1v 2023 P] Isolation is as important as compounds; moving less weight on curls doesn't mean less stimulus {{Z-hGdKz28RY@1320}}
- [1v 2023 P] Use at most five hard variations per movement pattern {2023-07 {"variations": "max 5"}} {{1TKqwXCge9E@1834}}
- [1v 2023 P] You should be able to justify every variation and its rep range; never add variations just to cure boredom {{1TKqwXCge9E@2335}}
- [1v 2023 P] Hard variations differ in movement pattern (pull-up vs lat pulldown, squat vs leg press); soft variations are near-identical (high-bar squat vs paused high-bar squat) and you can have as many as you like {{1TKqwXCge9E@1899}}
- [1v 2023 P] A variation is a movement on the same line of specificity as another; a rotation alternates two movements in one slot, either on a schedule (week A/B) or by autoregulation for advanced lifters {{1TKqwXCge9E@1389}}
- [1v 2023 P] Specificity: an RDL is close to the conventional deadlift and a split squat is far from it; bodybuilders judge specificity by the muscle trained, not by the lift's carryover {{1TKqwXCge9E@1580}}
- [1v 2023 P] Keep variations few: each one cuts how often you train every lift, so you may lose weight on the bar from forgetting the skill rather than from muscle loss {{1TKqwXCge9E@1771}}
- [1v 2023 P] Only add a variation when a movement keeps stalling despite evolving rep ranges; that shows it needs extra relevant volume from elsewhere {{1TKqwXCge9E@1516}}
- [1v 2023 P] Novices should use 0-1 variations per lift, intermediates 1-2 and advanced lifters 3-5 {2023-07 {"variations": "novice 0-1, intermediate 1-2, advanced 3-5"}} {{1TKqwXCge9E@1960}}
- [1v 2023 P] Mistake two: matching a character's athletic feats doesn't give you his physique; doing 15 pull-ups won't give you the back of someone who can do 15 pull-ups {{ZDWFfYLYVNs@141}}
- [1v 2023 P] An exercise's value is the attributes it builds (e.g. bear crawl trains core, upper back, shoulders), not the movement itself; by that logic every resistance exercise is functional {{ZN5ohmPPfjM@505}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pick exercises you enjoy within each movement pattern because enjoyment aids adherence {{7uBT8vp0lNw@509}}
- [1v 2023 A] His sessions got shorter and he skipped isolation for the big three, which gave him a spider physique with lagging arms {{XK9KjVP-5Wo@628}}
- [1v 2023 P] Rotating connected variations (RDL, good morning, hyperextension) won't detrain the pattern; fear of regressing drives the spam-squats-and-deadlifts mentality {{43F9unmSc_I@1069}}
- [1v 2023 P] Intermediates are prime targets for gimmicky new exercises; stick with time-tested lifts {{16fjHEtZTCA@1525}}
- [1v 2023 P] Don't swing from compound-only to no compounds; keep the lifts that worked best as a novice as pillars of the new program {{16fjHEtZTCA@1462}}
- [1v 2023 P] Bodybuilding alternatives beat the big three on stimulus-to-fatigue, range of motion, tendon strain and CNS fatigue {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@350}}
- [1v 2023 P] Fix 1: don't focus on the big three; bench is just a horizontal press chosen because it is easier to judge than dips {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@1230}}
- [1v 2023 O] Many bodybuilders report their chest or quads grew after dropping the bench or squat; the big three are overrated for hypertrophy {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@380}}
- [1v 2023 P] Even if the big three beat weightlifting lifts for size, they lose to bodybuilding lifts: dips and push-ups over bench, RDLs over deadlifts, hack squats over squats {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@315}}
- [1v 2023 O] Not everyone is built for the squat, bench and deadlift, yet powerbuilding forces them on everyone {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@442}}
- [1v 2023 P] Rotations and variations are B tier: use them against overuse injuries and accommodation, but master a few lifts first and only build a 'B team' after they stall {{SxqjF2oPasg@1069}}
- [1v 2023 O] 'Shocking the muscle' (constant rotation, skipping warm-ups) is D tier Arnold bro-science that blocks long-term progression {{SxqjF2oPasg@1008}}
- [1v 2023 O] Resistance curves are B tier; a bad curve can be useful because it forces you through sticking points {{SxqjF2oPasg@2330}}
- [1v 2023 O] Unilateral movements are A tier; he trains every limb separately to remove compensation {{SxqjF2oPasg@2520}}
- [1v 2023 P] Lifts from mirror days can be interchanged (e.g. Kroc rows on pull 1), but avoid this at the start {{DA7QvIAyptw@1704}}
- [1v 2023 P] Train a lift seriously for at least 3 months before judging it {2023-12 {"weeks": 12}} {{DAZRYoex8_w@950}}
- [1v 2023 P] After 6 months to a year you can rotate exercises weekly, but too much rotation can drop a lift's frequency to once a month, which is not enough {2023-12 {"months": "6-12"}} {{Rc9jhSVGoxM@570}}
- [1v 2024 O] The Gymshark-era powerbuilding style of spamming compounds like sumo deadlift and bench is subpar for aesthetics; those physiques are a byproduct despite the method {{jDk3LytTEjA@329}}
- [1v 2024 P] The idea that isolation gives definition and striations while compounds give size is debunked bro science; striations come from leanness, every resistance exercise grows muscle {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@314}}
- [1v 2024 P] A sword's weight sits far from the hands, creating a long lever arm and momentum, so the exercise becomes about controlling inertia rather than loading muscle {{29XrRmOrCMs@755}}
- [1v 2024 P] Sword swinging fails for hypertrophy for three reasons: poor biomechanics, overuse from repeating one plane of motion, and no progressive overload {{29XrRmOrCMs@254}}
- [1v 2024 D] Horizontal swings are mostly core rotation, and studies on the similar golf swing show underhand swings are mostly lower body (quads, adductors) {{29XrRmOrCMs@443}}
- [1v 2024 O] Adding plates to a sword like Guts does only worsens balance and momentum {{29XrRmOrCMs@1130}}
- [1v 2024 D] Gymnasts also lift weights; biceps, triceps and shoulder isolation is part of their routine to grow those muscles without extra tendon stress {{tbY5VMSmlP8@825}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lesson one: exercises that safely load muscles in their lengthened position give great gains {{tbY5VMSmlP8@890}}
- [1v 2024 D] The big three made up about 80% of BBBS and around 70% of its exercises could be replaced with better hypertrophy lifts {{Ft039H8jzXU@2197}}
- [1v 2024 O] Minimalism works for legs, but BBBS was too extreme, with no neck, abs, forearms or rear delts {{Ft039H8jzXU@754}}
- [1v 2024 P] Specificity is not important for bodybuilding; pick the variations that let you progress {{Ft039H8jzXU@2072}}
- [1v 2024 O] Mediocre powerlifters who use their own physiques as proof isolation is unnecessary are actually proof that it is necessary {{2LoP6-KdEx4@1005}}
- [1v 2024 O] The barbell pullover, drag curl and sissy squat are good bodybuilding lifts, not strength lifts {{jUMmu583w34@186}}
- [1v 2024 P] Mocking hypertrophy training through the iliac pulldown is a strawman; proper hypertrophy training should be mostly heavy compound movements taken to failure {{jUMmu583w34@150}}
- [1v 2024 P] Use big compounds and precise isolations together: compounds alone risk imbalances and overuse injuries, while isolations alone build much less overall size {{jUMmu583w34@1420}}
- [1v 2024 O] Classic hypertrophy staples from 100 years ago are still relevant but have fallen out of favour because of science hype around shinier lifts {{jUMmu583w34@220}}
- [1v 2024 P] Romanian deadlifts, deep pull-ups and deep preacher curls build both mobility and size, and you progress by adding weight and range {{jUMmu583w34@1066}}
- [1v 2024 O] Combined lifts like the hip-thrust-to-floor-press make no sense, because the weight is right for only one of the two movements {{jUMmu583w34@640}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lifts now considered best for hypertrophy share two traits: high stability and a high stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {{jUMmu583w34@1280}}
- [1v 2024 P] Most movements need high stability to be optimal for hypertrophy {{jUMmu583w34@312}}
- [1v 2024 O] Risky stunt lifts with a poor risk-to-reward ratio have no place in a bodybuilding program {{jUMmu583w34@670}}
- [1v 2024 P] If a lift's form breaks down as soon as you load it, so you lose its effective range of motion, it's a garbage hypertrophy lift {{xqjfYhZ-CvQ@1712}}
- [1v 2024 P] Machines let you train hard by offloading stability {{sZntx8mqaH8@377}}
- [1v 2024 P] Try new exercises, but drop any that don't work for you {{JW73oft8Q60@1011}}
- [1v 2024 O] Advanced lifters should refuse to do exercises they dislike; there are enough options to always pick ones you look forward to {{JW73oft8Q60@960}}
- [1v 2024 P] Women benefit just like men from using as many implements as possible {{bRKQIOYo2u4@11719}}
- [1v 2024 O] Finger push-ups are a waste of time for hypertrophy {{bRKQIOYo2u4@8234}}
- [1v 2024 O] Rib cage expansion is unlikely without pullovers {{bRKQIOYo2u4@6568}}
- [1v 2024 P] For MMA fighters, heavy deadlifts twice a week waste energy; single-leg work, Zercher deadlifts or light Jefferson curls carry over better {{okm6bZHKHI4@12967}}
- [1v 2024 P] Compound-heavy strength templates with isolation tacked on create imbalanced physiques, like lots of pressing and only 3 sets of curls, which leads to small arms {{okm6bZHKHI4@12307}}
- [1v 2024 P] An upper body built only from compounds with no arm isolation leads to imbalance {{c7hl9u3h_s0@3387}}
- [1v 2024 P] Never open a day with shrugs before weighted pull-ups; do the most demanding movement first {{c7hl9u3h_s0@8087}}
- [1v 2024 P] For boxers bench isn't functional; prefer ring or deficit push-ups, face pulls for posture, lunges and trap-bar deadlifts {{c7hl9u3h_s0@9732}}
- [1v 2024 O] Top front-body upper exercises: dips, chin/pull-ups, forearm isolation, ab isolation, then a military press {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1713}}
- [1v 2024 P] Avoid convoluted multi-word exercise variants copied from influencers; use normal movements and do less {{c7hl9u3h_s0@4087}}
- [1v 2024 O] He will not learn Olympic weightlifting: too technical and time-consuming, hypertrophy takes all his time {{wBct3Pl8OY4@9990}}
- [1v 2024 P] Low cardiovascular impact matters because a lift should let you reach muscular failure before cardiovascular failure {{6yW3GSskl8g@569}}
- [1v 2024 P] Ease of use ranks 4th: a lift taking more than 3 minutes to set up will end up skipped {2024-11 {"setup": "<3 min"}} {{6yW3GSskl8g@1391}}
- [1v 2024 P] Compound-only makes more sense for legs than for arms, which need isolation to maximize each muscle {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@1449}}
- [1v 2024 O] Street lifting is incomplete for bodybuilding but excellent for the upper body {{hXx-KDH2p28@6133}}
- [1v 2024 P] Novices need 1-2 exercises per muscle and more later, but never more than 4-5; small variations are unlimited {{ZLheQPgEfoA@1974}}

## [general] open / weak / compound / alway (8)
- [2v 2023-2024 OP] Opening with an isolation exercise for a weak point is pointless; isolation suffers less from fatigue and fits after compounds {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@629}} {{c7hl9u3h_s0@7761}}
- [2v 2022 P] Never open a session with an isolation movement: you are freshest and strongest at the start, so use a compound {{wLx5BRx8AaU@100}} {{gizylGr0ebo@314}}
- [1v 2020 O] Calling something weak isn't calling it bad: the lateral raise is weak compared with the deadlift but still useful; you use strong and weak things for different purposes. {{j2u5TzD5fGg@1077}}
- [1v 2020 P] Open the workout with the most strenuous strength compound, not bodyweight pull-ups {{du7JYCA2pa4@249}}
- [1v 2020 P] Always open the training day with your strength work {{DQo0DGn_wI4@150}}
- [1v 2021 O] He is weak at calisthenics but still does them; a hard movement is building muscle {{HrUPp573ki0@2365}}
- [1v 2022 P] Options inside a rotation must target the same muscle in the same way, otherwise you will favour strong points and neglect weak ones {{BW-32tYoNxw@633}}
- [1v 2022 P] Always open with the compound and put isolation after, not before {{0pKnDucWcDc@1065}}

## [general] injuri / overuse / neurological / adaptation (8)
- [2v 2021 P] Specificity training lets you maintain neurological adaptation without repeating the same lift: choose lifts in a line of specificity with the main lift for carryover {{A55c7v1yMn4@1433}} {{8ELQ2XZgpls@1522}}
- [2v 2021 P] As a branch grows with work capacity, it holds more variations, which helps prevent overuse injuries; but 20 exercises is too many and neurological adaptations cannot be maintained {2021-01 {"exercises": "<20"}} {{dyeeKOQJhrM@620}} {{8ELQ2XZgpls@2253}}
- [1v 2020 P] Variations within specificity reduce overuse injuries, prevent imbalances by training different angles, and help bypass the so-called law of accommodation, which volume and intensity manipulation can also bypass {{KTuKLbl0TOE@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Exercise rotations give mental respite by keeping the program fresh and reduce overuse injuries by changing the movement pattern {{7ZS-mGePFXw@188}}
- [1v 2021 P] Training the same back squat and bench at full volume twice a week risks overuse injuries; use a variation (close-grip bench, pause squat) on the second day instead {2021-03 {"frequency": "2x/wk"}} {{8mGHG5C-EPE@688}}
- [1v 2023 P] Stick with one variation of big compounds; isolation lifts can be rotated because neurological adaptation matters less {{gpFdh0eBMGc@623}}
- [1v 2023 P] Soft variations carry over more strength; hard variations hit the muscle differently, may stimulate more growth and help prevent overuse injuries, but each one is a new skill to learn {{1TKqwXCge9E@2273}}
- [1v 2023 P] He uses a broad exercise selection but low variation: soft variations of the same pattern prevent overuse injuries without changing the pattern {{43F9unmSc_I@1821}}

## [general] specificity / repeat / similar / exact (8)
- [2v 2020 P] Strength athletes stay within a range of specificity, using squat variations and accessories that build the main lift without repeating the exact pattern all the time also: 2020-12: Any lift with a similar movement pattern and stance still feeds the main lift, so you don't need to repeat the exact lift for specificity {{KTuKLbl0TOE@0}} {{hYFO2eKn-m4@670}}
- [1v 2020 P] Alternative lifts in a slot should work similar muscles in similar patterns; floor press and paused bench do not. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@315}}
- [1v 2020 P] Within an exercise hierarchy (main, accessory, extra) you can go to 4-6 movements as long as specificity is respected {2020-11 {"movements": "4-6"}} {{6sFaB3rAY1A@507}}
- [1v 2021 P] Exercise menus should stay within the same tree of specificity and similar movement patterns {{A55c7v1yMn4@1246}}
- [1v 2021 P] You can bench and squat twice a week without repeating the same lift: variations stay specific, carry over and may progress better over time {{8mGHG5C-EPE@750}}
- [1v 2021 P] Keeping only the squat cuts the tree of specificity from its roots; variations and accessories feed the main lift {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@3384}}
- [1v 2021 P] Specificity still matters for bodybuilders: keep lifts in a line of specificity so they keep progressing {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@1255}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rotations let you keep working a muscle without repeating the exact same movement {{qrBLykvU_EE@878}}

## [general] delt / long-head / lack / ab (5)
- [3v 2020-2021 O] Boring But Big lacks long-head triceps, rear delt, forearm, trap, calf, neck, biceps and superset ab work also: 2021-03: Novice programs lack curls, triceps extensions, calf, neck, forearm and direct ab work, and GPP {{hT4zgNcMOBY@900}} {{S0qBQppUtTQ@1370}} {{HH7kCVkRyJc@685}}
- [1v 2020 P] Deadlift day 1 ends with wide-grip pull-ups, hyperextensions, grippers for forearms and ab training. {{7C5gtGrWYM0@314}}
- [1v 2020 O] Workout A lacks long-head triceps work, rear and side delt work (only front delts via bench) and upper back, and has no calves. {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@752}}
- [1v 2021 P] Add curls, long-head triceps extensions and rear delt isolation; they cost little recovery, help prehab, and can be supersetted {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@349}}
- [1v 2024 P] Bodyweight-only training usually lacks traps, side delts, forearms and posterior chain; add them {{c7hl9u3h_s0@8403}}

## [general] group / muscle / few / antagonistic (6)
- [2v 2021-2023 OP] An intermediate should already have more than one lift per muscle group {{A55c7v1yMn4@1183}} {{DA7QvIAyptw@945}}
- [1v 2020 P] Structure an arm day wide to narrow: start with many muscle groups, then as sets go on work fewer muscles with more isolation, ending on the ones you care about most. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@315}}
- [1v 2020 O] Military press, bench, weighted pull-up and Pendlay row are pretty much all the compound movements you need for overall development, apart from a few muscle groups. {{rucwL-SIefg@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Strength work can use any lift, but big compound movements make the most sense because they warm up more muscle groups {{A-asa7QMiG0@283}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pair antagonistic or unrelated muscles; supersetting similar muscle groups is advanced and he rarely does it {{XncJr9uMpWo@289}}
- [1v 2022 P] Exercise rotations are his main volume-control tool: have interchangeable exercises for different muscle groups that can be switched in or out each session {{BJXFeQWGUuY@1641}}

## [general] front / hold / don't / rack (6)
- [2v 2020 O] If you don't front squat, front rack holds are useless: the technical mastery needed isn't worth it since the benefits mostly carry over to front squats. {{wiuVyCccWC4@0}} {{8I6IPBr2_z0@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Carryover is limited because the torso angle shifts forward at the bottom of a squat, which holds at the top don't train; the same applies to box squats in front squat position. {{wiuVyCccWC4@125}}
- [1v 2020 P?] Do front rack holds for time, not reps; NH does 4 sets of about 3-5 seconds, and sees no point going beyond 10 seconds. {2020-06 {"reps": "3-5 s holds", "sets": 4}} {{wiuVyCccWC4@498}}
- [1v 2020 O] He dislikes power cleans for hypertrophy and novices: very technical, need a solid front rack, most people's elbow and wrist positions are garbage, and you cannot reverse engineer them without learning the clean first {{et6AylDr1uk@503}}
- [1v 2021 O] Pull-ups barely carry over to the deadlift since they don't involve the posterior chain {{pvpf7Yuayco@1630}}
- [1v 2021 P] Deadlift followed by front squat works because front squats load the lumbar spine less; an RDL after is fine in higher reps with controlled range {{P89o8FBSWDI@2335}}

## [general] compound / essential / program / bodybuild (5)
- [2v 2020-2022 P] Compound movements are essential; good novice programs are mostly compounds with isolation added {{QwoBxHI7NBU@0}} {{uYRIWAKlA0c@1189}}
- [1v 2020 P] No lift is essential: a novice who dislikes squats or deadlifts can use another knee flexion or hip hinge; novice programs wrongly groom everyone into powerlifters {{MCUpClPnPkQ@751}}
- [1v 2020 P] Programs built mostly from isolation movements are bad by definition; compounds must make up the bulk for bodybuilding because they accumulate more tonnage {{DQo0DGn_wI4@100}}
- [1v 2021 P] Compound movements are the best for hypertrophy and strength, but you can't build a program from only compounds {{S0qBQppUtTQ@126}}
- [1v 2023 P] Natural bodybuilding is mostly compounds plus isolation, but isolation is treated with the same priority and seriousness as compounds {{d1eLAd5WvIA@564}}

## [general] compound / replace / cake / complement (5)
- [2v 2024 P] Isolation should complement, never replace, compounds, like cable flies can't replace heavy pressing for chest {{6p2x4kDDjzs@4040}} {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@1575}}
- [1v 2020 A] He chose compound lifts that agree with him first, then picked variations and accessories that complement them {{KTuKLbl0TOE@187}}
- [1v 2020 P] Machines and isolation are important for a physique but must not replace compound movements {{TD9SB9Bt45o@253}}
- [1v 2020 P] Isolation practice doesn't replace compounds; heavier, safer compound work gives more tonnage, and lagging small muscles can be hit afterwards {{u9OoRQOC3G0@254}}
- [1v 2022 O] Isolation without compounds is like the cherry without the cake {{nmgidpn3DNM@1516}}

## [general] smith / machine / use / never (6)
- [1v 2020 P] Never squat in the Smith machine. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@505}}
- [1v 2020 O] He doesn't hate machines: if you like the feel of a machine, use it. {{0yIieHQjEiA@629}}
- [1v 2022 P] Advanced lifters can use the Smith machine for rows and front squats because balance is taken care of {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@2056}}
- [1v 2022 O] Advanced lifters use machines as a complement, never as the foundation; staying in the machine 'baby playground' means never learning fatigue management {{nmgidpn3DNM@1011}}
- [1v 2024 P] Bodybuilders compete on a stage, not on implements, so use every tool: calisthenics, barbells, dumbbells, kettlebells, Smith machine {{bRKQIOYo2u4@4835}}
- [1v 2024 A] Lifts he has dropped: standing curls, most free-weight triceps work (now mostly cables and Smith), the bench press, and the overhead press {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@5092}}

## [general] hip / grow / hinge / heavy (6)
- [1v 2020 P] If you can, do a heavy knee-flexion movement and a heavy hip hinge from the floor (any variation); you need them to grow. {{CFlRdyLiWOo@623}}
- [1v 2020 O] Two hip hinges on one day is worse for bodybuilding than pairing a knee-flexion movement with a hip hinge {{hT4zgNcMOBY@890}}
- [1v 2020 O] Saying deadlifts are unnatural is wrong; picking something up from the floor in a hip hinge is more natural than a horizontal press {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@62}}
- [1v 2021 P] Build big legs with hip hinges and knee flexions of any kind; you can grow huge legs without squats or deadlifts but never without a knee flexion and a hip hinge {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@1314}}
- [1v 2022 P] Any movement grows the muscles it uses, so rope work also grows forearms and upper back {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@3513}}
- [1v 2023 O] Heavy squats and deadlifts are mostly not the best for hypertrophy {{aJ1e41y8Na4@1763}}

## [general] boring / make / program / powerlifter (6)
- [1v 2020 O] Boring But Big is a powerlifting program, not a hypertrophy program; it will make you look like a powerlifter {{hT4zgNcMOBY@942}}
- [1v 2021 P] Cutting ~20% of the Big 3 volume to add diversity would make programs better and less boring {{S0qBQppUtTQ@1379}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machines are boring; boring exercises get skipped, and unless you do a movement consistently you cannot progress on it {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@1300}}
- [1v 2022 O] Bench, squat and deadlift were used by bodybuilders before powerlifting adopted them; doing them doesn't make you a powerlifter {{GNJkAcgUJ_0@5018}}
- [1v 2022 P] Trade two or three isolations for one compound to make a program more compact {{gizylGr0ebo@2934}}
- [1v 2024 P] Stability ranks 6th: bodybuilders choose boring, stable lifts over flashy ones because the goal is to look impressive, not to lift impressively {{6yW3GSskl8g@1139}}

## [general] band / neck / unnecessary / giant (5)
- [1v 2020 P] If he has energy left on day 1 he does a giant set of band work, chin-ups, push-ups, side raises and band front raises, then band curls for biceps tendon health, neck training, very high-volume calves and abs. {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@255}}
- [1v 2020 P] A bodybuilding version needs rows, traps, wrist extensions, upper back, calves, biceps (curls, chin-ups) and neck work {{oLm1CDuY9zg@1245}}
- [1v 2020 P] Superset to save time: incline dumbbell press with neck curls, dips with overhead triceps extensions, or a giant set of dips, extensions and neck curls {{Lj0m82JUtkM@360}}
- [1v 2021 O] Bands and chains are unnecessary for hypertrophy {{8ELQ2XZgpls@3959}}
- [1v 2021 P] Do not use loaded barbell compounds as finishers; replicate them with bands, like banded overhead press for shoulders, or bodyweight hyperextensions for the hip hinge {{P89o8FBSWDI@168}}

## [general] count / joint / muscle / compound (5)
- [1v 2020 P] A compound movement is any movement involving several joints and recruiting a lot of muscle (dips and push-ups count), not only squat, bench and deadlift. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Isolation movements are effectively more precise, focused versions of compound movements, with fewer joints and muscle groups involved. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] The more muscle a compound recruits, the more diffuse the stimulus, so some muscles you think you're hitting barely get worked {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@623}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lifting more total weight on compounds does not make them better for hypertrophy of a specific muscle, because the load is spread across several muscles and joints {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@376}}
- [1v 2024 P] A different rep range on the same lift counts as a variation {{c7hl9u3h_s0@6814}}

## [general] feed / every / choice / evaluate (5)
- [1v 2020 P] Every lift in a program needs other lifts feeding into it; a lift standing by itself is a bad choice and shouldn't be there. {{8I6IPBr2_z0@65}}
- [1v 2020 O] Deadlift progress feeds every other hinge; carryover to the squat is real but weaker than people think {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1880}}
- [1v 2021 P] Evaluate every lift by what it feeds, the tonnage it gives, and the risk if you fail {{sFMQj4Lflk0@1632}}
- [1v 2021 P] One variation can feed several main lifts, for example good mornings feed both squat and deadlift; every lift in a program is connected {{7n8RMhsxEB4@375}}
- [1v 2023 O] Flexibility in exercise choice day to day is a big plus of bodybuilding {{d1eLAd5WvIA@3943}}

## [general] serve / calisthenic / compound / both (5)
- [1v 2020 A] He does compound movements and calisthenics, but both serve hypertrophy {{RgGkGiaGqks@313}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hyperextensions 4x10-12 serve as active recovery, then standing calf raises 4x12-15 {2022-06 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@1000}}
- [1v 2022 P] Mixing weights and calisthenics gives the best of both: a statue-like upper body plus targeted work for neglected arm and leg muscles {{6YWa3qOu6BY@764}}
- [1v 2023 P] Calisthenics lacks isolation, so lagging muscles like triceps are hard to bring up; use calisthenics compounds for bulk work and free weights to finish {{_P0-kYyVMn4@65}}
- [1v 2023 O] The big three are a historical construct; dips, good mornings and front squats could serve just as well as compound basics {{SZEjCsuclx8@1904}}

## [general] selection / exercise / exercis / minimalist (5)
- [1v 2020 P] Be wary of minimalist exercise selection; if your development is limited, add exercises {{MIHPylz3f7Q@1742}}
- [1v 2020 O] Two or three exercises per day is very low exercise selection and accumulating sets on them risks overuse injury {{hT4zgNcMOBY@840}}
- [1v 2021 P] If the exercise selection is rotten, the whole program stinks {{rlWIogS2Qho@170}}
- [1v 2021 O] Minimalists cut exercise selection and workout length the way cost-cutting consultants slash budgets, chasing performance {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@1579}}
- [1v 2023 O] Exercise selection is only B tier: like soulmates, many exercises can do the job {{SxqjF2oPasg@3212}}

## [general] free / machin / let / path (5)
- [1v 2021 P] Free weights let you arrange body and bar around each other; machines force one path {{pvpf7Yuayco@460}}
- [1v 2021 P] Lifting weights, with bodyweight or free weights, is superior to other forms of resistance training for growing muscle {{8ELQ2XZgpls@380}}
- [1v 2022 O] There is no clear winner between machines and free weights; free weights are probably better for muscle, but machines are not inferior in every respect {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@150}}
- [1v 2022 P] Machines restrict freedom: they lock you into one path and one function of a muscle, whereas free weights let you train all its functions {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@930}}
- [1v 2022 P] Free weights let you adapt your body around the bar; they take practice but you will lift for 10 years anyway {{nmgidpn3DNM@760}}

## [general] motion / vary / specificity / understand (5)
- [1v 2021 P] Varying range of motion within the line of specificity, with partials and deficits, is smart and something good strength coaches understand {{OWbmOI3cuNs@1522}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machine-only lifters tend to skip legs and ego lift with partial range of motion {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@1605}}
- [1v 2022 P] Specificity matters less for bodybuilding than for weightlifting; varying exercises is fine as long as each hits the target muscle {{FC8x-cwd4DQ@521}}
- [1v 2023 P] Cutting range of motion on a third press (dip machine) or doing strict t-bar rows can be smart in context {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@1508}}
- [1v 2023 P] Understanding lines of specificity lets you add tons of variety to PPL without damaging progression {{DA7QvIAyptw@1199}}

## [general] toward / bias / steer / weightlift (4)
- [1v 2020 O] NH is biased towards barbell movements; the program uses no machines and keeps isolation work light. {{xFVva4-swKc@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] There is no true isolation movement; other muscles always assist, but you can bias work toward one muscle. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Sandbag carries with elbows high train thoracic extension like a front squat; they load less, so they lean toward GPP while farmer walks lean toward strength {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1255}}
- [1v 2024 O] Olympic weightlifting lifts can be steered toward hypertrophy {{bRKQIOYo2u4@10898}}

## [general] swap / same / muscl / exercis (4)
- [1v 2020 P] His two arm days share almost no exercises but train exactly the same muscles; to emphasize triceps, swap curls for a bench accessory or extensions. {{gVVj9QuCyFM@505}}
- [1v 2021 P] Upper-body days should have more exercises than lower-body days because the upper body has more small muscles, while lower days dominate in total tonnage {{yvhW-zyhpsM@442}}
- [1v 2022 P] Swap exercises only for ones hitting the same muscles; changing the pattern creates a different giant set with a different result {{QNxCVZro9jc@250}}
- [1v 2022 P] You may swap exercises if the replacement trains the same muscles the same way (e.g. Z press or Arnold press for dumbbell press) {{wB-ydKSfQ5k@1896}}

## [general] understand / outside / swap / three (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Outside the three main lifts and their variations, novices shouldn't swap exercises unless they understand variations and muscle activation, since not every lift is equivalent. {{_4wWgin1dVY@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] Outside powerlifting's dogma of judging strength by three lifts, there's no basis to say the big three are best for developing any muscle. {{qdPmmahWTVA@63}}
- [1v 2021 O] If you understand what a lift does but not what it does to its neighbours, you do not really understand the lift {{dyeeKOQJhrM@900}}
- [1v 2023 P] Exercises can be swapped for preferred options, but pick one and keep it; don't swap lifts every week {{Rc9jhSVGoxM@570}}

## [general] partial / aren't / pin / behind-the (4)
