- [1v 2020 O] Partial pin presses, hack/behind-the-back squats, pistol squats with bands etc. would all build muscle in a novice but aren't beneficial for building a base. {{QpTHtmI8nrU@63}}
- [1v 2020 O] Trap bar and conventional deadlifts aren't the same; try both. He doesn't like the behind-the-back (hack) deadlift at all. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@696}}
- [1v 2020 O] Odd lifts and partials may not be the best muscle builders, but they aren't useless. {{Cn38kgCdOhw@824}}
- [1v 2020 P] Partials such as pin presses and rack pulls let you lift more weight and target muscles the full lift underuses {{de4yF1k6kzQ@439}}

## [general] sumo / conventional / pain / stance (4)
- [1v 2020 P] If fixing form doesn't remove the pain, try switching stance or style; many people who can't deadlift conventional feel great pulling sumo and get extra lower back work elsewhere. {{Eh0fnXIyDMc@125}}
- [1v 2020 O] Almost everyone should find some stance (sumo, Jefferson, conventional) to pull safely from the floor. {{-wk3jSpm434@574}}
- [1v 2021 P] If you are tall and can high-bar and pull conventional without pain or form breakdown, keep doing them {{3nyuEtmqL-I@230}}
- [1v 2021 P] For bodybuilding he doesn't recommend sumo block pulls or sumo deficits; sumo is the main strength lift and other pulls stay conventional {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2140}}

## [general] strong / lifter / simply / already (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Lifters like him who already have strong leg drive and use a lot of quads in the deadlift do not need deficit pulls; it only reinforces an already strong habit. They need more hip-hinge work or simply more deadlifts. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@62}}
- [1v 2020 P] Deficit deadlifts help lifters who lack leg drive learn to push with the legs and hip hinge properly. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@47}}
- [1v 2020 P] Torso mass comes simply from heavy compounds like rows, pull-ups, bench and presses that everyone already does; arms are more complicated {{Tfa-eM5LUV0@313}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lifters' best body parts match the lifts they love and are strong at {{Z-hGdKz28RY@2011}}

## [general] chang / schedul / rotat / program (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Rotating lifts means changing exercises within a program without changing the program as a whole; it should always be scheduled, not done by feel. {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Rotating similar lifts lowers overuse injury risk by changing joint angles but reduces practice on each lift {{-anA4CV2SVU@1000}}
- [1v 2021 P] Variations and rotations are scheduled changes to movement patterns that keep progression going; there is nothing magical about either {{7n8RMhsxEB4@0}}
- [1v 2022 P] He never changes his exercise roster; keep 6-7 favorite lifts per muscle in rotation for consistent progress; same program for 4 years {2022-06 {"lifts": "6-7"}} {{QfHfxLYRu88@3393}}

## [general] respect / decide / must / well-written (4)
- [1v 2020 P] You can't decide on the day to swap bench for weighted push-ups; a well-written hypertrophy program must be respected. {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@128}}
- [1v 2020 P] The principle of specificity matters for bodybuilders, less than for powerlifters, but must still be respected, and lifting rotations are tied to it. {{uRWgz3nhqds@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Decide exercise selection before programming so lifts are synergistic, never on the spot {{a4TKJvbCAf0@686}}
- [1v 2021 P] The top of each branch is your most important lift, chosen subjectively (e.g. deadlift for the hinge), and the hierarchy below it must be respected {{dyeeKOQJhrM@125}}

## [general] shock / injury / mean / progres (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Throwing in a movement pattern you never otherwise train to 'shock the muscle' means no progress on it, possible injury and wasted time. {{8I6IPBr2_z0@127}}
- [1v 2020 O] The way most people shock the muscle is stupid (doesn't build muscle) and dangerous (can cause injury). {{FtwMg4iM5kA@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] Four curl variations and one bench variation means you will not progress {{DQo0DGn_wI4@127}}
- [1v 2022 P] You cannot shock the muscle; switch exercises for boredom or injury avoidance, not for growth {{5ANLeP4QvX8@1767}}

## [general] constantly / relearn / rotat / injury (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Rotating a huge exercise selection to avoid injury means constantly relearning lifts and not reusing or fatiguing the same fibers {{7dqBu5KLIoQ@189}}
- [1v 2020 O] People who constantly rotate lifts keep relearning them and aren't really progressing {{6z43GClpmK8@65}}
- [1v 2021 P] Constantly rotating exercises makes you relearn movement patterns and stall; variation is fine only if it stays specific to the key lifts {{THV7B11oalk@510}}
- [1v 2022 P] If an exercise works for you and rarely causes injury, repeat it constantly instead of chasing variety {{BW-32tYoNxw@823}}

## [general] skill / ring / take / technicality-to (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Technicality-to-hypertrophy ratio: some lifts grow muscle but take too much skill time to be worth it, and rings are one of them {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1444}}
- [1v 2020 O] Bodybuilders only need the low end of ring skill; you do not need iron crosses or planches to grow {{h-QiKaysok0@100}}
- [1v 2020 P] Elite ring skills cost leg size and tonnage; easy ring variations still give good hypertrophy {{6z43GClpmK8@634}}
- [1v 2021 P] Advanced bodyweight lifts take hundreds of hours of skill; every calisthenics lift can be replicated with weights more easily {{HrUPp573ki0@1320}}

## [general] functional / whether / average / person (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Squats are more functional than pull-ups for most people; the vertical pull is one of the least functional movements for the average person {{u9OoRQOC3G0@100}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bodybuilders do what makes muscle grow, whether it is natural or functional or not {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@123}}
- [1v 2022 P] No exercise is functional overall; low body fat is functional for calisthenics athletes' sport, not in general {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@1508}}
- [1v 2022 O] Functional fitness is an entry point; most people will eventually want to specialise {{B17sX2hT-5w@2376}}

## [general] time / frequency / progres / higher (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Keep conventional deadlifts at 5 reps or fewer; RDLs suit higher reps; the deadlift takes little time and does not need high frequency to progress {2020-10 {"reps": "<=5"}} {{sCMGXCXKVjs@440}}
- [1v 2020 P] Smart exercise selection does the work: RDLs and flies naturally suit higher reps; don't do flies for 3-5 {2020-12 {"reps": "not 3-5"}} {{twgzqTg4nug@507}}
- [1v 2021 P] Minimalism works for leg day: squats and deadlifts make beginners grow; reduce frequency and add variations over time {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@2699}}
- [1v 2021 P] Do not train the big barbell lifts all the time; for progress and longevity you need other movements and respect for recovery {{8ELQ2XZgpls@1110}}

## [general] optimal / 75 / marginally / program (4)
- [1v 2020 P] In training nothing is truly optimal; an exercise you like that is 75% optimal beats a 100% one you will not do, because the 75% is actually realized {2020-10 {"optimality": "75% vs 100%"}} {{uL-pwl2nfa0@64}}
- [1v 2021 P] Compounds must make up the vast majority of a natural's training; a program that is 75% isolation is garbage, and that is how enhanced pro bodybuilders train {2021-02 {"isolation": "75%"}} {{hDAcqWHzrJE@490}}
- [1v 2022 P] If you love a lift and it works, stick with it rather than switching to something marginally more optimal {{gYyqqbs2SLI@252}}
- [1v 2023 O] There is no conventional deadlift in the program because it is not optimal for hypertrophy (C tier); RDLs or barbell rows instead {{gpFdh0eBMGc@561}}

## [general] frequency / round / intensity / program (4)
- [1v 2020 P] The lifts are the skeleton of a program; volume, intensity and frequency are built around them, not the other way round {{gWdAfpJIXbc@373}}
- [1v 2021 P] Before choosing a template or frequency, build your exercise list; all programming variables (volume, intensity, frequency) apply to movement patterns {{dyeeKOQJhrM@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Build any good program around the lifts first, then set volume, intensity and frequency; choosing a 5x5 and then a lift is backwards {2021-06 {"reps": 5, "sets": 5}} {{7n8RMhsxEB4@313}}
- [1v 2021 O] Most programs for people wanting a V-taper and round shoulders lack these movements and are only squats, deadlifts and benches {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@503}}

## [general] flexion / hinge / knee / hip (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Follow the strength lift with an antagonist pattern (knee flexion then hip hinge), performed with exaggerated, as-deep-as-possible range and light weight {{sK78-yohV1s@802}}
- [1v 2021 P] Picture lifting as a tree whose branches are movement patterns (knee flexion, hip hinge, vertical pull...); neighbouring branches like knee flexion and hip hinge are more related than distant ones like hinge and vertical pull {{dyeeKOQJhrM@63}}
- [1v 2021 P] An almost perfect leg day starts with squats followed by Romanian deadlifts, covering knee flexion and hip hinge and about 80% of your bases {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@376}}
- [1v 2021 P] Beginners do better with one knee flexion and one hip hinge variation per day than three lower-body lifts {{P89o8FBSWDI@2283}}

## [general] evolution / likely / resistance / calisthenic (4)
- [1v 2021 O] Resistance training most likely started as calisthenics; weight training is its natural evolution {{HrUPp573ki0@1489}}
- [1v 2021 O] The Jefferson deadlift likely sits between trap bar and sumo in difficulty, with heavy quad involvement and less spinal loading {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1828}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machines are not better just because they are more recent; they are a different way to move weight, not an evolution of free weights {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@270}}
- [1v 2022 P] There are no 'fake muscles' from weights versus 'real muscles' from calisthenics; all training is resistance training {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@3208}}

## [general] prime / mover / stabilizer / protect (4)
- [1v 2022 P] Most people who cite 'stabilizer muscles' to defend free weights cannot explain what they are; stabilizers are small muscles that guide and protect the prime movers and tendons {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@330}}
- [1v 2022 P] Few biceps curls or rear-delt isolations: the aim is to build prime movers, not to maximise hypertrophy {{v-F3QHSM2hg@1814}}
- [1v 2023 P] The bigger a compound's prime mover gets, the more it steals work from small muscles, so neglecting isolation early creates deficits that are hard to fix later {{elk4uYvjXos@756}}
- [1v 2023 O] Stability is B tier: unstable lifts like heel-elevated squats or ring push-ups can be excellent, and stabilizers protect you from things like slipping on ice {{SxqjF2oPasg@1258}}

## [general] tricep / accessori / skull / crusher (3)
- [1v 2020 P] A chest/shoulders/triceps day should open with bench and bench variations, then add accessories and isolation for muscles presses under-train: skull crushers for the triceps long head, overhead press, front and side raises, rear delt work. {{4FY2Ct22qXU@251}}
- [1v 2020 P] Accessories: skull crushers for the triceps long head, hammer curls for forearms, hyperextensions for lower back health and hamstrings, chin-ups for biceps; keep the pull-ups because they're great for back, spine and shoulder health and not taxing. {{_4wWgin1dVY@693}}
- [1v 2020 P] Build the day on compound movements because they move a lot of weight, involve many muscles, accumulate tonnage quickly and drive progression, then add isolation like flyes, triceps extensions and rear delt work {{A4KNy_uMCRM@316}}

## [general] everyth / depend / cover / muscl (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Compound lifts don't cover everything: which muscles they train depends on your leverages, and a whole universe of muscles around them gets no work. {{RaGX9rPQr4k@1421}}
- [1v 2020 P] Depending on individual, stance and grip, compounds emphasise some muscles and may give absolutely no growth in others they supposedly train. {{qdPmmahWTVA@125}}
- [1v 2021 P] Bodybuilders should not neglect the deadlift: it puts mass on everything, covers the posterior chain and works the core {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@823}}

## [general] paus / deficit / swap / spoto (3)
- [1v 2020 P] If you swap a main lift (e.g. front squats, dips, Jefferson deadlifts), swap its accessory accordingly (paused/pin front squats, Jefferson block pulls); default accessories are paused squats and deficit deadlifts. {{_4wWgin1dVY@568}}
- [1v 2020 P] Week 2 of template 2 swaps in incline bench, deficit deadlifts, dips and dumbbell bench, and fewer squat sets to protect the lower back {{HVD5j7hOobI@438}}
- [1v 2020 P] Good close variations: paused squat for squat, paused or deficit deadlift for deadlift, paused bench, close-grip bench or Spoto press for bench {{Rz4uzjM068M@314}}

## [general] space / athletic / through / energy-expensive (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Compounds give the most size in the least time, are hard but necessary, and improve balance and the ability to move through space with weight. {{QpTHtmI8nrU@378}}
- [1v 2020 O] In a bodybuilding context, 'athletic' means moving your body through space: a pistol squat or push-up is athletic, a squat or bench is not. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@1511}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do not superset two energy-expensive compounds such as RDL with chin-ups; give them their own space {{6p2x4kDDjzs@5174}}

## [general] morning / includ / replace / whichever (3)
- [1v 2020 P] The fix is to do both: replace one squat session with good mornings. Good mornings help many people's squat, including his, adding strength and allowing more volume. {2020-03 {"frequency": "1 squat session/week replaced"}} {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@312}}
- [1v 2020 P] Only replace deadlifts with rack pulls if you have to; between good mornings and stiff-leg deadlifts pick whichever you can do safely. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@758}}
- [1v 2021 P] Every lift can be replaced, including squat, bench and deadlift (for example with dips, lunges and good mornings); choose lifts by their purpose and only do ones you enjoy {{0RoULYC_tao@188}}

## [general] twice / once / novic / week (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Block pulls suit those with lower back issues and can be a deload that lets lifters pull more than once or twice a week, but novices don't need them. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@758}}
- [1v 2021 P] Having two versions of each day adds variation and lowers overuse injury risk, but suits advanced lifters more than novices {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@250}}
- [1v 2021 P] Minimalism is ideal for lower body: pick one knee flexion and one hip hinge, do them once or twice a week, and add variations {2021-06 {"frequency": "1-2x/wk"}} {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@1440}}

## [general] hyper / hurt / reverse / hyperextension (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Adding a few sets of reverse hypers or hyperextensions at the end of a program doesn't hurt. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@758}}
- [1v 2020 P] Back extensions, reverse hypers and hyperextensions are good lower back work if done as a hip hinge without rounding the lower back {{QLUXXk0OtX4@374}}
- [1v 2020 O] Cutting all pulls from the floor out of a program hurts progress {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@311}}

## [general] attention / span / tree / shorter (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Lift rotation is becoming more popular because people have shorter attention spans, but most do it wrong. {{TwuT80wJql8@187}}
- [1v 2021 P] Isolation movements belong on the tree at the bottom of branches; a bodybuilder needs them but they get less attention than compounds {{dyeeKOQJhrM@400}}
- [1v 2021 O] Channels built around one gimmick, especially endless exercise-selection videos, feed beginners' attention spans without making them bigger {{NVs5F-0e3dk@125}}

## [general] dips / underrat / rotation / regular (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Keep rotations among close variations (e.g. close-grip vs regular bench); dips and bench are too different to share a rotation, so rotate dips with pause dips. {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@509}}
- [1v 2021 P] Treat pause reps as variations complementing touch-and-go main lifts; as a main lift only in rotation (day A regular, day B paused); deadlifts are always from a dead stop {{MP01uQbcoLc@1429}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dips and pull-ups are underrated for hypertrophy {{okm6bZHKHI4@5545}}

## [general] powerlift / bodybuild / bodybuilder / king (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Saying bodybuilders do not need specificity is condescending; bodybuilding is as intellectual and strategic as powerlifting {{KTuKLbl0TOE@63}}
- [1v 2021 O] Compound lifts belong to bodybuilders too; 1950s bodybuilders used them before powerlifting existed {{S0qBQppUtTQ@1780}}
- [1v 2024 O] The deadlift is the king of powerlifting lifts, not of bodybuilding lifts {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@317}}

## [general] paus / volume / joint / intensity (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Shock the muscle within the principles of volume, intensity and specificity: use variations closely resembling your lifts but with different joint angles, tempos, pauses or bars. {{FtwMg4iM5kA@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Eventually you will need variations, different volume and intensity styles, pauses and some partials, but not something new every week {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@374}}
- [1v 2021 O] Paused bench and paused squat are the best pause exercises; they tax the joints less, allowing more reps, volume and frequency {{MP01uQbcoLc@993}}

## [general] stick / three / same / big (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Sticking to the big three and their variations hammers the same muscles over and over {{7dqBu5KLIoQ@128}}
- [1v 2020 P] Olympic lifters don't curl and powerlifters stick to the big three because maintaining mastery leaves no time {{6z43GClpmK8@506}}
- [1v 2023 P] Any lift can be swapped for another in the same family; 'or' means pick one and stick with it (novice/intermediate) or rotate weekly (advanced) {{gpFdh0eBMGc@188}}

## [general] isolation-only / small / stay / plateau (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Big-three-only training leaves you with an unaesthetic body, while isolation-only bros stay small and weak and can never catch up in tonnage {{x3YHLNqftr4@251}}
- [1v 2020 P] Isolation-only training sends little tonnage to small muscles; machine-only guys stay small unless enhanced {{Of7VDakRks4@567}}
- [1v 2023 O] Isolation-only bodybuilding programs are bad and lead to plateaus {{d1eLAd5WvIA@564}}

## [general] power / clean / have / player (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Doing power cleans after deadlifts in phase 3 is better since you are warmed up, especially thoracic extension {{et6AylDr1uk@757}}
- [1v 2020 O] Football coaches should have players deadlift instead of power clean {{et6AylDr1uk@567}}
- [1v 2021 O] Power cleans have no place in a novice program and add nothing for hypertrophy {{HH7kCVkRyJc@501}}

## [general] hip-hinge / worth / knee-flexion / hybrid (3)
- [1v 2020 O] The SSB squat is a knee-flexion/hip-hinge hybrid worth trying even for pain-free straight-bar squatters {{I7vk4Kk5neA@563}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pick two knee-flexion and two hip-hinge variations and rotate them, opening alternate leg days with a different one {{gizylGr0ebo@2117}}
- [1v 2024 O] He is not a fan of straight-arm pulldowns, and hybrid movements like the Bradford press are not worth it {{ZLheQPgEfoA@5764}}

## [general] sport / carry / joint / everyday (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Squats and deadlifts carry over to sport better than farmer's walks, where the joints are locked {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@1069}}
- [1v 2021 O] Jefferson and trap bar carry over best to sports and everyday life {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1889}}
- [1v 2022 P] Never load a kick, punch, takedown or choke; instead pick lifts that reproduce joint angles found in the sport {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@291}}

## [general] order / exercis / list / ones (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Order exercises by importance: the lower on the list, the less important, because you want to be freshest for key lifts and the last exercises get skipped most {{cGzlMRCxvxo@863}}
- [1v 2021 P] Before choosing a template, list exercises you have actually done (not ones you like) as trees of specificity, ordered from most to least specific; the longer the list, the more advanced you are {{G7rVTseabFU@460}}
- [1v 2022 P] Order exercises by priority, with the ones giving the most tonnage first {{wLx5BRx8AaU@984}}

## [general] rating / snatch / clean / technical (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Bodybuilders shouldn't do full snatches or clean and jerks; use block or partial variations that lower the technical rating {{6z43GClpmK8@442}}
- [1v 2021 P] The pure bodybuilding program contains no Oly lift technique at all, only plain compounds and isolation, so its technical rating is very low {{jAnfGqpsK38@64}}
- [1v 2021 P] The hybrid program applies both dehybridization and bastardization, keeping clean and snatch variations but never the full lifts {{jAnfGqpsK38@150}}

## [general] raise / top / calf / invert (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Always open the workout with strength work on a big compound movement pattern such as squat, deadlift, bench, good morning, overhead press, curl or dips, not a lateral raise, calf raise or leg extension {{ehxjCB_MdKc@250}}
- [1v 2021 O] Top 5 bodyweight moves for hypertrophy: pull-up, inverted row, push-up, leg raise, rings {{HrUPp573ki0@1990}}
- [1v 2021 P] Avoid hybrid lifts like squats ending in a calf raise or knee raise; balancing at the top of a heavy lift is dangerous {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@63}}

## [general] unlike / vari / strength / laundry (3)
- [1v 2021 O] Machine strength varies little whether done at the start or end of a workout, unlike free weights {{-anA4CV2SVU@1638}}
- [1v 2022 P] Upper-body days should look like a laundry list of varied exercises {{JnWWva91QUM@566}}
- [1v 2023 O] Specificity is C tier for bodybuilders, unlike strength athletes {{SxqjF2oPasg@3212}}

## [general] upper-body / horizontal / calisthenic / winn (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Bro splits push too much isolation; an upper-body day with no horizontal or vertical press is pointless {{hDAcqWHzrJE@460}}
- [1v 2021 P] Horizontal press, horizontal row and vertical press is the winning combo for an upper day {{8mGHG5C-EPE@937}}
- [1v 2021 P] Calisthenics movements are very good for upper-body hypertrophy {{HrUPp573ki0@1113}}

## [general] natural / driv / by-product / workout (3)
- [1v 2021 P] For naturals, programming drives muscle building; exercise selection is a by-product {{K1GVimJowoM@0}}
- [1v 2022 P] Naturals should skew toward compounds; a 70/30 isolation-to-compound ratio as here is typical of pros but wrong for naturals {{gizylGr0ebo@1423}}
- [1v 2022 O] (Guest GVS) Boring workouts build more muscle than epic ones; Instagram workouts are useless because novelty drives clicks {{E1er3oQ3IeM@63}}

## [general] well / develop / monster / push-up (3)
- [1v 2021 O] Calisthenics develop the front well: chest, shoulders (with handstand push-ups), triceps and abs {{pvpf7Yuayco@2546}}
- [1v 2022 P] Olympic lifting still develops the upper back, shoulders and legs well, but it cannot explain the aesthetic upper bodies {{FC8x-cwd4DQ@255}}
- [1v 2022 O] Weighted chin-ups can build a monster back and decline ring push-ups grow chest and shoulders as well as incline presses {{6YWa3qOu6BY@764}}

## [general] baki / program / want / whether (3)
- [1v 2021 P] If you want lower body growth, follow the Baki program instead; intermediates who want to get big should follow the Tokita Ohma program {{-U6X37OXuLA@124}}
- [1v 2023 P] Whether a lift is good depends on what you want from it in that program {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@1508}}
- [1v 2024 P] Ideal program: train presses like Baki and complement with isolation work {{2LoP6-KdEx4@1005}}

## [general] zercher / choic / fun / athlete (3)
- [1v 2021 O] Zerchers make a good variation but not a main squat unless you are an athlete such as a wrestler; Zercher squats and deadlifts need lower back rigidity and core strength novices lack {{P89o8FBSWDI@1267}}
- [1v 2024 P] Training your weak link only makes sense if that quality serves your goal; a strongman needs a strong Zercher hold, a bodybuilder does not {{JW73oft8Q60@840}}
- [1v 2024 O] Zercher lifts are fun but there are better choices for hypertrophy {{bRKQIOYo2u4@2622}}

## [general] isolat / misuse / dodge / people (3)
- [1v 2023 P] Machines are great for isolating muscle and sparing tendons with little weight; most people misuse them with too much weight and no range of motion {{43F9unmSc_I@2008}}
- [1v 2023 O] Risk-to-reward ratio is C tier because people misuse it to dodge excellent lifts like the good morning {{SxqjF2oPasg@879}}
- [1v 2023 P] Isolating a muscle always makes it grow, whatever the reason for isolating it; that's why gymnasts and calisthenics athletes have big arms {{M2yqxp4IHXg@1885}}

## [abs] plank / crunch / core / brace (24)
- [1v 2020 O] He doesn't dislike cable crunches but never got anything from them; the angle feels weird. {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@689}}
- [1v 2020 O] RDLs without deadlifts leave the lower back less developed than it could be; heavy compounds and twice-weekly high-rep overhead pressing train the core plenty. {2020-06 {"frequency": "2x/week"}} {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@1172}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dumbbell side bends are not very good; do side knee raises instead {{NiV6lE0XUH8@1342}}
- [1v 2020 O] A rigid core is always valuable, even if some floor-pull muscles are less useful for fighting {{PFQ30yaL1t0@1193}}
- [1v 2021 O] Standing side bends with a dumbbell or plate don't train the obliques; the load is moved by spine, back and hips {{f-XiVNy25tw@1090}}
- [1v 2021 O] Floor crunches focus on the brace not the stretch and are not the best; any core exercise emphasising the stretch is good {{f-XiVNy25tw@2861}}
- [1v 2022 P] Russian twists suit BJJ because you must rotate the torso while sitting or on your back {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1460}}
- [1v 2022 P] Turkish get-ups train balance, core and thoracic spine rigidity for grapplers {2022-01 {"reps": 3, "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1258}}
- [1v 2022 O] Planks belong in programs for bracing; he puts them in clients' programs although he no longer needs them {{yoROLGGUPXU@2270}}
- [1v 2022 P] Planks are hard to progress, lack stretch and are redundant with bracing on compounds, so he programs them on upper body days {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1265}}
- [1v 2022 P] If you're too strong for planks, do them at the end of the workout in a giant-set circuit {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1265}}
- [1v 2023 P] Beginners who can't brace may split volume with planks (e.g. 3 stretch + 3 plank sets); advanced lifters get enough isometric work from heavy compounds {{7KDB5wzgY4c@446}}
- [1v 2023 P] The exercise is the sit-up: pelvis and feet fixed while the whole spine flexes and extends; floor, decline bench, GHR all fine {{7KDB5wzgY4c@320}}
- [1v 2023 O] Crunches only flex the thoracic spine with no stretch and are worthless for hypertrophy {{7KDB5wzgY4c@383}}
- [1v 2023 O] Standing cable crunches become a noob trap where body weight does the work; kneeling cable crunches fix this and are one of the best ab exercises {{JSOvpTDcSvA@1257}}
- [1v 2023 O] Planks teach novices to brace, but they become redundant, the arms tire first, and compound lifts already give isometric core work {{JSOvpTDcSvA@378}}
- [1v 2023 O] Windshield wipers waste about half their range of motion and are hard to load; Russian twists are better {{JSOvpTDcSvA@2136}}
- [1v 2023 O] Ab twister machines lock the hips and force unnatural spinal twisting; twisting should involve the hips and feet {{JSOvpTDcSvA@1195}}
- [1v 2023 O] Crunches are bad mainly because of tiny range of motion and minimal stretch, not because of lower back danger {{JSOvpTDcSvA@378}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bicycle crunches look hard and tax cardio but give poor results; don't train to impress others {{JSOvpTDcSvA@2074}}
- [1v 2024 P] Abs left to the end of the session usually get sandbagged or skipped; move them earlier, for example between two presses {{okm6bZHKHI4@3811}}
- [1v 2024 P] Suitcase deadlift barely taxes the low back, is grip-heavy, builds forearms and heavily works the obliques {{qvWxIzhT1QU@127}}
- [1v 2024 D] With 50 lb in the backpack the long-lever plank was very hard; a normal plank can easily take 100 lb {2024-12 {"load": "50 vs 100 lb"}} {{qvWxIzhT1QU@317}}
- [1v 2024 O] Standard push-up-position planks put a lot of emphasis on arms and shoulders rather than the core {{qvWxIzhT1QU@317}}

## [abs] rais / leg / decompres / hang (3)
- [3v 2020-2021 OP] Superset leg raises with pull-ups or rows on the back day to decompress the spine {{Lj0m82JUtkM@875}} {{hYFO2eKn-m4@1126}} {{qDH088UUiMA@685}}
- [2v 2021-2024 P] Russian twists are hit or miss; side twists and oblique raises on a hyperextension bench let you play with range of motion also: 2021-10: For obliques, use Russian twists or side leg raises hanging or on a bench, which stretch them {{f-XiVNy25tw@1126}} {{ZLheQPgEfoA@8689}}
- [2v 2024 OP] Leg raises are best replaced by knee raises: easier to load and control {{c7hl9u3h_s0@7698}} {{ZLheQPgEfoA@8751}}

## [abs] abs / ab / regularly / pick (5)
- [1v 2021 P] The best ab variation is the one you do regularly that stretches the abs; pick one or two and do them {{f-XiVNy25tw@2902}}
- [1v 2022 O] Decline bench crunches are probably the best ab exercise: the angle maximally stretches the abs, especially the lower abs, and they are easy to load or drop-set {{P0SI2703w-I@447}}
- [1v 2022 O] Leg raises have a bad reputation on YouTube for supposedly not training the abs biomechanically, but anyone who has done them feels the abs work hard {{P0SI2703w-I@508}}
- [1v 2023 P] Don't pick more than two ab exercises; spinal flexion trains the whole core anyway {{7KDB5wzgY4c@1074}}
- [1v 2023 P] Rejecting ab exercises because they recruit hip flexors is wrong; muscles work together and the hip flexors move the body so the abs can stabilise and stretch {{JSOvpTDcSvA@62}}

## [abs] sit-up / decline / ab / stretch-bas (4)
- [1v 2022 P] Medicine ball slams using hip and core rotation are more specific than decline sit-ups; ab, neck and forearm work can be moved to other days {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2112}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machine crunches lead many to overuse the lower back; prefer stretch-based ab work like leg raises or decline sit-ups {{JnWWva91QUM@540}}
- [1v 2022 O] Decline sit-ups weighted for 10-15 reps are the best ab exercise for hypertrophy {2022-09 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{JnWWva91QUM@1290}}
- [1v 2023 P] For details: windshield wipers for obliques, decline sit-ups with rotation for serratus, leg/knee raises with pelvic movement for lower abs {{7KDB5wzgY4c@1011}}

## [abs] crunch / floor / abs / rais (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Train abs more, but avoid crunches, which compromise the lower back by rounding at the top; do leg raises on the floor or from a bar with a straight body instead. {{IqCdPJopcC4@312}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg raises from a bar beat floor leg raises because on the floor you feel the hips a lot and don't get the stretch at the bottom. {{egBEW3Iaiak@126}}
- [1v 2020 O] His favorite lower ab exercise is decline-bench crunches, letting bodyweight stretch the abs at the bottom. {{egBEW3Iaiak@251}}

## [back] upper / barbell / posterior / chain (144)
- [1v 2020 P] His main deadlift day uses conventional deadlifts and the second uses snatch-grip deadlifts: still a pull from the floor, arguably harder, but a different stimulus that lets his body recover. {{cHYOYTgiRlM@187}}
- [1v 2020 D] The 'Bugenhagen row' is a mix between Kroc rows and Dorian rows done without posting up (no supporting hand), named after Eric Bugenhagen, who recommends not posting up when rowing. {{S-GpCI3vCCY@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] He avoids pulling conventional from the floor too often because he stagnates; on day 2 he uses snatch-grip deadlifts for higher reps (4-8) since they feel natural to him. {2020-01 {"reps": "4-8"}} {{7C5gtGrWYM0@314}}
- [1v 2020 P] Day 2 continues with towel grip, Yates rows from the rack (partial), wide-grip pull-ups and rack pulls for lats and traps, then calves and hyperextensions. {{7C5gtGrWYM0@378}}
- [1v 2020 O] Daily max rows fry the grip without straps and turn into cheat rows as you chase PRs; strict curls become cheat curls; the usable lift pool ends up small. {{RaGX9rPQr4k@852}}
- [1v 2020 P] Back hyperextensions are the best lower-back isolation: progress from bodyweight to plates, dumbbells, then a barbell to make the lower back 'bulletproof'. {{IqCdPJopcC4@249}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hyperextensions train the lower back, hamstrings and glutes while stressing the muscles without stressing the spine. {{IqCdPJopcC4@249}}
- [1v 2020 O] Reverse hypers are another good lower-back exercise if your gym has one. {{IqCdPJopcC4@312}}
- [1v 2020 P] Skipping deadlift negatives leaves a portion of the movement untrained; doing slow-negative deadlifts from the rack on another day fills that gap. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@248}}
- [1v 2020 P] The deadlift is a posterior-chain exercise, not a grip or forearm exercise; straps let you focus it on back and legs. {{sT8z3ENnqAQ@63}}
- [1v 2020 A] NH can do deadlifts in 40 minutes with a top set in the mid-400s (lbs); matching that posterior-chain work without deadlifts would add about two hours. {2020-02 {"load": "mid-400s lbs", "time": "40 min"}} {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@377}}
- [1v 2020 O] For bang-for-your-buck posterior chain hypertrophy nothing beats deadlifts, despite being positive-only with no bottom stretch. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@377}}
- [1v 2020 O] Even though he loves conventional deadlifts, he rejects the dogma that you're a coward if you don't pull conventional; it's only the best if you can get into position for it. {{Eh0fnXIyDMc@318}}
- [1v 2020 A] He tried sumo deadlift for eight months to a year, it never felt right, so he dropped it and went back to conventional and snatch-grip deadlifts, which feel amazing. {2020-02 {"weeks": "~35-52"}} {{Eh0fnXIyDMc@382}}
- [1v 2020 P] Properly done Pendlay rows are strict with an uncontrolled negative, so no stretch at the bottom; Coan/cheat rows (cheat up, control the negative) stretch the back, important for yoke. {{np9LdwTPfNs@1016}}
- [1v 2020 O] Without back problems and with plenty of pull-ups for spinal decompression, a bear-mode program doesn't need hyperextensions. {{np9LdwTPfNs@1140}}
- [1v 2020 P] For lifters with a fried lower back, choose weighted pull-ups over Pendlay or Coan rows after deadlifts. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@1193}}
- [1v 2020 P] Chin-ups are the exception: the angle and the fact that the back is what fully extends make them safe, especially bodyweight chin-ups. {{1roZlKwps8Y@190}}
- [1v 2020 A] Snatch-grip deadlifts also use more quads, but he keeps them because they develop his upper back so well that the trade-off is worth it. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] One-arm dumbbell rows after cable rows are fine because the unilateral, against-gravity pull works a different plane. {2020-05 {"reps": 12, "sets": 3}} {{CFlRdyLiWOo@313}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pendlay rows are popular with his subscribers but not a movement everyone needs; 5x5-8 is a good range for rows. {2020-05 {"reps": "5-8", "sets": 5}} {{rucwL-SIefg@1229}}
- [1v 2020 O] If you do low-rep rows, Pendlay rows and dead-stop cheat rows are the best options. {{0yIieHQjEiA@506}}
- [1v 2020 P] The deadlift fatigues most people through tendon and mechanical fatigue because they cannot maintain thoracic extension, not because it is a bad lift {{boPKiKE26Rw@381}}
- [1v 2020 O] He does not encourage high-rep deadlifts, for other reasons than cardio {{boPKiKE26Rw@381}}
- [1v 2020 P] Alternating deadlifts and bent-over rows isn't rotating lifts but rotating programs; with proper form you should row barely half your deadlift, so tonnage discrepancy is even worse. {{hi-kNbZnXz0@315}}
- [1v 2020 P] Front rack holds don't train the legs; they train the core and upper back statically for rigidity and stability. {{wiuVyCccWC4@249}}
- [1v 2020 O] Prioritize vertical pulls; they decompress the spine, valuable for a rugby player compressed in scrums {{u10c43TRDb0@501}}
- [1v 2020 P] Not everyone can deadlift conventional with a neutral spine even with good mobility; tall lifters with poor leverages often thrive switching to sumo and train hamstrings and lower back with accessories {{rsbqqfM6ZFM@380}}
- [1v 2020 P] Good mornings with knee bend replicate losing thoracic extension in a squat; training that position makes your upper back hold up better on normal squats. {{FtwMg4iM5kA@129}}
- [1v 2020 P] Without floor pulls, add hyperextensions for lower back and hamstrings {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@1500}}
- [1v 2020 O] Dead-stop rack pulls for 7 reps at RPE 8 are a good range and let you reset and focus on form each rep {2020-07 {"reps": 7, "rpe": 8}} {{YcHkXwsIOI8@436}}
- [1v 2020 O] He prefers rack pulls from below the kneecap; the higher the block the less specific, and above the knee it is no longer a hip hinge and does not carry over to the deadlift {{YcHkXwsIOI8@436}}
- [1v 2020 P] Barbell rows after deadlifts and overhead press pile another tonnage bomb on the posterior chain; use cable rows instead {{QKpAtCK6oPQ@814}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pendlay rows (dead stop from the floor every rep, negative not controlled) are the toughest rows; 6-8 reps suits them {2020-08 {"reps": "6-8"}} {{jSUmn33lmlI@248}}
- [1v 2020 O] Horizontal rows rank fairly low among movement patterns but are great for posture and teach holding a neutral spine {{jSUmn33lmlI@312}}
- [1v 2020 P] Machines isolate upper back well but always doing the same movements can overwork certain parts {{2ag0uM9zhs4@847}}
- [1v 2020 A] He deadlifts conventional in a 2-5 rep window because he can always tell if his form is off {2020-08 {"reps": "2-5"}} {{pUpQypTV3Ck@375}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pause deadlifts off the floor reinforce a tight start and engage abs, lower back, glutes and hamstrings more than Dimel deadlifts {{RgGkGiaGqks@1069}}
- [1v 2020 P] Partial deadlift variations are fine for sticking points but for hypertrophy go deeper with full ROM {{RgGkGiaGqks@1007}}
- [1v 2020 P] Letting the small forearm muscles limit the much bigger hamstrings, glutes and back is idiotic; nobody holds a back squat with the hands {{-GYV9s5Ad8s@820}}
- [1v 2020 P] Tall lifters should try sumo deadlifts: neutral back, more tonnage, less lumbar damage even if less posterior chain {{qlIicZePKtU@660}}
- [1v 2020 O] Deadlifts to the knees make little sense since the sticking point for most is past the knees; do full reps unless using rack pulls or deficits for a specific purpose {{oLm1CDuY9zg@444}}
- [1v 2020 P] Two deadlift variations in one day makes no sense; stick to one and progress it {{-banYSTnkq4@1001}}
- [1v 2020 P] Superset bench with rows to fit horizontal pulls; dumbbell rows standing on both feet are best when you do not want long warm-ups or lower back strain {2020-09 {"reps": "6-12", "sets": 3}} {{HVD5j7hOobI@123}}
- [1v 2020 P] Rope climbing trains biceps and forearms; depending on grip and wrist/elbow flexion it becomes more limb-dominant, with lats and upper back always involved {{ZdcKQXMmmPY@813}}
- [1v 2020 P] Deadlift machines don't load the spinal erectors and posterior chain like real deadlifts; use a barbell {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1162}}
- [1v 2020 O] Horizontal rows shouldn't be strength work because they require warmed-up legs and lower back {{TD9SB9Bt45o@720}}
- [1v 2020 O] Ring rows are great but hard to load (a 10 kg vest isn't enough), which is why NH does seal rows {2020-10 {"load": "10 kg"}} {{MIHPylz3f7Q@1677}}
- [1v 2020 P] Snatch-grip shrugs (his Nippard shrugs) 10-15 reps; build up grip width gradually or start light; gives a mid-back stretch you have never felt {2020-10 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@2096}}
- [1v 2020 P] Stiff-legged deadlifts put the back parallel to the floor and target the mid back more than the traps; 6-10 reps for 4 sets {2020-10 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 4}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1098}}
- [1v 2020 P] Rack pulls touch-and-go for 8-12 to emphasize the upper back; with lower reps the first rep becomes the hardest {2020-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": "3-4"}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@2033}}
- [1v 2020 P] Barbell rows fit well on upper days because the lumbar spine and posterior chain are not pre-fatigued from squats or deadlifts, lowering injury risk, though they need extra warm-up {{dHXPhjaWBWk@189}}
- [1v 2020 O] Ring rows are a very underrated upper back movement and solve the difficulty of training the back with bodyweight {{dfnn-YdXerg@377}}
- [1v 2020 O] Inverted rows are the most underrated upper-back movement; changing grip and pull width lights up different parts of the upper back {{h-QiKaysok0@770}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you do six deadlift variations, the deadlift should be your strength work because it feeds them most {{A-asa7QMiG0@819}}
- [1v 2020 D] Chinese weightlifters deadlift all the time and Russians use snatch-grip deadlifts {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1694}}
- [1v 2020 P] Barbell rows fit well on upper days because you aren't fatigued from squats or deadlifts; 4x8-10 is a good range {2020-11 {"reps": "8-10", "sets": 4}} {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@463}}
- [1v 2020 P] Chest-supported dumbbell rows are much harder than normal rows because you can't use your legs; if you max out the dumbbells, add fat grips {2020-11 {"reps": "10-12"}} {{9jAjNyxeiXc@251}}
- [1v 2020 O] The deadlift trains the upper back less effectively than most think and ignores whole portions of it {{ZScs2L9rero@1490}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you cannot pull from the floor safely, use sumo or block pulls to raise the danger zone {{UzTfF1VduxM@900}}
- [1v 2020 O] Overhead pressing has almost no impact on weighted pull-up strength, so the pull-ups can still be taken near failure {{DukfPhcoYZE@140}}
- [1v 2020 P] Placing pull-ups between bench and incline presses improves press performance by giving rest and helps the shoulders feel better {{DQo0DGn_wI4@375}}
- [1v 2020 P] The deadlift should come first and be programmed as strength work {{DQo0DGn_wI4@1180}}
- [1v 2020 P] Leaning-back sternum pull-ups turn the pull-up into more of a horizontal pull that hits the upper back harder {{hYFO2eKn-m4@400}}
- [1v 2020 P] Use both neutral and pronated grips for overuse-injury prevention and complete upper-back training; they can be supersetted without much mutual fatigue {{YDTUYiKFcFo@580}}
- [1v 2020 P] Block or rack pulls below the knee allow more load and recruit more upper back, though not more posterior chain {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@1063}}
- [1v 2021 O] If you can do 5x5 Pendlay rows you're sandbagging early sets; they also tax the lower back after squats {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@535}}
- [1v 2021 P] RDLs do not represent the deadlift on an intensity day; you need a heavy deadlift style that emphasizes the concentric {{-anA4CV2SVU@126}}
- [1v 2021 P] A shrug done hip-hinged forward is a vertical pull that hits the mid back {{-anA4CV2SVU@316}}
- [1v 2021 P] Reworked pull day: deadlift 1x5, pull-ups, Meadows rows supersetted with face pulls, EZ curls supersetted with triceps, calves or abs, optional bodyweight chin-ups as a finisher {2021-01 {"reps": 5, "sets": 1}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@656}}
- [1v 2021 O] The chest-supported T-bar row hurts his sternum, and after a pulldown there is nothing left to offload from {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1380}}
- [1v 2021 P] Deadlift once a week: 1x5, 3x3, or 1-3 sets of 2-5 {2021-03 {"frequency": "1x/wk", "reps": "2-5", "sets": "1-3"}} {{1MOLCv6ojgw@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Ring rows are easy to recover from; do them bodyweight and elevate the feet for difficulty; pull with the back, minimize arm involvement {{1MOLCv6ojgw@320}}
- [1v 2021 P] Prefer dumbbell rows over barbell rows for a better stretch from hip stability; torso angle shifts emphasis between traps and lats {{1MOLCv6ojgw@265}}
- [1v 2021 P] Bodyweight horizontal rows: 3 sets AMRAP every two days or 5 sets AMRAP every four days {2021-03 {"reps": "AMRAP", "sets": "3-5"}} {{1MOLCv6ojgw@0}}
- [1v 2021 O] Barbell rows fit as a strength lift, work as a hip hinge and carry over to the deadlift {{8mGHG5C-EPE@1441}}
- [1v 2021 O] Doing barbell rows after squats and bench costs a re-warm-up, but the rest lets the lower back recover so you row with a solid lumbar spine; a price worth paying {{8mGHG5C-EPE@248}}
- [1v 2021 O] The front squat is the best lift for building thoracic extension {{pHNNbwju0GU@656}}
- [1v 2021 P] Good back day example: deadlifts 1x5 or 3x3, weighted pull-ups 3x5 or 3x3, dumbbell or seal rows 4x12, shrugs or high pulls 4x15 {2021-05 {"sets": "1x5 or 3x3; 3x5 or 3x3; 4x12; 4x15"}} {{qDH088UUiMA@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] The opening pull doubles as a warm-up; you are a bodybuilder, so do not burn out on it or the session becomes a lower-body day {{qDH088UUiMA@136}}
- [1v 2021 O] Flat pullovers tension lats and chest at the bottom due to leverage; decline pullovers add range but the stretch starts later; for decline use an EZ bar, or do both as main lift and variation {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@1823}}
- [1v 2021 P] His one tip for a better-looking back physique: do pullovers {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@1323}}
- [1v 2021 P] Paused deadlift: stop about an inch off the floor and hold, teaching rigidity and preventing hips shooting up; submaximal only; he skips it because the floor is his strong point {{MP01uQbcoLc@1056}}
- [1v 2021 P] Open the second pull day with deficit or stiff-leg deadlifts, or standing rows that load legs and back, before pulldowns {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@1709}}
- [1v 2021 P] You do not need five types of cable rows; one big pull covers the bases, but do not rely only on deadlifts for lats either {{w4-2c3iWvDQ@3094}}
- [1v 2021 P] Rows after squats and deadlifts are a bad idea: the lower back is fried and the row is technically and muscularly demanding {{ZkQZPnTVKpg@625}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pullovers and dumbbell rows are versatile enough to fit anywhere in a session {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@378}}
- [1v 2021 P] Trap bar deadlifts build muscle but not the same as conventional because torso angle and knee flexion are different {{lG5SZbRdOjA@1324}}
- [1v 2021 P] The conventional deadlift is the king of hip hinges for posterior chain and upper back growth, but it is not for everyone at maximal effort {{lG5SZbRdOjA@376}}
- [1v 2021 P] Conventional loads the lumbar spine and posterior chain more than any other deadlift style {{lG5SZbRdOjA@437}}
- [1v 2021 A] He started snatch grip deadlifts two years earlier and uses a very narrow stance; start with about two plates {2021-08 {"load": "2 plates"}} {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2390}}
- [1v 2021 P] A hip hinge pull from the floor is a necessary part of every bodybuilder's program {{lG5SZbRdOjA@63}}
- [1v 2021 P] Once past the novice stage with a solid deadlift, strict braced rows can be an upper back builder; superset them with bench or put Pendlay rows on arm day {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@1576}}
- [1v 2021 O] Rows fill novice programs because of their carryover to the deadlift, being in the same line of specificity, not for upper back growth {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@1136}}
- [1v 2021 A] None of his programs, novice or otherwise, include barbell rows {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@189}}
- [1v 2021 P] Programming rows at 3x5 or 5x5 is a mistake; treat the row as a muscular-endurance lift in higher reps and never grind the last rep {2021-08 {"sets_reps": "not 3x5/5x5"}} {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@375}}
- [1v 2021 O] Kroc-style cheat rows carry over well to the deadlift but cause trouble if done after deadlifting {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@1449}}
- [1v 2021 P] Swimming/water polo Purist template: Day A barbell rows 3x8-10, OHP 3x6-10 plus wide-grip pulldowns 3x12-15, rear lateral raises 3x10-15 plus flutter kicks; Day B weighted pull-ups 3x5, DB rows 3x8-12 plus DB flies 3x10-15, lateral raises plus lying leg raises {2021-09 {"reps": "5-15", "sets": 3}} {{cNGPCn9Uupc@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Don't stack heavy compounds in a giant set, for example rack pulls then pull-ups then DB rows; keep heavy compounds to bi-sets {{XncJr9uMpWo@1013}}
- [1v 2021 A] NH does a lot of snatch-grip deadlifts and snatch-grip work because a wide grip is excellent for upper back development {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1511}}
- [1v 2021 D] Chinese lifters do dumbbell rows standing, unsupported, braced with the core, no bench {{jAnfGqpsK38@562}}
- [1v 2021 P] Dumbbell or seal rows 4x8-12 give upper-back volume without taxing a posterior chain already loaded by squats {2021-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@520}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pick the second exercise based on the first: after rows do good mornings, after deadlifts do weighted pull-ups; higher-capacity lifters can do both {{bqcRtC3bbK8@502}}
- [1v 2022 A] A subscriber pointed out his wrestling program had too much pushing; he agreed it had relied on a meathead stereotype and shifted it toward pulling {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1622}}
- [1v 2022 P] Croc rows are staggered-stance dumbbell rows pulled like starting a lawnmower, excellent for grip and upper back {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@3276}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pendlay rows or lat pulldowns follow squats for wrestlers; if too fatigued to stay rigid, do cable rows instead {2022-01 {"sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1793}}
- [1v 2022 O] Five years ago inverted rows and seal rows were niche; today's legless, bodybuilding-style back training will produce bigger backs {2022-02 {"years": "5"}} {{dVssxcEh1UQ@1727}}
- [1v 2022 D] Knights struck heavy objects top to bottom all day, which built the upper back like wood chopping does {{3W-yE2pgTec@2452}}
- [1v 2022 O] Street lifting builds the best natty upper backs and strong mindsets, though it's limited for legs; 30-minute minimalists get mogged by park lifters {2022-06 {"duration": "3 h"}} {{QfHfxLYRu88@3769}}
- [1v 2022 O] Climbing a wall isn't a strict pull-up; your center of gravity is behind you, so a cheated one-arm-then-the-other muscle-up is closer to real climbing {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@504}}
- [1v 2022 A] His upper back was built by heavy deadlifts, rows and pull-ups; cable isolation alone doesn't give enough tonnage {{uYRIWAKlA0c@501}}
- [1v 2022 P] Dumbbell rows 3x8-12 are optional for women who feel it is too much back work {2022-06 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@1000}}
- [1v 2022 P] The barbell row is a full-body compound with lower back stress and leg drive by design; don't expect it to isolate the upper back {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@380}}
- [1v 2022 P] Do your standard barbell rows first, then rope-plate rows for higher reps with a short grip, a big stretch and an exaggerated squeeze {{BayeNJ0NUTE@691}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Pendlay row is mediocre for lats: torso parallel to the floor, the floor cuts the stretch and there is no controlled negative. It is better for pulling strength, traps and upper back; he still does them with a snatch grip to the sternum {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1567}}
- [1v 2022 O] The cheat barbell row is Peewee tier: it is more of a deadlift, has no controlled negative, a worse stimulus-to-fatigue ratio for the upper back than deadlifts, and a higher injury risk; he used to do them {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1442}}
- [1v 2022 P] You do not need every top-tier lift, but a back program with none of them leaves gains on the table; S and A tier exercises are enough for a massive back, and everything below decent is laughably weak {{Cbq-vxVba4k@3200}}
- [1v 2022 O] The T-bar row is a noob trap he would ban: people always cheat with the knees and torso, and the two-hand handle blocks the squeeze and the inward arm rotation that involves the lats {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2072}}
- [1v 2022 O] Kettlebell swings do not build lats; they are a posterior chain exercise, and anyone claiming big back gains from them got them elsewhere or from drugs {{Cbq-vxVba4k@3138}}
- [1v 2022 O] The renegade row is Peewee tier: the floor removes the stretch, so you only get the squeeze, and balance and core limit the load instead of the back {{Cbq-vxVba4k@946}}
- [1v 2022 O] The standing one-arm cable 'shotgun' row is only decent because standing encourages cheating; do it seated for hypertrophy {{Cbq-vxVba4k@3138}}
- [1v 2022 O] Calisthenics are king for upper back development, but adding free weights gets you further {{Cbq-vxVba4k@757}}
- [1v 2023 P] Ring rows to failure, or barbell/seal rows 4x8-12, superset with EZ curls 4x6-10 or cable curls 4x10-15 {2023-01 {"sets": 4}} {{_P0-kYyVMn4@505}}
- [1v 2023 P] The deadlift trains glutes, hamstrings, lower back and traps but ignores the small upper-back muscles and the front of the body {{elk4uYvjXos@316}}
- [1v 2023 O] People who dislike dumbbell rows have not done them properly or found their style yet {{ABTeMrXdgI4@313}}
- [1v 2023 P] You can superset a back row with itself using a different grip if another muscle's exercise sits in between: first the variation for the weaker muscle (neutral row for lats), then the one for the stronger muscle (croc row for traps) {{B5eAFxfON3A@569}}
- [1v 2023 O] This double-row trick works for horizontal rows but probably not for pull-ups, where performance would suffer {{B5eAFxfON3A@632}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pull-ups are the best calisthenics movement for the back and one of his favorite exercises {{zuvTDBbAWN8@442}}
- [1v 2023 O] Life-or-death justifications like hanging from a bridge are silly; pull-ups are worth doing because they are fun and build strength {{ZN5ohmPPfjM@442}}
- [1v 2023 P] Barbell rows should be programmed as the day's hinge, opening the session, for 6-10 reps; going lower degrades form {2023-09 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{7uBT8vp0lNw@509}}
- [1v 2023 P] The back is made of several muscle groups, but that doesn't require many variations per area; pick 1-2 exercises and get strong on them in relevant rep ranges {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@146}}
- [1v 2023 P] Most back exercises train several muscles at once, so don't panic about leaving gains on the table {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@314}}
- [1v 2023 O] The deadlift recruits the most muscle but that makes it fatiguing, not better for back growth; people who obsessed over it for years were disappointed with their backs {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@568}}
- [1v 2023 O] After a year, the pull-up trainee will have the more aesthetic back; the deadlifter may be thicker, so do both hinges and pull-ups {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@693}}
- [1v 2023 O] No need for dedicated lower-back slots unless injured; heavy rows and hinges already develop it {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@314}}
- [1v 2023 P] Kroc rows and Meadows rows train such similar muscles that you can alternate them week to week without interrupting progression {{DA7QvIAyptw@1199}}
- [1v 2024 P] Program the deficit T-bar row as a total-back exercise; it is tough without the mobility, so work up to it {{-OYtq2AHGX8@448}}
- [1v 2024 A] He swapped bent-over dumbbell rows for chest-supported rows on a bench, though starting with bent-over rows helped build his posterior chain {{bRKQIOYo2u4@4963}}
- [1v 2024 O] Machine rows and Meadows rows are both horizontal pulls but serve different purposes; Meadows rows done properly hit the rear delts much more {{okm6bZHKHI4@1534}}
- [1v 2024 O] Barbell rows are an overall posterior-chain builder, not a replacement for RDLs, and rank low for upper back versus pulls where the upper back is the limiting factor {{c7hl9u3h_s0@767}}
- [1v 2024 O] Bar pull-ups beat ring pull-ups; rings are less stable and just more portable {{c7hl9u3h_s0@5863}}
- [1v 2024 O] The deadlift is an energy vampire; RDLs or stiff-leg deadlifts plus spinal erector isolation give better hypertrophy at less fatigue and risk {{6p2x4kDDjzs@8211}}
- [1v 2024 O] Narrow-handle seated rows are unproductive; use a wide grip for traps, and vertical pulls for lats {{6p2x4kDDjzs@5700}}
- [1v 2024 P] If your proportions don't suit the deadlift, drop it entirely and train the posterior chain with other movements {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@8505}}

## [back] row / heavy / open / sets (8)
- [3v 2022 P] Always open a back day with a heavy hip hinge or heavy pull (e.g. heavy row from the floor or heavy pull-up variation), never a light pulldown or machine, since you are freshest at the start also: 2022-02: Open back day with a leg movement, or with a heavy row, Pendlay row or heavy weighted pull-up {{wLx5BRx8AaU@1071}} {{gizylGr0ebo@2470}} {{bJ7gfILR6RA@628}}
- [1v 2020 P] For one set of 6 on a second back exercise, use a heavy row after pull-ups so form breakdown matters less. {2020-05 {"reps": 6, "sets": 1}} {{4aThavRpYLw@757}}
- [1v 2021 P] Back day template: heavy pull 1x5 or 3x3 (3x6-10 for RDL or row variations); vertical calisthenic pull 3x3 or 3x5 weighted or AMRAP bodyweight; horizontal pull 4x8-12 or a second hinge; yoke 4x10-15, a legless row, or hyperextensions/GHR/reverse hypers {{qDH088UUiMA@0}}
- [1v 2021 O] Opening back day with deadlifts (4 sets, 12/10/8 to failure) is good; heavy compounds aren't bad for hypertrophy {2021-08 {"reps": "12/10/8/failure", "sets": 4}} {{qq459xqmq5A@190}}
- [1v 2021 P] Option: one heavy deadlift top set to failure, then 1-2 easy row warm-ups and 2-3 heavy row back-off sets; never heavy rows followed by deadlift back-offs {2021-08 {"sets": "1 top + 2-3 back-off"}} {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@1512}}
- [1v 2022 O] Plate-loaded chest-supported row machines are mediocre: they are rarely adjustable, so you cannot row to the belly button, and the pad crushes the sternum, which makes heavy stretched sets miserable {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2385}}
- [1v 2023 P] You can open a back and biceps day with a heavy row/pull instead for more stimulus {{BiVfpZEchws@187}}
- [1v 2023 P] Choose a horizontal pull that does not load the legs and lower back (dumbbell or cable row); a heavy barbell row counts as a hip hinge {{7uBT8vp0lNw@256}}

## [back] horizontal / vertical / pattern / need (9)
- [2v 2021-2023 P] Do not treat the back as one muscle; it needs several movement patterns: hip hinges, vertical pulls and horizontal pulls {{qDH088UUiMA@37}} {{hEuy__sCbZA@569}}
- [1v 2020 P] Vertical and horizontal pulls are different movement patterns and should not be swapped as variations of each other {{-6i7xt92Khg@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dumbbell rows and pull-ups complement each other rather than being interchangeable; a vertical and horizontal pull on the same day is good, unlike bench plus dips. {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@1109}}
- [1v 2020 O] Horizontal pulls are the least important movement pattern in his opinion {{et6AylDr1uk@882}}
- [1v 2020 O] Bodybuilding pull-ups are the only vertical pull you need for upper back development {{uyTgjl3miAE@0}}
- [1v 2021 O] You can build a big back with only deadlifts and weighted pull-ups: deadlift is king, then vertical pull, with horizontal rows the lame duck of the family {{-anA4CV2SVU@252}}
- [1v 2021 P] The barbell row is a hybrid of a hip hinge and a horizontal pull and taxes the posterior chain hard, but programs treat it as a pure horizontal pull {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@502}}
- [1v 2023 O] A proper back day is one vertical pull, one horizontal pull and one shrug pattern; 6-7 back exercises is bro-split garbage {{DA7QvIAyptw@1261}}
- [1v 2024 P] For a bigger back, do heavy vertical and horizontal pulls with a stretch and felt tension {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@380}}

## [back] invert / grip / supinat / pronat (8)
- [2v 2020 OP] Supinated inverted rows are one of the few safe supinated row options. {{eFhwWirR70o@1245}} {{u10c43TRDb0@753}}
- [1v 2020 O] Inverted rows are very challenging for overweight lifters and stimulate the back well. {{mViZdjZIv6s@386}}
- [1v 2020 P] A pronated grip on the T-bar row hits less lats and more upper back {{du7JYCA2pa4@1000}}
- [1v 2020 P] The neutral grip is the most shoulder-friendly pull-up grip and also trains the arms {{DQo0DGn_wI4@400}}
- [1v 2021 P] Later in the workout use legless rows such as seal rows, dumbbell rows, weighted inverted ring rows or reduced-range rack rows {{qDH088UUiMA@497}}
- [1v 2021 P] Weighted pull-ups and inverted rows alone cover much of the back {{HrUPp573ki0@1259}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pick weighted chin-ups if you're advanced and unweighted supinated inverse rows otherwise {{bqcRtC3bbK8@404}}
- [1v 2022 O] The TRX inverted row is Peewee tier: too vertical and too much arm {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2888}}

## [back] replace / upper / love / seal (6)
- [2v 2021-2024 P] If you love rows, replace deadlifts with rows one week out of two; it will not hurt your deadlift or upper back {2021-05 {"frequency": "every other week"}} {{qDH088UUiMA@485}} {{okm6bZHKHI4@7557}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bent-over rows at 4x8-12 is a rep range he loves. {2020-05 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{rucwL-SIefg@564}}
- [1v 2020 P] If deadlifting twice in some weeks is too much, replace it with Pendlay rows or seal rows rather than cheat rows, to get horizontal pulling without loading the lumbar spine. {{o3RTXWAia4A@1135}}
- [1v 2020 P] People use too much leg drive on T-bar rows; replace them with a cable or seal row for the upper back {{DQo0DGn_wI4@1030}}
- [1v 2020 P] What the deadlift adds over other hip hinges is a weighted stretch for the upper back; good mornings don't stretch the upper back {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@311}}
- [1v 2021 P] Replace snatches with snatch-grip deadlifts to get the posterior-chain benefits of the pull without putting the bar overhead {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1480}}

## [back] posterior / chain / upper / row (7)
- [1v 2020 O] The barbell row resembles a pull from the floor and involves the posterior chain heavily, which is why some say it is not the best for the upper back {{Lj0m82JUtkM@450}}
- [1v 2021 P] The hip hinge takes priority because it develops the whole posterior chain plus the upper back, so always open the back day with a pull from the floor; any deadlift variation or a rack pull works {{qDH088UUiMA@76}}
- [1v 2021 P] Done properly, dumbbell rows hit the back without involving the lower back or posterior chain and can be done weekly without structural damage {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@315}}
- [1v 2021 O] The deadlift is essential for bodybuilders who want to develop the upper back and posterior chain {{twEeL-z4Zug@0}}
- [1v 2022 O] The barbell row is only decent for lats and upper back: it is diffuse, uses the legs and posterior chain, is hard to progressively overload without cheating, and its systemic fatigue leaves less energy for direct upper back work {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1317}}
- [1v 2022 O] The seated two-hand cable row is only decent: results depend heavily on the machine, and both hands at once limit the stretch {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1820}}
- [1v 2024 O] The deadlift-style barbell row (reset at the floor, torso fairly upright) is not his favourite but has value for the posterior chain and upper back; control the negative and prefer 8-12 {2024-11 {"reps": "8-12"}} {{6p2x4kDDjzs@2837}}

## [back] seal / barbell / drive / spare (4)
- [2v 2021-2022 P] Supported rows (dumbbell, seal) spare the lower back; keep barbell row reps higher to avoid ego lifting {2022-09 {"reps": "8", "sets": "4"}} {{8mGHG5C-EPE@750}} {{JnWWva91QUM@1253}}
- [2v 2021 P] Barbell rows tax the lower back like a deadlift without leg drive; dumbbell or seal rows avoid the hip hinge if your back suffers also: 2021-01: With lower back issues, replace Pendlay rows with rows that don't tax the lower back: ring rows, seal rows, dumbbell rows {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@535}} {{-anA4CV2SVU@316}}
- [1v 2020 O] Barbell seal rows are among the best rows because they take the posterior chain and leg drive out and let you pull with a horizontal torso {{4ORCb_i7MxY@185}}
- [1v 2020 O] Seal rows don't involve legs or lower back, so they're one of the few horizontal pulls NH would program with lower reps and higher intensity {{TD9SB9Bt45o@232}}

## [back] floor / style / rdls / underdevelop (6)
- [1v 2020 O] Pull from the floor in any style you like, as long as you pull from the floor with full range of motion. {{RaGX9rPQr4k@1483}}
- [1v 2020 O] The conventional deadlift is not for everyone; some people cannot pull it from the floor with a neutral back. {{iY2uzM3vlBA@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] RDLs aren't a pull from the floor; add deficit deadlifts (4 sets of 8-12, light) after 5/3/1 sumo, superset with pull-ups, for a second floor pull. {2020-02 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{np9LdwTPfNs@953}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pulls from the floor are essential in every style; many natural bodybuilders skip them and that is why their backs are underdeveloped {{YcHkXwsIOI8@750}}
- [1v 2021 P] You need deadlifts from the floor; RDLs alone are not enough, they are a variation that carries over to the deadlift {{cNUwR9TmVRs@501}}
- [1v 2021 P] Every deadlift style builds muscle, but training style A versus style B for three years produces noticeably different bodies {2021-08 {"duration": "3 years"}} {{lG5SZbRdOjA@188}}

## [back] rigidity / mobility / pendlay / chin (5)
- [1v 2020 O] Pendlay rows are the most technical row variation; especially with a wide grip they need lower back rigidity, hip positioning and hamstring mobility most novices lack. {{OATtf3kPtdI@313}}
- [1v 2020 O] He dislikes Pendlay rows for novices: they lack the mobility and discipline to do them right, cheat with legs and torso, and can hurt their lower back. Cable rows are a safer horizontal pull. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@378}}
- [1v 2020 P] If weighted chins were done wide, row to the sternum or higher to hit traps and upper back; after close-grip chins a normal Pendlay row is fine {{xQQJUXf58ZE@400}}
- [1v 2020 P] Unsupported two-arm dumbbell rows build coordination, hamstring mobility and spinal rigidity that carry over to deadlifts and barbell rows {{YDTUYiKFcFo@64}}
- [1v 2022 P] For BJJ, barbell rows or good mornings 4x8-12 force a bent-over compromised position that demands core rigidity and hip control, as on the mat {2022-01 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1075}}

## [back] never / pattern / optional / bodyweight (5)
- [1v 2020 O] You can never do too much pull-ups. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@754}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pulling from the floor should never be optional; it can be Jefferson, sumo or block pulls, but the pattern must be trained {{GClcGq2tIDU@753}}
- [1v 2020 P] Never open a workout with rows, especially a supinated 5x5; static ranges and low weight on rows are a bad idea {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@1100}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep bodyweight chin-ups even when doing weighted ones; never turn your back on bodyweight work, it helps injury prevention {{xQQJUXf58ZE@1301}}
- [1v 2023 P] Constantly rotating back exercises (e.g. every two months) means relearning the pattern and never grinding through plateaus {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@187}}

## [back] rdl / morn / hinge / neighbour (3)
- [2v 2021-2023 P] Any hip hinge works (deadlift, RDL, good morning, even hyperextension); there is no need to force the deadlift also: 2021-01: A deadlift on pull 1 and another hinge (good morning, RDL, stiff-leg or deficit deadlift) on pull 2 {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1240}} {{7uBT8vp0lNw@194}}
- [1v 2021 P] Lifts low on a branch often carry over more to their own top lift than a neighbouring branch's top lift does: the RDL and back extension carry over to the deadlift more than the squat does {{dyeeKOQJhrM@250}}
- [1v 2023 P] A pull-up variation, rows, dumbbell rows and pullovers plus a hinge like the RDL cover all upper back gains {{ABTeMrXdgI4@590}}

## [back] favourite / cable / nh's / stricter (3)
- [2v 2020 O] Cable rows are one of NH's favourite cable movements for the upper back {2020-06 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{hi-kNbZnXz0@379}} {{xQQJUXf58ZE@1220}}
- [1v 2020 P] Smith machine rows standing on a box turn the row into a pure isolation movement with no leg or lower back help, stricter than cable rows where people swing. {{TrU2m-4yZT0@505}}
- [1v 2021 O] Snatch grip deadlift is his favourite variation: harder than conventional, carries over closely, more knee flexion, hip hinge and upper-back stretch {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2329}}

## [back] size / catch / positive-only / strength (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Pendlay rows are good for strength and size but lack stretch because you let the bar fall instead of catching it; lifts that are positive-only (like dumping a deadlift) are weaker for hypertrophy. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@1762}}
- [1v 2020 O] Coan rows let you catch the weight and lengthen the upper back, and allow more load than Pendlay rows. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@1824}}
- [1v 2020 P] Open a pull/legs day with trap bar deadlifts: it builds strength and size and warms the body up. {{eFhwWirR70o@1307}}
- [1v 2020 O] Positive-only lifts like the deadlift express 100% strength where you are mechanically weakest, so saying deadlifts do not build size is wrong {{L5gcFxTG_cU@350}}

## [back] higher / fresh / barbell / session (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Alternate barbell rows and one-arm dumbbell rows across sessions, doing one with intensity and the other with higher volume, to keep training fresh. {{fSOiGuUPqBc@1132}}
- [1v 2020 P] He always advises a pronated grip for barbell rows, and thinks barbell rows suit higher rep ranges (12-15) rather than pushing intensity. {2020-05 {"reps": "12-15"}} {{mViZdjZIv6s@258}}
- [1v 2020 P] Doing rows after squats on leg day means the posterior chain is warm but fresh enough since no hip hinge was done yet {{Lj0m82JUtkM@810}}
- [1v 2022 P] Open sessions with barbell rows like a deadlift, with higher reps and volume, then move to isolation that removes lower back and legs; don't bury rows after squats and a heavy hinge {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@423}}

## [back] first / glut / hamstring / warm (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Deadlifting first warms up hamstrings, glutes and lower back so the following barbell rows are more efficient {{GClcGq2tIDU@815}}
- [1v 2020 O] NH believes deadlifts shouldn't be done in high rep ranges: hamstrings and glutes stay fresh while thoracic extension gives out first, raising injury risk and failing to challenge the muscles. {{T_ZMmSNmLbs@192}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pendlay rows suit higher reps for volume rather than intensity; warm up with face pulls first after heavy pressing because they can tax shoulders {{DQo0DGn_wI4@560}}
- [1v 2024 P] The deadlift is a hip hinge that mainly grows the posterior chain (glutes, hamstrings, lower back); it is not a good overall back builder {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@190}}

## [back] floor / conventional / include / jefferson (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Include some pull from the floor; it need not be conventional, trap bar or Jefferson are fine {{u10c43TRDb0@1134}}
- [1v 2020 P] Athletes should include a submaximal pull from the floor, even trap bar, to learn to transfer force from floor to hips; it's a good pull-day opener {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@960}}
- [1v 2020 P] Cleans and snatches are pulls from the floor and count as deadlift variations; when he says deadlift he means the conventional deadlift {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1630}}
- [1v 2021 P] For about 90 percent of people a pull day should open with a pull from the floor (sumo, conventional, Jefferson, behind-the-back or block pulls) to warm up the whole body {{pey1Ni6HPlY@476}}

## [back] whole / synergy / barbell / unsupport (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Start pull day with deadlifts rather than barbell rows; it improves the synergy of the whole day {{Lj0m82JUtkM@502}}
- [1v 2020 P] On bench day, follow with a horizontal row for synergy {{hT4zgNcMOBY@520}}
- [1v 2021 O] Dumbbell rows are underrated because people favor barbell rows, and most who do them just go down and up without using the whole upper back {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@252}}
- [1v 2022 P] Unsupported barbell rows train the whole posterior chain, not just lats {{gizylGr0ebo@1487}}

## [back] bread / butter / thicken / rhomboid (4)
- [1v 2021 P] Deadlifts and pull-ups are the bread and butter for upper back; deadlifts thicken the posterior chain, traps and rhomboids {{1MOLCv6ojgw@200}}
- [1v 2022 P] Legless pulls (cable rows, seal rows, dumbbell rows, vertical pulls with dangling legs) should be the bread and butter for upper back growth {{dVssxcEh1UQ@1592}}
- [1v 2022 P] Back training should be split into lats/mid-back, traps/upper back and lower back/posterior chain, because an exercise can be great for the lats and poor for the traps {{Cbq-vxVba4k@22}}
- [1v 2023 P] Hip hinges like good mornings and RDLs also thicken the erectors and rhomboids; only doing pull-ups leaves you flat as paper from the side {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@251}}

## [back] wrong / chest-support / hits / application (3)
- [1v 2020 P] He likes all rows (chest-supported, dumbbell, barbell, rings); find a variation that hits the upper back, and if you feel it all in arms and shoulders you're doing it wrong or picked the wrong row. {{3oZD6Hk525A@134}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do chest-supported rows on an incline bench: a flat bench limits the lat stretch and the incline arm angle hits the upper back differently {{9jAjNyxeiXc@251}}
- [1v 2021 P] There are no bad lifts, only bad applications; barbell rows are a poor lift for novices, and for most people, because they are done for the wrong reasons {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@62}}

## [back] decompres / teach / spine / help (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Pull-ups and chin-ups are non-negotiable: they don't tax the CNS, decompress the spine and teach beginners to pull with the back for rows and deadlifts. {{OATtf3kPtdI@875}}
- [1v 2020 P] Back hyperextensions are excellent for the lower back and teach pulling and pushing with the legs {{W-s1ft_HqYE@500}}
- [1v 2021 O] Weighted pull-ups after deadlifts help decompress the spine {{pey1Ni6HPlY@510}}

## [back] pendlay / lats / seal / better (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Pendlay rows supersetted with barbell shrugs and skullcrushers; in a crowded gym, swap Pendlay rows for seal rows to reuse the bench. {2020-03 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": "4"}} {{b4rMVrWFVEg@442}}
- [1v 2022 O] He prefers Pendlay rows to seal rows for bracing and slight posterior-chain work and hates a chest pad; in a proper Pendlay the lower back stays still {{QfHfxLYRu88@2515}}
- [1v 2024 O] Pendlay rows are not a good hypertrophy movement; dumbbell rows are better for lats {{6p2x4kDDjzs@4560}}

## [back] rear / delt / shrug / high-pull (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Use a high-pulling grip on rows to target the upper back, traps and rear delts {{-6i7xt92Khg@696}}
- [1v 2021 P] Finish the back day with isolation such as shrugs, rear delt work or rhomboid-focused pulls {{qDH088UUiMA@576}}
- [1v 2022 P] Back isolation includes small cable rows, one-arm standing rows, lateral raises for rear delts and shrugs {{dVssxcEh1UQ@2125}}

## [back] choice / row / fan / type (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Instead of rowing 10% lighter on day B, a pendlay row variation would have been a better choice than manipulating intensity. {2020-06 {"percent": 90}} {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@1004}}
- [1v 2021 P] With 'row of choice', pick one row type and stay in one tree of specificity {{cNUwR9TmVRs@63}}
- [1v 2024 O] He is not a fan of the seated cable row; a dumbbell row or a pullover is a better choice {{6p2x4kDDjzs@1511}}

## [back] trap / lack / bar / choice (3)
- [1v 2020 O] A dumbbell deadlift is close to a deficit trap bar deadlift; he did them for years for lack of choice {{PFQ30yaL1t0@1131}}
- [1v 2020 O] A light trap bar deadlift day is questionable since the trap bar is already a non-invasive variation you can go heavy on {{DukfPhcoYZE@770}}
- [1v 2021 O] Calisthenics alone can't max upper-body development: they lack loading for upper traps, rhomboids and erectors and lack triceps long head, wrist flexion and neck work {{pvpf7Yuayco@2483}}

## [back] conventional / sumo / pick / eventually (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Pick conventional or sumo eventually rather than training both {{RgGkGiaGqks@1069}}
- [1v 2020 P] Sumo and conventional deficit deadlifts shouldn't rotate in the same slot; pick one style {{i4YmJhlw7cs@623}}
- [1v 2021 O] A clone pulling conventional for three years would beat one pulling sumo on posterior chain, lower back and upper back, unless conventional gets him injured {2021-08 {"duration": "3 years"}} {{lG5SZbRdOjA@752}}

## [back] deficit / block / days / contradict (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Deficit pulls work the posterior chain and quads deeper while block pulls work the upper back more; rotate them across days if pairing them is too much {{i4YmJhlw7cs@685}}
- [1v 2020 P] Deficit deadlifts and block pulls on the same day contradict each other; put them on different days {{oLm1CDuY9zg@960}}
- [1v 2020 P] Snatch-grip and deficit deadlifts are very similar: both lower the hips and add knee flexion; deficit loads the lumbar more, snatch grip the upper back and quads {{xQQJUXf58ZE@1540}}

## [back] thicknes / mandatory / necessarily / yet (3)
- [1v 2020 A] Deadlifts are mandatory for a bigger back; about 85% of his back thickness came purely from deadlifting {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1844}}
- [1v 2020 P] Vertical pulls are mandatory on an upper day; if you can't weight them yet, don't {{xQQJUXf58ZE@189}}
- [1v 2021 O] Back thickness comes from the deadlift, not necessarily rows {{-anA4CV2SVU@316}}

## [back] kroc / supersett / upper / technique-focus (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Kroc rows done to target the upper back can be supersetted with cable rows, which become a stretch- and technique-focused horizontal pull {2020-10 {"reps": "10-12", "sets": 3}} {{Lj0m82JUtkM@590}}
- [1v 2020 P] Incline dumbbell press can be supersetted with incline chest-supported rows for upper back at no time cost {{niD0UuwTUvg@754}}
- [1v 2024 O] Kroc rows are incredibly underrated: unmatched for traps and upper back and very safe; back can be pushed hard with little injury risk {{6p2x4kDDjzs@2584}}

## [back] week / 6-12 / bent-over / twice (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Dumbbell rows twice a week for 4 sets of 6-12 {2021-03 {"frequency": "2x/wk", "reps": "6-12", "sets": 4}} {{1MOLCv6ojgw@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Bent-over rows after deadlifts double up on lower back fatigue; rotate deadlifts and bent-over rows week to week instead {{qq459xqmq5A@252}}
- [1v 2021 P] Alternate barbell rows and deadlifts week to week; if you do both, deadlift first, though he doesn't recommend it {{bqcRtC3bbK8@502}}

## [biceps] bicep / incline / excellent / cable (80)
- [3v 2020-2021 O] He dislikes incline dumbbell curls; do chin-ups on another day instead {{PYmr91aELnE@289}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@610}} {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@1203}}
- [2v 2023-2024 P] For a big biceps stretch with constant tension, use Bayesian cable curls instead of very deep incline curls also: 2023-12: Don't do incline curls on a cable; use Bayesian curls for a cable-aligned strength curve {{DAZRYoex8_w@1583}} {{jUMmu583w34@1520}}
- [1v 2020 P] An EZ bar works for curls with wide or narrow grips and for triceps movements such as behind-the-head extensions. {{Mnp9m8qpNkU@64}}
- [1v 2020 O] Seated concentration curls are a good pump movement but tend to over-extend the shoulder, straining the rotator cuff and front delt, and they train the biceps generally rather than a specific region. {{6-hX7F6f0Bk@0}}
- [1v 2020 D] In Pumping Iron Arnold did concentration curls standing and bent over from the floor; most gym-goers do them seated instead. {{6-hX7F6f0Bk@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] The variation is really an armwrestling exercise, but bodybuilding borrows any move that builds muscle; NH doubts Arnold actually did them this way. {{6-hX7F6f0Bk@0}}
- [1v 2020 A] He has no experience with blood flow restriction training so won't comment; might try it someday if he can't reach 20-inch arms otherwise. {2020-03 {"measurement": "20 in arms"}} {{VVckf4vyYPE@877}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pure isolation is impossible (curls also involve forearms and shoulders), but that doesn't make a movement useless; it prioritises the target muscle with help from others. {{g4N7gpzHm5k@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Barbell and dumbbell preacher curls are not interchangeable: different strength curve and shoulder stress. Curls are very important for arm development. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@441}}
- [1v 2020 O] Following barbell curls straight away with alternate curls raises intensity artificially and lowers tonnage; same-muscle back-to-back work needs a reason, which he only finds for triceps because of their angles {{GClcGq2tIDU@944}}
- [1v 2020 P] Finish with pullovers and stand straight up to curl the same dumbbell with both hands {{NiV6lE0XUH8@1175}}
- [1v 2020 O] Following a supinated biceps movement with a triceps extension usually does not hurt either and can reduce elbow tendinitis {{Hn3oGdUUhic@316}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dumbbell cheat curls cap the usable load, keep the weight close to the body and physically force a proper curl finish because you can't swing it to your face {{uND20Zam2jI@442}}
- [1v 2020 O] Incline curls stretch too far, and high reps on them mostly produce artificial lactic-acid failure {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@938}}
- [1v 2020 O] Incline curls are not the best biceps exercise and are useless for a grappler; resting three minutes after one set of curls is excessive {2020-09 {"rest": "3 min"}} {{ZdcKQXMmmPY@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] Free weights give better biceps results than cable curls {{TD9SB9Bt45o@849}}
- [1v 2020 O] Barbell curls, the most overloadable curl, should not be done last {{sCMGXCXKVjs@377}}
