- [1v 2020 P] Hammer curls after wrist curls at 6-10 for 3 sets so mechanical failure does not limit him; replace with pinwheel curls if shoulders dislike the position {2020-10 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1640}}
- [1v 2020 O] Front and lateral raises and strict curls are better with dumbbells, because the arms can stay at the sides; barbell curls push the weight forward and recruit the front delts {{ZScs2L9rero@1810}}
- [1v 2020 O] Incline dumbbell curls force light weight and most people bounce out of the bottom using tendon elasticity; they should never be the main biceps progression lift {{ZScs2L9rero@2400}}
- [1v 2021 P] The more exercises in a day, the harder progression becomes; focus on one {{RSkzA1l5jlk@590}}
- [1v 2021 P] The EZ bar mainly affects wrist comfort and forearm engagement, not biceps activation {{N4-K79MZNPs@64}}
- [1v 2021 P] Using a huge number of curl variations reduces neurological and technical adaptation, so you keep relearning instead of growing {{K1GVimJowoM@40}}
- [1v 2021 O] Biomechanics gurus' tweaks like angling the arm 30 degrees on a curl are things you learn yourself by lifting, and another person's body is not yours {{EP1Qw9aEKOU@1500}}
- [1v 2021 O] Alsruhe isolates the core but relies on compounds for arms and traps, which limits bodybuilders; if you isolate the core, isolate those muscles too {{OWbmOI3cuNs@966}}
- [1v 2021 O] Curls are not a waste of time; he rejects people who dismiss direct arm work {{Ugx0N3nR4YPJHxllS_d4AaABCQ@0}}
- [1v 2022 P] Muscle is potential: specificity matters only to a degree, and a biceps built with curls can still pull on a belt or gi {{v-F3QHSM2hg@690}}
- [1v 2022 P] Curls and most isolation lifts barely touch the CNS; nobody fries their CNS with three sets of curls {2022-03 {"sets": 3}} {{NPlGK0-7h0A@1438}}
- [1v 2022 O] Claiming curls are for bench carryover is a lie; any carryover is negligible and curls are done for looks {{GeMs1MAEK60@1938}}
- [1v 2022 O] Curls beat rings on time-to-stimulus because they need no learning; you'd get similar results in a tenth of the time {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@1009}}
- [1v 2022 O] Training rings for biceps is like becoming a chess grandmaster to improve your memory {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@634}}
- [1v 2022 P] Every lift is functional for something; curls are functional for hypertrophy, and resistance training was made to isolate the muscle-growing part of physical work {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@2709}}
- [1v 2022 O] Mentzer's claim that EZ curl bars don't work the biceps is unjustified guru talk {{lmhW2W2UQto@1073}}
- [1v 2022 A] If a curl variation gives you tendonitis, swap it; regular curls hurt his shoulders so he uses cross-body pinwheel curls {{yoROLGGUPXU@2040}}
- [1v 2022 O] He prefers pinwheel curls to hammer curls because hammer curls in front of the body over-recruit the front delt {{_KypKq9Hbe0@590}}
- [1v 2022 P] Modified Arnold curl: seated upright, elbow braced on the leg, cupped or thumbless grip, range from shoulder to parallel only, ideally with a fat grip {{_KypKq9Hbe0@400}}
- [1v 2022 P] The preacher pad stabilises elbow and shoulder so you can go to failure safely and isolate the biceps {{_KypKq9Hbe0@252}}
- [1v 2022 P] Dumbbell incline curls 3-4x6-10 (or 6-8) without bouncing at the bottom, with rope pushdowns 3-4x15-20; swap pushdowns for an overhead extension to hit the long head {2022-08 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": "3-4"}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@819}}
- [1v 2022 O] Occlusion (blood flow restriction) curls are mediocre: a gimmick mostly pushed by enhanced lifters; he does not use them and has huge biceps {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1762}}
- [1v 2022 O] Band curls are mediocre for building muscle; use them for recovery or to finish a session {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1699}}
- [1v 2022 O] Barbell curl is only decent: the straight bar can cause shoulder and wrist discomfort; the EZ bar was built for curls and is excellent {{RwEJpHTwWm0@62}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pinwheel (cross-body) curls are excellent: they hit the brachialis, brachioradialis and upper biceps; he has done them for years {{RwEJpHTwWm0@942}}
- [1v 2022 O] Incline curl is mediocre and overrated: shoulders shift to assist, the stretch is less than believed, it is risky, strength drops off a cliff between reps, and it cannot be ground out safely; at most a 3rd or 4th biceps exercise {{RwEJpHTwWm0@376}}
- [1v 2022 P] A good biceps exercise is judged by how much biceps you use relative to the rest of the body: stable elbow and shoulder, controllable stretch, no swinging {{RwEJpHTwWm0@189}}
- [1v 2022 O] Concentration curls are excellent: elbow braced on the leg prevents rebounding, the negative is controllable and bottom half reps are safe {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1133}}
- [1v 2022 O] Ring curls are decent, good for novices with difficulty adjusted by torso angle, but hard to overload and risky for biceps tears if overextended {{RwEJpHTwWm0@565}}
- [1v 2022 O] Behind-the-back cable curl is Arnold tier: a pelican curl without the skill, full stretch and contraction; do not do it with dumbbells {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1004}}
- [1v 2022 O] Cable curls are excellent: constant tension through the concentric and a peak contraction at the top, unlike a barbell {{RwEJpHTwWm0@439}}
- [1v 2022 O] Waiter curls are noodle tier: cramped position, poor squeeze and no full range; promoted only by people with no muscle or on drugs {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1321}}
- [1v 2022 O] The arm blaster is excellent because it teaches you to curl with the biceps by locking elbows and shoulders in place {{RwEJpHTwWm0@189}}
- [1v 2022 O] One-arm chin-ups are noodle tier for biceps: twisted body, momentum, huge skill requirement and little biceps load {{RwEJpHTwWm0@880}}
- [1v 2022 O] Crucifix curls are mediocre ego exercises that hog two cable stations; a single-arm cable curl does it better {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1575}}
- [1v 2022 O] Arm-wrestling style curls are decent; the wrist rather than the biceps is often the limiting factor {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1068}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machine curls are excellent, nearly Arnold tier, but most machines are built for one body type {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1258}}
- [1v 2022 P] Stick to Arnold and excellent tier exercises; decent ones can spice things up, ignore the rest {{RwEJpHTwWm0@2077}}
- [1v 2023 P] For novices favour less strict, intense isolation movements (standing dumbbell curls, lying triceps extensions) over precise ones like slow-tempo 45-degree cable extensions; precision comes later {{HXoY7xzHlWU@249}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pick the arm movement you enjoy and can do comfortably, not the trendy one; trying several curls before settling on one is fine, but then stick to it {{HXoY7xzHlWU@249}}
- [1v 2023 P] If you want incline curls, do them on a cable from a bench in the middle of the cable station; the cable resistance profile fixes the strength curve, reduces cheating and allows grinding and progression {{CGhPZ9AxBJE@1073}}
- [1v 2023 O] The incline curl is safe because the shoulder stays mobile; its problem is hypertrophy, not injury {{CGhPZ9AxBJE@189}}
- [1v 2023 P] Train lengthened and mid-range separately rather than combining them in one hybrid lift {{CGhPZ9AxBJE@633}}
- [1v 2023 O] Atlas stones and sandbags are a poor stimulus-to-fatigue way to train biceps: few reps, isometric only {{d1eLAd5WvIA@1376}}
- [1v 2023 O] Rings are his favourite way to do chin-ups because the wrists can rotate freely {{P7ADeDVghxg@188}}
- [1v 2023 P] Don't drop curls for a single trendy movement; if two things work, do both {{M2yqxp4IHXg@1696}}
- [1v 2023 O] Incline curls are an A/S-tier biceps exercise; his earlier C-tier rating was wrong because he relied on theory over practice {{DAZRYoex8_w@1079}}
- [1v 2023 O] Strong lifters who disdain isolation work are coping; Haviland trains biceps with full intensity and his arms may be his best body part {{hEuy__sCbZA@1766}}
- [1v 2024 A] He keeps partial preacher curls because they feel good on his tendons, he progresses and grows {{vVQmYGw7Kj8@1891}}
- [1v 2024 P] A four-plate chin-up does not guarantee big biceps; chin-ups are primarily a lat movement, and half that strength progression on curls gives better biceps results {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@566}}
- [1v 2024 P] No grip tweak turns a pulldown into a biceps exercise unless the shoulder is stationary and only the elbow moves, which is an isolation movement {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@630}}
- [1v 2024 O] The claim that 70% of arm gains come from compounds is made up; it is only true if you do nothing but compounds {2024-02 {"percent": "70%"}} {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@126}}
- [1v 2024 O] Hopping on rings for bigger biceps is silly; the hardest biceps skills take years to build, and you would be better off just doing curls {{tbY5VMSmlP8@700}}
- [1v 2024 O] Cheat curls were his only biceps exercise from 15 to about 22 and they built big arms, but that is survivorship bias; most people should use other curl variations {{Ft039H8jzXU@816}}
- [1v 2024 O] Incline curls on a slight incline are excellent; a near-horizontal incline makes the bottom too hard and the top too easy, which spoils progression {{jUMmu583w34@1470}}
- [1v 2024 P] Preacher curls need a minimum of 6 reps; 6-12 gives a cushion for a risky lift {2024-09 {"reps": "6-12"}} {{okm6bZHKHI4@6966}}
- [1v 2024 P] He is a mid-head, not a biomechanics nerd; dissecting which exercise hits which biceps head leads to bad programs with five curls; pick two or three and train hard {{wBct3Pl8OY4@3680}}
- [1v 2024 O] Barbell curls with a straight bar may not be the best way to target the biceps, though a beginner can make them work {{yZC3Nsmmzb8@4225}}
- [1v 2024 A] He put the incline curl in C tier, got furious reactions, and later realised it was S tier: he had just been bad at it, not the lift {{6yW3GSskl8g@64}}
- [1v 2024 A] Carrying with extended arms after arm days makes the arms the limiting factor, not legs or cardio {{qvWxIzhT1QU@1072}}
- [1v 2024 O] Proper Husafell stone carry (arms crossed, upper back squeezed) does little for hypertrophy; he holds it from the bottom like a pot with straight arms to load biceps and triceps {{qvWxIzhT1QU@1009}}
- [1v 2024 O] Banded curls are excellent: good resistance curve, safe, and allow rest-pause; it is the only curl he really feels in his biceps {{ZLheQPgEfoA@4878}}
- [1v 2025 O] He likes partials for forearms and does partials for biceps on preacher curls; for other exercises he always uses full range of motion because it feels better {{-7bzbmL-x3Q@440}}

## [biceps] ez-bar / bicep / preacher / stand (7)
- [2v 2020-2023 AP] Do curls and most movements standing to engage the core; EZ-bar and barbell curls are better for biceps strength progression {{ZScs2L9rero@2490}} {{HXoY7xzHlWU@1388}}
- [2v 2022-2023 O] Close-grip EZ-bar curl is the king of barbell curls (Arnold tier): many variations, easy to grind, safe also: 2023-08: The close-grip EZ-bar preacher curl is the best biceps exercise and is safe when done right {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1637}} {{B5eAFxfON3A@950}}
- [2v 2023 OP] Behind-the-back standing cable curls train the biceps lengthened without the dumbbell incline curl's problems {{CGhPZ9AxBJE@1117}} {{aJ1e41y8Na4@1070}}
- [1v 2020 O] Straight or EZ-bar curls are great because you can load them heavily; incline curls require low weight, overextend the tendon, and he thinks they are a poor biceps exercise. {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@502}}
- [1v 2020 O] Incline dumbbell curls have too much range, don't protect the biceps tendons and use too much elastic energy; do standing curls for strength at 3-7 reps strict {2020-08 {"reps": "3-7"}} {{2ag0uM9zhs4@1036}}
- [1v 2022 P] Be less greedy with exercise selection: a beginner needs only one biceps exercise, such as dumbbell or EZ-bar curls, while NH, being advanced, uses 4 to 6 {2022-05 {"exercises": "1 beginner, 4-6 advanced"}} {{BW-32tYoNxw@759}}
- [1v 2022 O] EZ-bar preacher curl is Arnold tier: both hands on one bar stretch both biceps equally, reveal the weaker arm, and train the forearm better {{RwEJpHTwWm0@251}}

## [biceps] chin-up / bicep / performance / hurt (8)
- [1v 2020 A] Pre-fatiguing biceps with curls does not really hurt chin-up performance since chin-ups are mostly upper back; he supersets heavy curls with chin-ups himself. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@503}}
- [1v 2020 O] Supersetting pull-ups with barbell curls isn't synergistic but won't hurt performance {{u10c43TRDb0@565}}
- [1v 2020 P] To add biceps to a pressing day, use band curls (easy on tendons) or chin-ups, which also add upper back work {{a4TKJvbCAf0@561}}
- [1v 2021 P] Step one: compound movements that involve the arms, such as chin-ups and rows for the biceps and horizontal and vertical presses for the triceps {{K1GVimJowoM@100}}
- [1v 2022 P] Choose chin-ups over pull-ups because the programs have no direct biceps work, and biceps matter for grappling and injury prevention {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2993}}
- [1v 2022 O] Chin-ups are a back exercise, not a biceps builder; few people build big biceps from chin-ups alone and EMG data claiming otherwise is flawed {{RwEJpHTwWm0@817}}
- [1v 2023 O] Chin-ups would suit a back and biceps day better than pull-ups {{BiVfpZEchws@377}}
- [1v 2023 O] Chin-ups train the biceps well but are a back exercise, not enough on their own for biceps, and carry real injury risk when strong {{M2yqxp4IHXg@1696}}

## [biceps] gymnast / bicep / elite / gymnastic (6)
- [1v 2020 O] Gymnasts get big biceps by being elite at rings from childhood; copying their training will not give you their arms {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1490}}
- [1v 2020 O] Gymnasts have big biceps because rings are their entire sport; reaching their level would mean sacrificing everything else, including legs {{h-QiKaysok0@123}}
- [1v 2022 O] Using gymnastic rings to build gymnast biceps fails because gymnasts get them as a byproduct of 10+ years of 5-6 hours of daily training {2022-06 {"hours": "5-6/day", "years": "10+"}} {{9kG_0Y7UYnw@509}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pelican curls give an immense stretch and gymnasts' biceps show their potency, but they need so much skill some people cut weight to do them {{RwEJpHTwWm0@689}}
- [1v 2022 O] Straight-arm work (gymnastics) is decent given gymnasts' huge biceps from the loaded stretch, but you do not need that skill to grow biceps {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1511}}
- [1v 2024 P] Intermediate curl strength reliably grows biceps, while elite pull-up strength may not {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@2785}}

## [biceps] towel / preacher / narrower / soft (3)
- [2v 2023 P] Soft variations (same movement, different implement, e.g. dumbbell to cable curl, towel or rope) are unlimited because they do not cost progression; switching preacher curl to cheat barbell curl is a real variation also: 2023-09: Preacher variations with rope, towel, dumbbell, fat grip or EZ bar are 'soft' variations of one exercise, not too many exercises {{HXoY7xzHlWU@1198}} {{43F9unmSc_I@695}}
- [1v 2020 P] Like rings, towels let you change wrist angle without discomfort, so towel curls hit muscles at angles a dumbbell can't {{gSpySaBZqn4@252}}
- [1v 2021 P] Without a preacher bench, raise the elbows and set them close to the chest with a narrower grip to be your own preacher {{N4-K79MZNPs@560}}

## [biceps] cheat / supinat / clean / turn (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Barbell cheat curls turn into a supinated clean; dumbbell cheat curls stay closer to a real curl; a cheat curl is a strength movement and a fixed 3x8 offers some injury protection {2020-06 {"reps": 8, "sets": 3}} {{PYmr91aELnE@974}}
- [1v 2020 P] With EZ bar cheat curls the weight climbs through neural and technical adaptation rather than biceps growth, turning them into supinated cleans {{uND20Zam2jI@64}}
- [1v 2020 O] People who cheat curl three plates with a supinated grip don't have biceps strong enough to curl that weight; it is whole-body explosiveness {2020-07 {"load": "3 plates"}} {{uND20Zam2jI@253}}
- [1v 2022 O] Cheat barbell curls are noodle tier: just a supinated clean with hip momentum; if you want negatives, do preacher curls {{RwEJpHTwWm0@502}}

## [biceps] ez / cheat / bar / way (4)
- [1v 2020 P] The only redeeming part of an EZ bar cheat curl is a controlled negative, but you can get that more easily on a preacher bench or with heavy single-arm negatives assisted on the way up {{uND20Zam2jI@253}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do both EZ bar and dumbbell curls in general; this comparison concerns only cheat curls {{uND20Zam2jI@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] EZ bar preacher curl with a close grip, not going all the way down, is very good for the biceps; 8-12 to prevent ego lifting {2020-10 {"reps": "8-12"}} {{1f4aaqgOfXk@101}}
- [1v 2021 O] Dumbbells make cheating harder than the EZ bar {{N4-K79MZNPs@350}}

## [biceps] drag / sensation / uncomfortable / mainly (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Drag curls rely on sensation more than tension; novices should do standard curls and save drag curls as a later variation {{_L5wLHlFfI0@503}}
- [1v 2022 O] Drag curls are decent but boring and uncomfortable {{RwEJpHTwWm0@1824}}
- [1v 2023 O] People keep doing incline curls mainly for the stretch sensation {{CGhPZ9AxBJE@1010}}

## [biceps] preacher / spider / staple / bicep (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Spider curls are not preacher curls: the shoulders can move, making full extension safe for the biceps {{gizylGr0ebo@1237}}
- [1v 2022 O] Spider curls are Arnold tier: like a preacher curl with full range of motion, can be ground out, and tend to relieve tendinitis whereas incline curls cause it {{RwEJpHTwWm0@753}}
- [1v 2024 O?] Preacher curls are a good staple for big biceps {{bRKQIOYo2u4@7914}}

## [biceps] week / step / rotate / spider (3)
- [1v 2023 P] Step 5 (advanced): add one very strict movement per muscle, like preacher curls or a triceps machine, and rotate (e.g. two movements week A, two week B) among three favourites {{HXoY7xzHlWU@1325}}
- [1v 2023 P] Step 3 (intermediate): add one more movement per muscle, different from the first, for 2 biceps and 2-3 triceps movements total {{HXoY7xzHlWU@880}}
- [1v 2024 P] Spider curls after incline curls are done fatigued; rotate them week to week instead {{c7hl9u3h_s0@6690}}

## [calves] calv / calf / come / heel (13)
- [1v 2020 P] Toshiki raises (named for Japanese weightlifter Toshiki Yamamoto): essentially a high pull that doesn't reach the torso, using the momentum of the pull to drive an explosive calf raise with a squeeze at the top. {{AK6xwDBOJLE@123}}
- [1v 2020 P] Opening a workout with calves is a waste of energy; on leg day start with squats {{2ag0uM9zhs4@721}}
- [1v 2023 P] Squats train quads, hamstrings to some extent, glutes and hips, but not the calves, contrary to Rippetoe {{elk4uYvjXos@253}}
- [1v 2024 O] Running and jump rope are C tier for calves: burn is not growth {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3094}}
- [1v 2024 A] Plyometrics are D tier for calves: power comes from knees and hips; he could dunk but had poor calves {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3031}}
- [1v 2024 O] Seated calf raises are B tier: big stretch and you can push on the weight in the stretch beyond failure, but strong position and knee pad discomfort {2024-02 {"reps": 15}} {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2781}}
- [1v 2024 O] Sled push is a fun calf builder because the heels come off the floor; strongmen have big calves {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3659}}
- [1v 2024 P] Calf press on the leg press is S tier: great tension, easy to superset and do several times a week {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3534}}
- [1v 2024 O] Donkey calf raises are C tier: hard to load, stress the lower back, target nothing special {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2717}}
- [1v 2024 P] Tiptoe farmer's walk is A tier for calves: 4-5 minutes with straps, but taxing {2024-02 {"minutes": "4-5"}} {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3472}}
- [1v 2024 P] Biking is B tier for calves if set up so the heel comes off the pedal {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3155}}
- [1v 2024 P] Weighted StairMaster on tiptoes with a backpack is S tier for calves {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3283}}
- [1v 2024 O] Tibialis raise is C tier: great for shin splint rehab {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3597}}

## [calves] calf / raise / tempo / bad (7)
- [1v 2020 P?] His plan uses three calf movements that only need a barbell and weights (plus a bench for one), including plain calf raises with an improvised machine. {{TN76DttpTKI@63}}
- [1v 2021 O] Padded standing calf raise machines are usually too vertical and are bad for spine, knees and calf growth {{pvpf7Yuayco@500}}
- [1v 2024 P] Standing two-leg calf raise is S tier with foot elevated, slow tempo, high reps and progressive load; machine or Smith rather than barbell {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2905}}
- [1v 2024 O] Single-leg calf raise is A tier: fixes side imbalances and ankle flexibility but is hard to load {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2843}}
- [1v 2024 O] Bodyweight calf raises are D tier because calves already do that daily when walking {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2655}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dumbbell calf hops are D tier: poor stretch, bad tempo, low load {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@2969}}
- [1v 2024 O] Calf launcher (Nordic-style calf exercise) is B tier {{ryWJV6yN3ZE@3597}}

## [chest] chest / push-up / dips / incline (104)
- [2v 2020-2024 OP] His first bench day opens with touch-and-go Slingshot bench for 1-3 heavy sets of 4-8 reps; raw touch-and-go kills his shoulders, but he wants heavy weight and the unmatched stretch at the bottom. {2020-01 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": "1-3"}} {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@0}} {{fPU3nRIH8sM@9225}}
- [2v 2020-2021 OP] Dumbbell incline bench beats barbell because you can tuck and adjust the elbow angle and spare the shoulders {{4ORCb_i7MxY@747}} {{pvpf7Yuayco@2190}}
- [1v 2020 O] Don't get stuck thinking only compound movements matter; even if bench is the best chest builder, it shouldn't be all you do. {{4FY2Ct22qXU@251}}
- [1v 2020 P] He follows with paused bench without a Slingshot for 3 heavy sets of 4-8: it works the chest more, and the pause keeps him tight, prevents shoulder overextension and causes zero rotator cuff pain. {2020-01 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 3}} {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@65}}
- [1v 2020 P] On day 2 he does Spoto press for 4 sets up to 12 reps, chasing the pump, supersetted with skull crushers (long head), dragon flags and band work. {2020-01 {"reps": "up to 12", "sets": 4}} {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@317}}
- [1v 2020 P] With a SPRI band, step on it and do standing flyes; bending the arms more makes it easier. {{vLAhljbROlk@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Finger push-ups are for advanced trainees bored of regular, diamond and other variations; don't try them if you're not good at normal push-ups. {{S5i157GyQGo@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Finger push-ups deload the chest somewhat, put more emphasis on the back (teres major), deload the wrists but strain the fingers. {{S5i157GyQGo@78}}
- [1v 2020 P] The first reason for compounds is training economy: one bench press covers chest, three triceps heads and shoulders, saving time for people with jobs and families. {{NAkUxLa5Ttg@63}}
- [1v 2020 P] Accept that you cannot bench raw all the time; use variations that deload the shoulder (reverse grip, close grip, slingshot, floor press, any dumbbell press) so the shoulder rests between flat barbell sessions. {{IahVc0kbGOU@251}}
- [1v 2020 O] Slingshots do deload the bottom of the bench and are shoulder-friendly, but he believes slingshot strength doesn't carry over to the raw bench, while raw bench carries over to slingshot bench. {{xoLo7fptNA8@250}}
- [1v 2020 P] Replace one bench day per week with a paused bench day: pause on the chest, on pins at chest level, or above the chest (Spoto press, pin press); pauses deload the shoulder. {2020-02 {"frequency": "1x/week"}} {{xoLo7fptNA8@186}}
- [1v 2020 P] Upper body options are limited: bench needs spotters or safeties, and lockout on bench is not a resting position; cable work lets you rest between reps at will. {{uTHCzF7Exck@635}}
- [1v 2020 O] Don't think you must do a lift just because it's one of the big three; push-ups agree with more people than the bench press, which was chosen for powerlifting for other reasons. {{Eh0fnXIyDMc@125}}
- [1v 2020 P] Choose bench or dips based on what agrees with you; that choice determines the follow-up variation (close-grip bench or paused dips). {{b4rMVrWFVEg@250}}
- [1v 2020 O] Paused bench is one of the best variations for improving bench press form. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@249}}
- [1v 2020 O] Shifting pressing volume from flat bench to dumbbell variations likely costs gains; more flat bench and less dumbbell pressing would be better, and the same for barbell OHP over seated dumbbell press. {{fSOiGuUPqBc@881}}
- [1v 2020 A] He doesn't personally do incline or decline pressing. {{4aThavRpYLw@1323}}
- [1v 2020 O] At the other extreme, sets that are too easy barely register as fatigue; push-ups for an advanced, not heavy bencher can be repeated almost forever, so they are poor for building big muscles despite accumulating tonnage. {2020-05 {"reps": 40}} {{Vbk_2AALm3M@318}}
- [1v 2020 P] If the bench is your main chest builder, your rotation should include bench variations and other lifts that complement it by working the chest. {{uRWgz3nhqds@64}}
- [1v 2020 P] Having a strength focus on one exercise is good, but you don't have to do only its variations (close grip, Spoto press, board/pin presses); you can branch out to any movement targeting the muscle. {{uRWgz3nhqds@64}}
- [1v 2020 P] Three dumbbell bench variations back to back is too much; do dumbbell bench as strength work for 4x4-8, then rotate incline or floor press for 4x8-12 {2020-06 {"reps": "4-8 / 8-12", "sets": 4}} {{NiV6lE0XUH8@635}}
- [1v 2020 A] Dumbbell floor press is a subpar dumbbell bench lacking the bottom stretch; he did it for three years and got poor chest results {2020-06 {"years": 3}} {{PFQ30yaL1t0@630}}
- [1v 2020 A] He ends a chest and shoulder session with a resistance band finisher before flexing {{CBqEI-vDF_w@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] He uses pre-exhaustion for chest but calls it activation: firing the muscle with a band improves performance, while hard cable isolation beforehand reduces it {{RqS8AxVE28I@632}}
- [1v 2020 O] He dislikes incline barbell bench: most benches' inclines make it closer to a horizontal press than people realize, it over-recruits the front delt, and it has a higher injury rate than flat bench {{4ORCb_i7MxY@58}}
- [1v 2020 P] Supinated band or cable flyes raising the arms up and inward hit the upper and inner chest for anyone {{m-XeEY4xRDU@124}}
- [1v 2020 P] Doing the flyes between bench sets won't take strength away from pressing {{m-XeEY4xRDU@248}}
- [1v 2020 P] With only two horizontal press days a week on upper/lower, stick to the deep bench press {2020-09 {"frequency": "2x/wk"}} {{i4YmJhlw7cs@124}}
- [1v 2020 P] Push-ups develop the entire chest, triceps and to an extent the shoulders {{ZdcKQXMmmPY@800}}
- [1v 2020 P] Decline push-ups increase range of motion and time under tension, harder still at 300 lb bodyweight {{4An70JgJrVw@300}}
- [1v 2020 O] He is biased against machines that replicate compounds: they remove stabilization, lock you into one angle and usually cut the range short at the bottom stretch {{eobG2o3Ww4M@437}}
- [1v 2020 P] Flies suit high reps, multiple sets and supersets, not one heavy set {{6kRRzKx7lKs@722}}
- [1v 2020 P] Progress ring dips from feet on the floor, to feet on a box, to feet off the floor with knees raised {{h-QiKaysok0@563}}
- [1v 2020 P] A typical hierarchy: bench, then close-grip bench, then a fly for the pump; hierarchies are also horizontal (spoto press alongside close-grip, push-ups alongside flies) {{6sFaB3rAY1A@694}}
- [1v 2020 P] You could alternate dips one week and bench the next, letting each develop on its own while the muscles carry over {{6sFaB3rAY1A@570}}
- [1v 2020 O] You cannot grow your chest with only chin-ups and overhead press as a minimalist program {{OtoSjIZudIA@2452}}
- [1v 2020 O] Paused bench is an excellent bodybuilding exercise, not just for powerlifting {{niD0UuwTUvg@568}}
- [1v 2020 P] He sees each movement pattern as a tree ranked by specificity: bench, spoto press, close-grip bench, push-up, diamond push-up, down to banded flies {{twgzqTg4nug@443}}
- [1v 2021 P] Separate two similar strength lifts like incline bench and dips with other work so you're fresher for the second {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@853}}
- [1v 2021 O] Kneeling landmine press and incline press are shoulder-type vertical presses, not chest exercises; one top set of flyes as your only chest work will not grow a natural {{A55c7v1yMn4@1246}}
- [1v 2021 P] Lift variations should stay in the same movement-pattern line: incline bench and chest fly are not interchangeable {{cNUwR9TmVRs@126}}
- [1v 2021 P] Because full body hits everything every session, you do not need to bench three times a week; rotate bench variations to stay healthy long term {2021-01 {"frequency": "3x/wk"}} {{G7rVTseabFU@340}}
- [1v 2021 O] Incline cable fly 3x12-15 is a good chest finisher; some people get a good upper chest contraction from it, others do not {2021-01 {"reps": "12-15", "sets": 3}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@140}}
- [1v 2021 P] Distant branches do not carry over: no horizontal press helps the deadlift; overhead press carries over to bench more than flies {{dyeeKOQJhrM@360}}
- [1v 2021 O] Splitting the chest into upper/middle/lower areas creates an 'isolationist mindset' that misses the big picture: compound movements that build quality tonnage fast {{62OBPecQPnM@64}}
- [1v 2021 O] Feet-up bench removes leg drive so you lift less and must brace hard through the core for stability, which can make it a useful variation, but it is not a must-do {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@70}}
- [1v 2021 P] Rotate reverse-grip and regular dumbbell bench rather than doing both: regular at higher intensity, reverse for volume; detraining between them is minimal {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@2139}}
- [1v 2021 P] Muscle that doesn't fit the sport can hurt performance; a sprinter with huge pecs is slowed by the extra mass and oxygen demand {{cNGPCn9Uupc@942}}
- [1v 2021 P] Weighted push-ups 3x4-8, weighted chin-ups 3x3-5 and hanging knee raises 3xAMRAP; bench press is fine but felt out of place in an Oly program {2021-10 {"sets": 3}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@700}}
- [1v 2021 P] Weighted dips 3x3-5 on Friday keep strength up without overdoing the chest; lean more upright for triceps {2021-10 {"reps": "3-5", "sets": 3}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@880}}
- [1v 2022 P] Most chest press and shoulder press machines have a short range of motion because the handles stop you; free weights allow a deeper stretch, which is better for hypertrophy {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@1243}}
- [1v 2022 O] Push-ups are not just for novices; advanced lifters with a lot of muscle still benefit from them {{vyKzL85QwY4@65}}
- [1v 2022 P] Boxing Day A opens with dips or weighted push-ups 3x6-8 as the main press, then seal or incline DB rows 4x8-12 with landmine twists, to build explosive upper-body power {2022-01 {"reps": "6-8", "sets": 3}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@389}}
- [1v 2022 P] Karate and kung fu programs emphasise pressing because projecting the shoulder matters for strikers; neutral, reverse or Swiss-bar bench grips are fine {2022-01 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2378}}
- [1v 2022 O] He prefers dips or weighted push-ups over bench press for BJJ because moving through space suits the sport, but bench is fine if you like it {2022-01 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1075}}
- [1v 2022 O] The one-arm DB press for karate is a slightly gimmicky nod to punching with one arm at a time {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2642}}
- [1v 2022 P] Neutral-grip presses match how you grip in judo and sumo {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2896}}
- [1v 2022 P] Movement patterns share neural patterns, so you don't need to bench every other day; any press every other day maintains it {2022-02 {"frequency": "every other day"}} {{BJXFeQWGUuY@1327}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pullovers make little sense for most women because a barrel chest is not a desired feminine trait {{D2ERyDt4Pqo@420}}
- [1v 2022 P] The best chest movement is the one you enjoy and can do for a long time; if the bench injures you every four months, drop it {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@498}}
- [1v 2022 P] For chest use one big press, maybe a second press or accessory, and maybe one isolation; more is pointless {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@372}}
- [1v 2022 P] Push-ups AMRAP or bench press 4x6-8 (4-8 in the description); little pushing because chest and shoulders respond poorly to hypertrophy in women {2022-06 {"reps": "6-8", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@312}}
- [1v 2022 P] Vary grip: a non-powerlifter can do back-off bench work with a close grip to take stress off the shoulders {{yoROLGGUPXU@689}}
- [1v 2022 P] Bench 6-8 and overhead press 5-8 or 8-10 are good rep ranges for opening compounds {2022-09 {"reps": "6-8 bench, 5-8 or 8-10 OHP"}} {{JnWWva91QUM@377}}
- [1v 2022 P] Guillotine press is a risky Silver Era specialty lift due to internal rotation; keep it at 10-15 reps or use rings {2022-10 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{g-pqgciOSgE@1197}}
- [1v 2022 O] Diamond push-ups are decent for chest because elbow flexion shifts work to triceps; Pike push-ups are mediocre because they are mostly shoulders {{cUkWvnY8AJI@437}}
- [1v 2022 O] Alternating dumbbell press is only decent: no reason to stretch one side at a time when lying on a bench {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1942}}
- [1v 2022 P] If you do incline presses, treat them as a priority lift, not an afterthought {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1253}}
- [1v 2022 O] Larsen press is excellent: instability and no leg drive train you differently but remove the stability that lets you grind reps {{cUkWvnY8AJI@251}}
- [1v 2022 O] Reverse-grip barbell bench is mediocre: awkward on the wrists, only a little more upper chest; reverse-grip dumbbell press is excellent because each hand is independent {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1316}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hindu push-up is mediocre, planche push-up decent, one-arm push-up A-cup: advanced calisthenics skills are not what builds the muscle, the basic training to get there is {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2071}}
- [1v 2022 O] Guillotine press is excellent with submaximal loads and a spotter; NH prefers it on rings {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2822}}
- [1v 2022 O] Dips are Gorilla tier, arguably better than bench in some ways for stretch and range, but growing young men should watch for sternum or rib cage pain {{cUkWvnY8AJI@813}}
- [1v 2022 O] Dumbbell press is Gorilla tier and underrated; anyone who does not feel bench in the chest should start with dumbbells {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1879}}
- [1v 2022 O] Swiss-ball press is A-cup tier: an unstable back surface makes scapular retraction, which protects the shoulders and keeps the chest engaged, harder {{cUkWvnY8AJI@374}}
- [1v 2022 P] Ring flies are excellent but risky; build up from neutral-grip ring push-ups by gradually widening the arms to avoid tearing a pec or rotator cuff {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1001}}
- [1v 2022 P] The steeper the incline, the more shoulder work; a heavy flat press immediately followed by a heavy incline press makes the two compete {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1253}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pullover is mediocre for chest: the chest feels a passive stretch from the shoulder position, which can be risky to overload {{cUkWvnY8AJI@3324}}
- [1v 2022 O] Decline (feet-elevated) push-up is excellent; weighted deficit push-up gives full control of hand position and full stretch {{cUkWvnY8AJI@875}}
- [1v 2022 O] Band-resisted push-ups are excellent for overloading push-ups without a vest; crucifix push-ups are mediocre {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2948}}
- [1v 2022 O] Medicine ball chest press and medicine ball push-ups are A-cup; clap push-ups are only decent, add weight instead {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2507}}
- [1v 2022 O] Football (neutral grip) bar bench is excellent; athletes and linemen should press this way for sport specificity {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1628}}
- [1v 2022 P] Upright horizontal presses complement lying presses the way cable rows complement bent-over rows {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1190}}
- [1v 2022 O] Cambered bar bench is Gorilla tier: it lets the elbows and hands go deeper for extra stretch {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2885}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bench and overhead press can be the meat and potatoes for chest and shoulders; claims they are suboptimal are overblown {{d1eLAd5WvIA@1251}}
- [1v 2023 P] Consistency, not stability, is the goal: unstable lifts like ring push-ups are fine if executed consistently {{Zqv7KVsmu78@821}}
- [1v 2023 P] Open the session with the variation that targets your weak area (Alex: incline press for upper chest) since that is when you are freshest {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@252}}
- [1v 2023 O] The barrel press is a biomechanically sound fly alternative: more stretch, less shoulder and elbow stress, more load {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@434}}
- [1v 2023 A] Dips followed by pullovers is a double chest stretch that left him with upper-chest soreness; may be counterproductive for less advanced lifters {{awwJ5sbyz6A@825}}
- [1v 2023 P] Anyone doing dumbbell flies should try the power fly instead; biomechanically it makes more sense and you probably won't go back {{0X2PxuT3tuE@192}}
- [1v 2023 A] Most blind spots are self-created; he only now realised he should load ring push-ups, which he ranks first in his hypertrophy tier list {{43F9unmSc_I@1697}}
- [1v 2024 P] Sword swinging ignores the chest entirely and barely trains biceps, the very muscles people admire on Guts {{29XrRmOrCMs@506}}
- [1v 2024 O] Flyes still help build the lower pecs, feel good and help thoracic mobility {{-OYtq2AHGX8@826}}
- [1v 2024 A] He stopped push-ups because YouTube said they were for noobs; that was wrong {{Ft039H8jzXU@1508}}
- [1v 2024 O] Bodybuilders dismiss calisthenics movements because they are bad at them; NH ditched bench press for dips {{2LoP6-KdEx4@2460}}
- [1v 2024 P] Once you know how to push, a 15-rep max on a Smith incline press is excellent for hypertrophy; free weights and machines are equally effective with different benefits {2024-06 {"reps": 15}} {{jUMmu583w34@540}}
- [1v 2024 A] After 15 years he found weighted dips are the chest/shoulder/triceps movement that fits his build best, letting him push the most load with the least pain; he never skips weighted dips and pull-ups {2024-09 {"weeks": "15 years"}} {{bRKQIOYo2u4@1854}}
- [1v 2024 P] Deadlift is not the best hypertrophy lift; bench-only pressing invites plateaus and shoulder issues, so add a vertical press, bench variants and face pulls {{c7hl9u3h_s0@9102}}
- [1v 2024 O] Bench press supersetted with pullovers is his favourite superset {{c7hl9u3h_s0@3513}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dips are hugely underrated for bodybuilding; he does them with three plates plus two 10s for sets of five {2024-10 {"load": "3 plates + 2x10 lb", "reps": 5}} {{wBct3Pl8OY4@3304}}
- [1v 2024 A] He dropped a cable upper-chest press that only felt right 20% of the time for dumbbell incline press, which gives about 90% of the result {{6yW3GSskl8g@1455}}
- [1v 2024 P] The bench press is not bad for bodybuilding; try it, and if it agrees with your body keep it, otherwise use something else {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@883}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dips and bench press are about equal for hypertrophy; dips are a better overall builder of triceps and shoulders if your shoulders tolerate them {{ZLheQPgEfoA@566}}

## [chest] dips / rotate / redundant / ring (14)
- [2v 2024 OP] Ring dips followed by ring push-ups on the same day are redundant; rotate the two instead also: 2024-10: Bench press and weighted dips on the same day are redundant; pick one and rotate {{okm6bZHKHI4@2809}} {{c7hl9u3h_s0@9354}}
- [1v 2020 P] The anchor lift doesn't have to be one of the big three; any multi-joint compound that hits the same muscles works (e.g. weighted dips or weighted push-ups for chest). {{4FY2Ct22qXU@186}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bench, back-off bench, overhead press and weighted dips on one day is too many compounds; rotate bench and weighted dips as the strength lift (e.g. 2-month blocks or alternating), keeping back-off bench always. {2020-06 {"weeks": "2 months"}} {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@860}}
- [1v 2020 P] Floor press and bench press train different qualities and shouldn't rotate in the same slot; put them on separate days {{i4YmJhlw7cs@62}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't do both cable press and dumbbell flies in one day; pick one and rotate them like the main bench variations {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@313}}
- [1v 2020 O] People who have the shoulders and rib cage for dips should do dips; dips and bench are each well complemented by push-ups {{6sFaB3rAY1A@570}}
- [1v 2021 P] Incline bench, bench and dips in one session repeat the same pattern; keep one of bench or dips, or rotate them weekly, and raise volume on the one kept {{lZP5NoQ5pF4@158}}
- [1v 2021 P] Same with exercises: if bench hurts your shoulder, dips or weighted push-ups can build the chest {{xi9CuNbLd9g@689}}
- [1v 2022 O] Ring push-ups are Gorilla tier: the rings can rotate, they spare the wrists, allow more range and let the shoulder blades retract {{cUkWvnY8AJI@938}}
- [1v 2023 O] Three chest presses plus cable flies in a row is a lot; close-grip bench after triceps isolation is a redundant compound that mirrors the bench {{BiVfpZEchws@62}}
- [1v 2024 O] Rings trade stability for range of motion on dips; he prefers a dip bar because it was made for dips {{okm6bZHKHI4@4379}}
- [1v 2024 O] Ring dips are not better for hypertrophy; regular dips on a dip station are good {{lyj1BFZ85Dg@1845}}
- [1v 2024 A] He has replaced bench press entirely with ring push-ups and dips and is doing better for it {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1205}}
- [1v 2025 O] Decline bench press is redundant {{0O0nG2jLadU@195}}

## [chest] push-up / ring / chest / upper (12)
- [1v 2020 P] His chest training is very simple: bench press, push-ups and Olympic rings. {{D3EIDEZqCwU@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Push-up variations can bias the upper chest {{m-XeEY4xRDU@310}}
- [1v 2020 O] Ring push-ups are harder and better for chest than push-ups thanks to deeper stretch and free wrist/elbow angles {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1600}}
- [1v 2022 A] NH builds his chest with a mix of bench press and push-ups; bodyweight or weighted push-ups alone are not enough stimulus for him {{vyKzL85QwY4@0}}
- [1v 2022 P] Ring push-ups are his favorite pec-protection lift: the elbows travel far back and the hands reach chest level, which a bench cannot allow {{yoROLGGUPXU@372}}
- [1v 2023 A] NH has fallen in love with decline ring push-ups and favors them over regular ring push-ups for upper chest and shoulders {{_P0-kYyVMn4@568}}
- [1v 2023 P] Ring push-ups, elevated, are a hard variation he uses for upper chest and shoulders {{ABTeMrXdgI4@660}}
- [1v 2024 P] Rings are still useful for bodybuilding because straight-arm movements give an exceptional stretch; ring push-ups are great for the chest {{tbY5VMSmlP8@762}}
- [1v 2024 A] Bench suits him only with a very close, paused grip; he built his chest with push-ups and now grows best on weighted incline ring push-ups and dips with three plates, pain-free {2024-04 {"load": "dips +3 plates"}} {{Ft039H8jzXU@1508}}
- [1v 2024 P] Ring decline push-ups hit the whole chest, especially the upper chest, and give more range of motion than floor push-ups; go down until the rings reach the armpits for a big pec stretch {{JW73oft8Q60@66}}
- [1v 2024 P] Weighted ring push-ups (backpack) are an insanely good chest builder, recommended even by Louie Simmons {{6p2x4kDDjzs@5111}}
- [1v 2024 A] He dropped the bench press after repeated shoulder injuries; his chest originally grew from push-ups at home and he only went back to benching because of powerlifting dogma; ring push-ups and dips are S-tier {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@821}}

## [chest] incline / overhead / press / chest (7)
- [2v 2020-2024 OP] A chest program with only incline and decline dumbbell pressing and no flat movement is worth fixing; find a flat press you connect with also: 2020-08: Incline and decline presses work better with dumbbells, flat horizontal presses with a barbell {{jSUmn33lmlI@626}} {{yZC3Nsmmzb8@2500}}
- [2v 2021-2022 O] Incline bench 3x8 is a hybrid of horizontal and vertical pressing that gives more chest; some may prefer overhead press {2021-06 {"reps": 8, "sets": 3} | 2022-09 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": "4"}} {{ZkQZPnTVKpg@501}} {{JnWWva91QUM@1379}}
- [1v 2020 P] Incline bench 3x8-10 is a good follow-up to either bench or overhead press since it is a mix of vertical and horizontal pressing. {2020-06 {"reps": "8-10", "sets": 3}} {{hi-kNbZnXz0@190}}
- [1v 2020 O] The incline bench is a bastardized lift between horizontal and overhead press; he isn't a fan but 6-10 reps is a good range for it and close-grip bench {2020-07 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@126}}
- [1v 2020 O] Telling people to incline bench for upper chest is usually bad advice; those who do get upper chest from it usually get it from overhead pressing too {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}}
- [1v 2020 P] On chest day prefer a pure vertical press over a second incline press since chest already gets plenty of work {{2ag0uM9zhs4@721}}
- [1v 2022 O] He dislikes the incline press because people wrongly assume it covers both bench and overhead press {{QfHfxLYRu88@944}}

## [chest] pec / deck / greater / aren't (4)
- [2v 2020-2022 OP] Straight-arm pec deck is mediocre: a longer lever forces less weight and shifts stress to the shoulder; the bent-arm pec deck is excellent {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@564}} {{cUkWvnY8AJI@687}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pec flies aren't worthless but aren't the best for shoulder health. {{rucwL-SIefg@1480}}
- [1v 2020 O] Scrap the pec deck: it's just squeezing, can create shoulder pain and is a fairly unnatural joint motion you never do in real life. {{0yIieHQjEiA@193}}
- [1v 2020 O] Dips are just as valid as bench press and may be better because they give a greater pec and shoulder stretch {{MIHPylz3f7Q@1489}}

## [chest] horizontal / grew / follow / mix (3)
- [2v 2020-2021 OP] Opening with a horizontal press then following with a vertical press is NH's favorite way to program a bench day and good for shoulder health {{hT4zgNcMOBY@752}} {{62OBPecQPnM@251}}
- [1v 2022 P] The best mix is a heavy horizontal press followed by ring push-ups {{vyKzL85QwY4@510}}
- [1v 2022 A] The bench press grew his chest, but it is not the only horizontal press {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@434}}

## [chest] dips / dip / higher / risk (3)
- [1v 2020 O] He wouldn't recommend dips over bench because some people can't do them safely, they carry higher shoulder injury risk, and progression is cumbersome with a dip belt. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@375}}
- [1v 2022 O] Dips are in many ways superior to the bench for hypertrophy but carry slightly higher shoulder injury risk if done poorly {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@434}}
- [1v 2024 P] Chain dips with a 5-lb chain are pointless; master proper dips first, then load them with plates and a dip belt {2024-05 {"load": "5 lb"}} {{Eta-sxecx9E@1385}}

## [chest] reward / risk-to / awkward / extend (3)
- [1v 2020 P] The bench is the best horizontal press because of its risk-to-reward ratio. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@313}}
- [1v 2020 O] Weighted push-ups are more risk than reward and awkward to set up. {{K4UBm1dV6WM@499}}
- [1v 2020 O] He dislikes dumbbell flyes with extended arms: more dangerous than rewarding. {{fSOiGuUPqBc@881}}

## [chest] start / chest / upper / dips (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Start an upper day with a compound lift that recruits the most muscle; bench or dips involve more muscle than overhead press, so start with one of those. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@187}}
- [1v 2023 O] The weighted dip is the 'squat of the upper body', S-tier for chest, triceps and shoulders; novices starting with dips instead of bench would build better physiques {{DAZRYoex8_w@255}}
- [1v 2024 P] Starting both upper days with a chest press means chest is the priority; never start a day with your heavy shoulder press if chest comes first {{6p2x4kDDjzs@1511}}

## [chest] dips / chest / you're / unles (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Bodybuilders shouldn't be jacks of all trades: doing dips, weighted dips, bench and weighted push-ups all for chest diverts attention unless you're a chest specialist. {{uRWgz3nhqds@314}}
- [1v 2020 O] Having both bench and dips as top-level horizontal presses creates too much variation for most; pick one as king unless you're a hyper-specialist {{6sFaB3rAY1A@570}}
- [1v 2024 P] If bench does not hit your chest, swap it for weighted push-ups and weighted dips, and add chest isolation on another day {{ZLheQPgEfoA@4117}}

## [chest] 3x6-10 / accessory / excellent / antagonist-supersett (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Incline dumbbell press for 3x6-10 is an excellent accessory to the barbell bench {2020-06 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{PYmr91aELnE@726}}
- [1v 2022 P] Seated machine row 3x10-12 antagonist-supersetted with dumbbell bench 3x6-10 or 8-10; dumbbells or a cambered or football bar spare the shoulders {2022-08 {"reps": "6-12", "sets": 3}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@720}}
- [1v 2022 O] Close-grip bench is excellent but often limited by the triceps and has less elbow flare; better to prioritize it over the Larsen press as the main chest accessory {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1440}}

## [chest] push-up / effective / practical / load (3)
- [1v 2020 P] For novices push-ups are as effective as dips or an empty-bar bench because they are intense; as you progress loaded presses win because push-ups cannot be loaded {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1256}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep doing push-ups as an advanced lifter by placing them at the end of a press day so fatigue pushes them into an effective intensity range {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1340}}
- [1v 2020 O] Weighted push-ups are less practical than dips or bench because they are hard to load alone {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1318}}

## [chest] light / rather / make / shap (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Rather than light bench then dumbbell press, make the dumbbell press the strength work {{niD0UuwTUvg@691}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rather than light flyes for 'shaping', do an actual press for real muscle fiber damage {{D2ERyDt4Pqo@388}}
- [1v 2022 O] Weighted push-ups work; calisthenics athletes have big chests at light bodyweights {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@498}}

## [chest] horizontal / dips / push-up / change (3)
- [1v 2021 P] The bench is just a horizontal press; dips or push-ups can replace it with no other change to volume or intensity {{o05wgvQtSMc@191}}
- [1v 2022 O] Sandow's sunken chest is explained by the lack of horizontal pressing at the time: no bench press or dips, and push-ups alone are not enough {{KtoAImbVZF4@1049}}
- [1v 2024 P] Use more than one horizontal press (bench, dumbbells, dips, ring or weighted push-ups) to reduce overuse injury risk and build carryover {{2LoP6-KdEx4@819}}

## [chest] push-up / deep / range / ring (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Ring push-ups remove the drawbacks of regular push-ups: no wrist pain and no floor limiting depth, so you can go as deep as you want {{vyKzL85QwY4@260}}
- [1v 2022 A] Because of his leverages NH doesn't get full pec range of motion on the bench, so he gets the deep stretch from ring push-ups {{vyKzL85QwY4@385}}
- [1v 2022 O] Push-ups are excellent: many built big chests from high-volume bodyweight push-ups, but range is limited by the floor and they can tax the wrists {{cUkWvnY8AJI@437}}

## [chest] plate / chest / press / svend (3)
- [1v 2022 O] Plate presses will not build a big chest {{umQZm2ALxl8@2109}}
- [1v 2022 O] Plate press is A-cup: it becomes a grip exercise with no stretch; the standing Svend press does not even load the chest because gravity pulls down {{cUkWvnY8AJI@1691}}
- [1v 2022 O] Upper chest shrug (Michael Gundill) is decent: great stimulus-to-fatigue ratio for upper chest without pressing {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2757}}

## [chest] fly / gorilla / squeeze / cable (3)
- [1v 2022 O] Low-to-high cable fly is Gorilla tier, his top cable fly: stretch behind the body with bent elbows, easy squeeze, 15 reps for 4 sets with low fatigue {2022-12 {"reps": 15, "sets": 4}} {{cUkWvnY8AJI@561}}
- [1v 2022 O] Straight-arm dumbbell flies are only decent; fly presses are Gorilla tier, giving the same stretch and squeeze with more weight and safety {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2257}}
- [1v 2022 O] High-to-low cable fly is excellent; mid-level cable fly is only decent because a lying dumbbell fly can replicate it {{cUkWvnY8AJI@561}}

## [chest] a-cup / floor / decent / chest (3)
- [1v 2022 O] Hex/squeeze press is decent; seated cable chest press is excellent, standing versions are A-cup because people cheat with the body {{cUkWvnY8AJI@3385}}
- [1v 2022 OP] Landmine press is A-cup and Viking press mediocre: the more you press in front of you, the more chest {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2569}}
- [1v 2022 O] Floor press is only decent because the floor blocks the full stretch; floor fly is A-cup {{cUkWvnY8AJI@2381}}

## [forearms] forearm / wrist / grip / fingertip (43)
- [2v 2020 AO] Arm wrestlers tend to have the biggest forearms and biceps of all natural athletes, so he copies their training; bodybuilding is about doing what works. also: 2020-03: You can borrow from sports training without doing the sport: he copies arm wrestlers' training for arm size without being an arm wrestler. {{yd3UnP8tKK4@0}} {{VVckf4vyYPE@629}}
- [1v 2020 D] NH learned the Coan grip from a video by Ed Coan, whom he calls the greatest powerlifter of all time. {{VUNsazYt-uo@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Grip limits many lifts; for 100-rep RDLs you'd need straps, and straps start cutting into the wrists and circulation beyond about 30-40 reps. {2020-02 {"reps": "30-40"}} {{uTHCzF7Exck@319}}
- [1v 2020 A] Regular wrist curls hurt his wrists; the only version that works for him is with the forearm resting on the leg using fixed dumbbells, because plates on adjustable dumbbells rotate around the handle. {{cjHnyJPijWk@188}}
- [1v 2020 P] For hypertrophy do hangs at the end of the workout: you are warm, the fatigued grip raises the intensity, and it saves time; not for people chasing a fresh record {{aoVexKzTOt0@314}}
- [1v 2020 P] A wrist flexion exercise before biceps can destroy the ability to keep the wrist rigid, causing mechanical failure before the biceps reach muscular failure {{Hn3oGdUUhic@316}}
- [1v 2020 P] For grip-specific heavy holds, use shrugs or above-the-knee rack pulls for time {{gCYXqFzqkVc@295}}
- [1v 2021 P] Standing wrist curls behind the back avoid the thighs, allow heavy partial-range overload, and work well with a thumbless grip {{a9xfj9LysUc@125}}
- [1v 2022 O] Grippers lack a stretch component; they build grip strength and mostly one lump near the hand, not overall forearm size {{iaNBZcYLMkY@1006}}
- [1v 2022 P] Do not let a small muscle like the forearm dictate the work of a big muscle, though fat grips make the back work harder {{Mt7cGMJZjkw@374}}
- [1v 2023 P] Step 1 (novice): pick one forearm isolation movement based on enjoyment, not optimality; treat it like picking a toy {{clzLnKrm6Ao@697}}
- [1v 2023 O] Hand grippers are C tier: concentric-only crush with no negative; strongmen, climbers and arm wrestlers don't use them {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2510}}
- [1v 2023 P] Rack pulls from above the knee, without straps and with a normal double-overhand grip, are an A tier forearm exercise because the bar never rolls and the lower back is barely taxed {2023-03 {"sets": 3}} {{iPtxiNtBY-M@501}}
- [1v 2023 P] A mixed grip does not train forearms more; it only stops the bar rolling, which straps do better, and the stimulus-to-fatigue ratio for forearms is terrible {{iPtxiNtBY-M@376}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pull-ups are a decent (B tier) forearm exercise, better than rows and deadlifts; if your hands open before your back fails you have a grip-to-back strength imbalance to fix {{iPtxiNtBY-M@439}}
- [1v 2023 P] Finger curls are the superior replacement for grippers: letting the weight roll to the fingertips gives a loaded negative; cable version A tier, dumbbell version S tier {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2638}}
- [1v 2023 O] Knuckle push-ups, fingertip push-ups and fingertip pull-ups do nothing for forearm hypertrophy beyond finger resilience {{iPtxiNtBY-M@3075}}
- [1v 2023 P] Judge a forearm exercise by which forearm function it trains (wrist flexion/extension, pronation/supination, elbow flexion, isometric hold) and how well it does it {{iPtxiNtBY-M@62}}
- [1v 2023 O] Supination work is only B tier for gym rats because curls already train supination {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1383}}
- [1v 2023 P] The preacher station is the best piece of equipment for forearms; preacher hammer curls are S tier because they lock the shoulder and elbow and give a big stretch {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1003}}
- [1v 2023 P] The back works in the bottom and middle of a pull-up; top-range partial neutral-grip pull-ups (arm wrestling pull-ups) mostly train forearms and are A tier {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2073}}
- [1v 2023 P] Reverse curls are S tier when done standing but only C tier on the preacher, where the bar drifts away from the stacked wrist and onto the fingertips {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1509}}
- [1v 2023 O] Deadlifting for forearms is the worst advice: letting grip limit a posterior-chain lift gives you neither forearms nor back gains (D tier) {{iPtxiNtBY-M@314}}
- [1v 2023 P] The dead hang is S tier: it isolates the forearm grip, decompresses the spine and aids recovery; do one at the end of every lower body day {{iPtxiNtBY-M@595}}
- [1v 2023 P] Building dead hangs up to 2-4 minutes will bring visible forearm development {2023-03 {"duration": "2-4 min"}} {{iPtxiNtBY-M@627}}
- [1v 2023 O] Gooseneck wrist curls, an old-school variation, are better for hypertrophy than normal wrist curls; uncomfortable but A tier {{iPtxiNtBY-M@790}}
- [1v 2023 O] Behind-the-back wrist curls allow heavy loads but have a poor, unnatural range of motion and clash with big glutes; C tier {{iPtxiNtBY-M@689}}
- [1v 2023 O] Barbell rows are an upper-back lift and a mediocre (C tier) forearm exercise; dumbbell rows sit between C and B {{iPtxiNtBY-M@376}}
- [1v 2023 O] Kettlebell flips are B tier: the bell is made to be grabbed and the move stresses only the forearms {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1948}}
- [1v 2023 O] Plate flips are a D tier circus act that trains coordination more than grip and risks broken toes {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1948}}
- [1v 2023 O] Rope climbing or rope pulling is B tier: full-hand grip with control of wrist and elbow angle {{iPtxiNtBY-M@3137}}
- [1v 2023 O] Plate pinches are C tier because they are impractical to progressively overload {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1824}}
- [1v 2023 O] Resisting a hammer's leverage is a B tier grip-sport test but hard to progress {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2951}}
- [1v 2023 O] The newspaper twist gives a burn but no mass and cannot be progressed; D tier {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2135}}
- [1v 2023 O] Axe ulnar deviation trains the brachioradialis and resistance is adjusted by hand position, but it can't be progressively overloaded, so reverse curls are better {{P7ADeDVghxg@994}}
- [1v 2023 P] Arm wrestling training ignores full range of motion and balanced development: you only train what you use, in table positions {{GvuVZo70o1Q@257}}
- [1v 2023 P] Hammer-grip preacher curls with a towel are S tier for forearms and brachioradialis; stop slightly below parallel to avoid overextending the tendon {{awwJ5sbyz6A@1077}}
- [1v 2024 P] Not using straps tires your grip so you have nothing left for direct forearm training, and gripping is a secondary function giving minimal hypertrophy {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@1512}}
- [1v 2024 O] Pronation curls are vastly superior to Zottman curls {{okm6bZHKHI4@14138}}
- [1v 2024 P] Cable hammer curls with the cable behind you allow a strong stretch {{c7hl9u3h_s0@4650}}
- [1v 2024 A] He carries the Husafell stone hugged like a big pot with hands underneath because the normal grip wrecks his biceps and forearm tendons; it is harder but more comfortable {{6yW3GSskl8g@569}}
- [1v 2024 P] Preacher-style curl with full pronation on a one-arm bar for reps of 12 with rest-pause {2024-12 {"reps": 12}} {{qvWxIzhT1QU@696}}
- [1v 2025 A] He swapped a painful handle finger curl for fat-grip gooseneck reverse curls {{waeSP7DBmig@390}}

## [forearms] finger / forearm / wrist / grip (8)
- [2v 2020 OP] Finger push-ups strengthen finger ligaments and tendons that most grip work doesn't reach, useful for rock climbers. {{S5i157GyQGo@78}} {{gCYXqFzqkVc@738}}
- [1v 2020 O] Devon Larratt finger curls are an excellent forearm exercise if wrist curls hurt. {{OIsOhJsOdHs@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Finger curls give a strong forearm stretch and build support grip but aren't pinch grip {{gCYXqFzqkVc@503}}
- [1v 2021 O] His favourite forearm isolation is the one-handed supinated finger curl with an EZ bar, keeping the wrist from bending back {{a9xfj9LysUc@378}}
- [1v 2022 P] Fat grips work because they are convenient: preacher curls with fat grips, then finger curls, trains biceps and forearms together {{iaNBZcYLMkY@942}}
- [1v 2022 O] Wrist curls are boring and often cause wrist tendonitis or no feel; he replaced all his wrist curls with finger curls {{iaNBZcYLMkY@64}}
- [1v 2023 P] Anyone doing wrist curls should switch to finger curls for 1-2 weeks and will never go back {2023-03 {"weeks": "1-2"}} {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2701}}
- [1v 2023 O] Rock climbing builds back and forearms (B tier) but is limited to bodyweight loading {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1129}}

## [forearms] towel / grip / used / time (5)
- [1v 2020 A] He used to do towel grip holds with chains instead of a towel: more stable but less comfortable, and a good towel for the exercise is hard to find. {{HdiVXNOFgmo@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hangs are not time consuming; do them two or three times a week since too often is counterproductive, and experiment with grips and implements like fat grips or towels {2020-06 {"frequency": "2-3x/wk"}} {{aoVexKzTOt0@754}}
- [1v 2020 P] All support grip variations (pull-ups, hangs, rows, shrugs, curls, rack pulls, towel holds) help and should be used to some degree {{gCYXqFzqkVc@405}}
- [1v 2020 P] Towel through a plate, deadlifted and held for time, changes wrist angle and involves the fingers more {{gCYXqFzqkVc@341}}
- [1v 2022 P] Towel hammer curl: the towel frees the wrist so you can flex up at the top and stretch at the bottom; he took it from arm wrestlers and replaced his hammer curls with it {{iaNBZcYLMkY@817}}

## [forearms] forearm / inner / bicep / zottman (5)
- [1v 2023 O] Regular biceps curls train the inner forearm as a byproduct (many people have dominant inner forearms from spamming curls) but are only C tier for forearms {{iPtxiNtBY-M@877}}
- [1v 2023 O] A supinated forearm curl with an appropriate, non-overextended range of motion is the king of wrist curls and S tier for the inner forearm {{iPtxiNtBY-M@814}}
- [1v 2023 O] Zottman curls are C tier: a lift that trains more than forearms trains forearms poorly, and the forearms limit the biceps load {{iPtxiNtBY-M@3264}}
- [1v 2023 P] Fat-grip preacher curls with a cupping grip (all fingers on one side, no thumb wrap) build the inner forearm and biceps; exaggerate wrist rotation on the way down to keep the weight from rolling out {2023-04 {"reps": "6-12"}} {{kQMrIszr_Bw@251}}
- [1v 2024 P] Going strapless on back work does not build forearms; most people lift without straps and still have stick forearms {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@1448}}

## [forearms] loop / twist / belt / wrist (3)
- [2v 2023 P] The pronation twist, learned from arm wrestlers, is S tier: load the hand with a belt or loop and rotate inward; it is joint-friendly and grows the often-neglected pronator {2023-03 {"load": "20-30 lb"} | 2023-04 {"reps": "10-20"}} also: 2023-04: Pronation twist: loop a belt or band deep in the hand near the wrist with a plate attached and rotate inward; vary elbow position and angle {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1194}} {{kQMrIszr_Bw@819}}
- [1v 2023 P] Dumbbell wrist twists are D tier: the weight pulls sideways instead of down, cannot be loaded properly and leads to tendinitis; train rotation with a belt loop and plates instead {{iPtxiNtBY-M@1446}}
- [1v 2023 P] The hangman curl (neutral-grip rope curl on a cable or a rope looped through a plate) is S tier: constant tension, stretched negative and free wrist angle {{iPtxiNtBY-M@3201}}

## [forearms] hold / grip / timed / barbell (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Holding the barbell after the last deadlift rep trains grip, and also the traps if you let the weight stretch them. {{mViZdjZIv6s@258}}
- [1v 2020 P] Timed holds build grip strength and calisthenic skill {2020-09 {"duration": "30 s", "sets": 3}} {{i4YmJhlw7cs@375}}
- [1v 2021 O] Deadlifts are a poor forearm builder; they are mostly a static grip hold {{YHm-qSHgGSk@150}}
- [1v 2021 P] Dead hangs carry over better to vertical pulls, barbell holds to horizontal pulls: specificity {{pvpf7Yuayco@860}}

## [forearms] hammer / forearm / excellent / bicep (4)
- [1v 2022 O] Hammer curls are excellent for forearms but only decent for biceps and can cause shoulder pain {{RwEJpHTwWm0@942}}
- [1v 2023 P] Hammer curls chosen as a biceps movement in the arm plan count as the novice forearm movement {{clzLnKrm6Ao@948}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pinwheel (cross-body) curls are his favourite curl and B tier for forearms; hammer curls are A tier as a brachioradialis builder even though they hurt his shoulders {{iPtxiNtBY-M@939}}
- [1v 2024 P] Preacher hammer curls are excellent; he now does them standing beside the bench for a huge stretch, and they are easy on the tendons {{okm6bZHKHI4@13868}}

## [forearms] farmer / walk / super / yoke (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Farmer walks done properly with real weight are like a super deadlift and should not be supersetted {{du7JYCA2pa4@435}}
- [1v 2020 P] Farmer walks build grip, forearms and yoke together {{gCYXqFzqkVc@743}}
- [1v 2021 A] Farmer walks are genuinely hard, harder than a squat single; Blaha quit after about two weeks carrying around 100 lb per hand {2021-09 {"load": "100 lb/hand", "weeks": 2}} {{eaypnfdz3J0@1259}}

## [forearms] forearm / farmer' / taxing / hallmark (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Farmer's carries build forearms and hips but are very taxing; program them like a deadlift or squat {{QfHfxLYRu88@379}}
- [1v 2023 O] Farmer's walks without straps for time will grow your forearms (A tier), but they tax the rest of the body too much to be S tier; strongmen have jacked forearms from them {{iPtxiNtBY-M@553}}
- [1v 2023 P] The hallmark of a good forearm exercise is that it stimulates only the forearms without taxing other joints or muscles {{iPtxiNtBY-M@2011}}

## [forearms] wrist / elbow / step / fat (3)
- [1v 2023 P] Step 3 (intermediate): add 1-2 new forearm movements, either new wrist/elbow motions or pulls modified so the forearm is the limiting factor (fat grip pull-ups, towel curls, fat-grip hammer curls) {{clzLnKrm6Ao@1202}}
- [1v 2023 P] Step 6 (advanced): add 2-3 more movements to cover every wrist and elbow function and rotate; some biceps isolation can be swapped for hybrid lifts {{clzLnKrm6Ao@1579}}
- [1v 2024 P] Fat grips ruin hammer curls by turning a brachialis movement into grip work; they suit pull-ups, supinated curls, and triceps work like V-bar extensions or skull crushers, where they ease wrists and elbows {{ZLheQPgEfoA@628}}

## [full_body] body / cover / heavy / flexion (38)
- [1v 2020 O] If you can do them properly, squats and deadlifts are your best friends for lower body hypertrophy, though he doesn't say they are enough for everyone. {{awNEfmfGkmY@0}}
- [1v 2020 P] Deadlifts let you pile up a lot of lower-body tonnage early in a session with heavy loads, saving time on other lower-body movements. {{0yIieHQjEiA@381}}
- [1v 2020 O] Because the deadlift hits so many muscles with maximal loads, it spreads damage widely and racks up large tonnage in little time, saving work later in the session {{boPKiKE26Rw@444}}
- [1v 2020 P] Farmer's walks are taxing; use them as strength work at the start of a session or give them their own day, not after everything {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@945}}
- [1v 2020 P] Farmer walks can be programmed as strength work, GPP or their own day {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1194}}
- [1v 2020 O] Favorite athletic-hypertrophy crossover moves: high pulls with full triple extension, sprints, swimming, push-ups and pull-ups {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1568}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pure calisthenics leaves gains on the table because it lacks a hip hinge and heavy weighted knee flexion, shortchanging glutes, hamstrings, lower back and rhomboids {{ZScs2L9rero@120}}
- [1v 2020 P] A size program needs a vertical press, horizontal press, knee flexion, hip hinge, horizontal pull and vertical pull, since these cover the bulk of the musculature {{ZScs2L9rero@150}}
- [1v 2020 O] The deadlift is one of the best, if not the best, exercises for growing the entire body, contrary to claims it is bad for hypertrophy {{ZScs2L9rero@695}}
- [1v 2020 P] Example lower giant set: Romanian deadlift, shrugs with the same weight, calves, leg raises {{ehxjCB_MdKc@940}}
- [1v 2020 P] The deadlift works glutes, lower back, hamstrings, some quads, the entire upper back, forearms, arms, shoulders and neck: the most full-body compound {{3IMgRqcRN4Y@186}}
- [1v 2021 P] Cover the body with overlap: squats largely handle the lower body, supersetted with calf raises; bench covers the upper body, with triceps extensions supersetted later {{G7rVTseabFU@1506}}
- [1v 2021 P] Mistake two: stacking every compound lift into one day overloads overlapping muscles, especially the lower back, leading to injuries, plateaus and over-fatigue {{G7rVTseabFU@249}}
- [1v 2021 P] Every template should be compounds completed by isolation, with most tonnage from compounds and variations of compounds added as volume needs rise {{G7rVTseabFU@312}}
- [1v 2021 O] Mistake one: a full body made only of machines is just a machine circuit; machines dictate the path, and two years of it gives minimal gains {2021-01 {"years": 2}} {{G7rVTseabFU@125}}
- [1v 2021 P] Squats and deadlifts move the most weight and drive the most hypertrophy, but tall lifters have to approach them differently {{3nyuEtmqL-I@160}}
- [1v 2021 P] A heavy squat followed by Romanian deadlifts covers quads, glutes, hamstrings and lower back: three heavy squat sets and three or four RDL sets of 6-12 {2021-09 {"reps": "6-12", "sets": "3 + 3-4"}} {{yvhW-zyhpsM@504}}
- [1v 2021 P] Cycling Purist template: Day A squats 3x3, hyperextensions 3x10-12 plus seated calf raises 3x12-15, push-ups plus straight-arm planks AMRAP; Day B RDLs 3x6-8, lunges 3x10-12 plus triceps extensions 3x12-15, forearm planks plus single-leg hip thrusts AMRAP {2021-09 {"reps": "3-15", "sets": 3}} {{cNGPCn9Uupc@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Explosive lifts waste hypertrophy potential: the lifter tosses the bar and is not resisting it for much of the lift, while a bodybuilder wants to stay in control of the bar {{_Ak93cVwd1c@628}}
- [1v 2021 P] The Olympic lifts are essentially positive-only; the catch and drop remove the eccentric, which is the portion most important for hypertrophy {{_Ak93cVwd1c@720}}
- [1v 2022 O] He prefers trap bar or Jefferson deadlifts for fighters: less technical, the load sits under you, less lower-back torque and a more upright torso; sumo is for sumo specialists or very tall men {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2171}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hang power cleans or landmine thrusters teach gathering energy by dipping and exploding up; weighted rotations are probably overkill since rotation is trained in skill work {2022-01 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@540}}
- [1v 2022 P] Systema and krav maga programs are well-rounded because self-defence practitioners are less specialised {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@3388}}
- [1v 2022 O] By daily-use logic, squats and deadlifts (knee flexion and hip hinge) and even curls are more functional than pull-ups {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@634}}
- [1v 2022 O] He agrees humans should keep a full range of motion even if those motions aren't used daily {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@317}}
- [1v 2022 A] Drop lifts that do not suit your build, e.g. a tall long-legged lifter and conventional deadlifts; he dropped sumo because it hurt his ribs {{yoROLGGUPXU@1730}}
- [1v 2022 P] The deadlift's flaws (no eccentric stretch, high fatigue, poor stimulus-to-fatigue) are real but only a problem if you program it badly; keep floor deadlift volume minimal {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@117}}
- [1v 2022 P] Because the deadlift hits many muscles diffusely, use it to open a back/posterior-chain lower day, then focus on the muscles you want to grow {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@191}}
- [1v 2022 P] (Guest Bald Omni Man) Powerlifting-only training with no isolation is hogwash; work every muscle group {{E1er3oQ3IeM@4036}}
- [1v 2023 O] Barbell row is C tier for the posterior chain because the hinge is static; it does a bit of everything and nothing properly {{9L90ktKGPLw@314}}
- [1v 2023 O] Jefferson curl is B tier: a prehab exercise for spinal flexion, not a mass builder {{9L90ktKGPLw@1573}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lower-back isolation is pointless; heavy pulls from the floor already train it {{9L90ktKGPLw@2075}}
- [1v 2024 P] The deadlift leaves the arms and small core details untouched, lacks an eccentric (a big part of each rep), and has a terrible stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@254}}
- [1v 2024 P] The routine ignores whole muscle groups: no pull-up or row for the back, nothing for biceps, squats are not enough for hamstrings, and push-ups only partly cover triceps and shoulders, so the physique ends up unbalanced {{RPW-PxhYqrA@440}}
- [1v 2024 O] Calisthenics/street workout is the king of performance sports for aesthetics and hypertrophy; when many athletes in a sport look great, something in their training should be copied {{2LoP6-KdEx4@250}}
- [1v 2024 O] The perfect performance-based hypertrophy program is calisthenics for the upper body and free weights for the lower body; NH trains this way {{2LoP6-KdEx4@1130}}
- [1v 2024 O] Olympic lifts are terrible for hypertrophy because they are highly technical and depend on balance; power cleans and snatch-grip variants are more usable {{okm6bZHKHI4@1219}}
- [1v 2024 O] Heavy suitcase deadlift is not great for hypertrophy but is a good quick lift when he has only about 25 minutes to train {2024-12 {"time": "25 min"}} {{qvWxIzhT1QU@64}}

## [full_body] neck / head / long / delt (5)
- [1v 2020 O] For hypertrophy he would never recommend it: long head triceps, traps, neck, rear and medial delts, calves, mid traps, lats and biceps get little or no work {{YcHkXwsIOI8@1098}}
- [1v 2020 P] To round out the program add shrugs for traps, behind-the-head cable extensions for the long head, face pulls for rear delts, direct neck work for a bigger neck and wrist flexion for forearms {{du7JYCA2pa4@1317}}
- [1v 2020 O] Starting Strength has too much knee flexion and lacks elbow flexion, wrist flexion, horizontal pulls, calf, neck, ab and hyperextension work; long head triceps, rear delts and upper back are neglected {{et6AylDr1uk@882}}
- [1v 2021 O] If doable, push-ups would grow delts, chest and triceps, rope climbs biceps, forearms and upper back, neck bridges the neck, while bodyweight squats would not grow the legs much {{ivrGNRW0rwE@313}}
- [1v 2023 P] The big three plus OHP and rows still miss biceps, neck, calves, forearms, abs and the triceps long head {{elk4uYvjXos@316}}

## [full_body] clean / jerk / snatch / power (4)
- [1v 2021 O] Ranked for hypertrophy, the snatch and clean and jerk sit at the very bottom; they were designed to be easy to judge and demonstrate power, not build muscle {{_Ak93cVwd1c@560}}
- [1v 2021 P] Bastardization: cut the range of motion to reduce technicality, e.g. power cleans, cleans from a rack/hang, snatches from blocks {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1430}}
- [1v 2021 P] Dehybridization: split a complex lift into its parts; the clean and jerk becomes a pull, a front squat and an overhead press {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1350}}
- [1v 2021 O] Separating the clean and jerk into components is less time-efficient but around five times better for hypertrophy {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1410}}

## [full_body] every / each / body / cover (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Go through every body part when choosing exercises to make sure each is covered, noting the diffuse tonnage each lift adds {{x3YHLNqftr4@314}}
- [1v 2021 P] Hitting every muscle with one isolation exercise each takes far too long to work as a full body {{G7rVTseabFU@187}}
- [1v 2023 O] The bare minimum for a bodybuilding program is a 'big ten' covering every body part, not a big three or five {{ABTeMrXdgI4@754}}

## [glutes] glut / thrust / hip / reverse (34)
- [1v 2020 P] With his vertical torso and already overpowered glutes, sumo only made him feel his glutes, not his back, traps or legs, so it made a strong point even more dominant. {{7C5gtGrWYM0@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] Compound movements smoke glutes, hamstrings and lower back so no isolation is needed; he never isolated hip musculature and never had issues. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@944}}
- [1v 2020 P] Depending on leverages, hip thrusts are more glute-focused and RDLs more hamstring and lower-back focused. {{rucwL-SIefg@1040}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hip thrusts can rotate with Romanian deadlifts: both are hip hinges working glutes and hamstrings; hip thrusts emphasise quads more, RDLs the lower back. {{hi-kNbZnXz0@688}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hyperextensions and hip thrusts are low-specificity lifts; alternate them rather than doing both, even rotating weekly {{eobG2o3Ww4M@313}}
- [1v 2021 P] Tall lifters should try the sumo deadlift: it lets them keep a neutral spine and still trains the hamstrings and glutes hard while loading the lower back less {{3nyuEtmqL-I@245}}
- [1v 2021 P] Hyperextensions and reverse glute-ham raises are great for tall lifters, and you can move some volume from the sumo to them because they are safer {{3nyuEtmqL-I@490}}
- [1v 2021 P] Rotate weekly: do single-leg hip thrusts for 4 sets of 10-15 one week, then replace them with the Nordic curl and back extension superset the next {2021-06 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@950}}
- [1v 2021 O] Isolating glutes is a meme for most men; squats and deadlifts build them, and hip thrusts only emphasize rather than isolate {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@440}}
- [1v 2022 O] Hyperextensions, bridges and reverse hypers are valid, but they prioritize glutes and hamstrings with the lower back as stabilizer {{dVssxcEh1UQ@1410}}
- [1v 2022 O] The hip thrust is only bad if it replaces squats and deadlifts; as an added lift it is fine, 4x12-15, always above 10 reps {2022-06 {"reps": "12-15", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@687}}
- [1v 2022 P] Sumo deficit deadlifts assume you already pull sumo well; a conventional deficit or Romanian deadlift makes more sense {{JnWWva91QUM@1128}}
- [1v 2022 P] The close-stance safety-bar box squat forces a deadlift-like bottom position, heavily loading glutes, hamstrings and erectors, so it can replace a floor pull for glutes {{vlnnV8BdWCE@63}}
- [1v 2022 P] Use Bulgarian split squats as a secondary movement with modest weight for 10-15 reps; failure and drop sets are fine and the forward lean lowers knee stress {2022-09 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{vlnnV8BdWCE@1160}}
- [1v 2022 P] Don't manipulate general lifts to bias glutes, like pushing the butt back on squats; choose movements that force glute use {{vlnnV8BdWCE@582}}
- [1v 2022 P] Without a safety squat bar you can use a low-bar position, but it isn't quite the same lift {{vlnnV8BdWCE@63}}
- [1v 2023 O] Reverse hyperextension is A tier, a rehab movement that also builds glutes; Louie Simmons fixed his back with it; don't rebound {{9L90ktKGPLw@1636}}
- [1v 2023 O] Hip thrust off a bench with full range is S tier for glutes and hamstrings; he skips it himself because his glutes are big enough {{9L90ktKGPLw@2390}}
- [1v 2023 O] Split squats are B tier for the posterior chain; lean the torso forward in a banana path to make them strongly glute-dominant {{9L90ktKGPLw@880}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bodyweight donkey kicks push against air and do almost nothing; leg-press kickbacks are C tier and risky for the spine {{9L90ktKGPLw@1195}}
- [1v 2023 O] Forward lunge is polio tier for posterior chain: it is knee-dominant and people rush and rebound the negative {{9L90ktKGPLw@942}}
- [1v 2023 O] Hip abduction machine is C tier for the posterior chain; the adductor machine is a more interesting isolation {{9L90ktKGPLw@1826}}
- [1v 2023 O] Butt blaster machine is A tier: pad in the right spot, no strength-curve problem, but few gyms have it {{9L90ktKGPLw@1322}}
- [1v 2023 O] Cable kickbacks are B tier: awkward ankle strap and a resistance curve that is hardest at the start {{9L90ktKGPLw@1322}}
- [1v 2023 O] Cable pull-through is B tier: easy to mess up and a worse swing; useful only to learn the pattern {{9L90ktKGPLw@1888}}
- [1v 2023 O] Reverse lunge is S tier: stable, safe deep range, strong stretch; go heavy and to failure {{9L90ktKGPLw@942}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lateral step-ups are C tier: hard to load, too much balance, almost no stretch {{9L90ktKGPLw@817}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lateral lunge is C tier: too much hip shift and ligament stress, hard to load {{9L90ktKGPLw@1006}}
