- [1v 2023 O] Banded crab walks are polio tier: garbage resistance, pattern and no stretch {{9L90ktKGPLw@817}}
- [1v 2023 O] Frog pumps are polio tier {{9L90ktKGPLw@1763}}
- [1v 2023 O] Joel has almost no posterior chain because he skips hip hinges (no RDLs, good mornings or deadlifts), which Golden Era lifters avoided to keep the waist small {{Mn34v5Y1d-0@3331}}
- [1v 2024 P] Reverse lunges with an SSB destroy the glutes more than anything and spare the lower back; hold supports only for balance {{sZntx8mqaH8@566}}
- [1v 2024 O] He dislikes hip thrusts because there is almost always a better option {{c7hl9u3h_s0@7318}}
- [1v 2024 O] Reverse lunges can replace RDLs; they're underrated and are a different movement, not a weaker lunge {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@3438}}

## [glutes] glute / thrust / hip / bridg (6)
- [2v 2022-2024 P] If the glutes are weak, Bulgarian split squats and single-leg hip thrusts teach glute activation with knee flexion also: 2024-09: Bulgarian split squats beat hip thrusts when time is short; hip thrusts need huge loads {{yoROLGGUPXU@1128}} {{okm6bZHKHI4@13741}}
- [2v 2022-2023 O] For glute hypertrophy the hip thrust beats the glute bridge because it has more range of motion; bridges suit deadlift accessory work also: 2023-04: Barbell glute bridge is B tier because the floor limits range of motion {{vlnnV8BdWCE@456}} {{9L90ktKGPLw@2264}}
- [1v 2022 P] Punching power comes from the floor via hip rotation, so lower-body and glute work is mandatory; boxing programs full of bench press are wrong {{v-F3QHSM2hg@1877}}
- [1v 2022 P] For MMA he uses double-leg hip thrusts because being mounted means bridging with both legs to escape ground and pound; glute bridges are a more specific option {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2049}}
- [1v 2022 P] Single-leg hip thrusts suit BJJ because in positions like side control one leg is often pinned and you can only drive with one glute {2022-01 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1383}}
- [1v 2022 O] Glute cable kickbacks are unnecessary; the guys he knows built big glutes strictly from squats and deadlifts and he has never done one {{uYRIWAKlA0c@1506}}

## [glutes] thrust / hip / single-leg / load (3)
- [1v 2022 P] If his lower back is tired after SSB box squats he swaps good mornings for hip thrusts to train the posterior chain without spinal loading {{itnjfi6RYtY@940}}
- [1v 2023 O] Single-leg hip thrust is A tier and should be loaded, not done bodyweight only {{9L90ktKGPLw@880}}
- [1v 2024 O] Hip thrusts are redundant after squats, RDLs and leg press; he dropped them long ago and finds them weak even for glutes {{6p2x4kDDjzs@5210}}

## [hamstrings] rdls / hamstr / hip / hamstring (60)
- [1v 2020 P] Stiff-legged RDLs in this program must be light: the aim is to move through the range of motion and build the hamstrings, not to lift heavy. {2020-01 {"reps": "15-20"}} {{xFVva4-swKc@233}}
- [1v 2020 O] Most people doing RDLs are really doing stiff-legged deadlifts; the RDL is essentially the negative portion of a stiff-leg deadlift, so the separate name is semantically odd, though too entrenched to change. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@288}}
- [1v 2020 A] In his country/Europe the RDL was taught as a reverse deadlift: negative first from the rack, full range until the plates almost touch the ground, then back up; this is how NH does them. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@79}}
- [1v 2020 P] The popular short-range RDL allows heavy loads, sometimes more than a conventional deadlift, by using the stretch reflex and a short range, especially for short-femured lifters. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@120}}
- [1v 2020 D] The most-viewed YouTube RDL tutorials show minimal knee bend, lots of hip travel backward, a stretch on lower back and hamstrings, ending just below the kneecap. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@49}}
- [1v 2020 P] The full-range reverse deadlift forces much lighter loads because, as reps accumulate, the positive portions become increasingly taxing. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@156}}
- [1v 2020 P] The popular RDL is a 'pure hinge' with almost no knee or quad involvement, giving a better stretch, usually done for very low reps. {{cknNgkOKy8Q@185}}
- [1v 2020 O] 3x10-12 on RDLs is fine if heavy: the RDL starts with the negative, so it's easier to grind reps than on squats. {2020-06 {"reps": "10-12", "sets": 3}} {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@374}}
- [1v 2020 P] Instead of hip thrust machine, GHD, reverse hyper and leg curls, do barbell RDLs 4x8-12 plus one isolation like hip thrusts or reverse hypers {2020-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{TD9SB9Bt45o@505}}
- [1v 2020 P] The leg curl is the lift best suited to negatives and forced reps {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1978}}
- [1v 2020 P] Dorian's 'deadlift' is actually an RDL; 8-10 reps is too high for conventional or sumo deadlifts but a good range for RDLs {2020-10 {"reps": "8-10"}} {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1288}}
- [1v 2020 P] Drop awkward banded hamstring curls since stiff-legged deadlifts already give the stretch; if you like them, do them on rest days to boost recovery {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@965}}
- [1v 2020 P] RDLs suit a 4x8-12 window because they quickly enter dangerous territory for muscle strains {2020-12 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{niD0UuwTUvg@442}}
- [1v 2020 P] RDLs should follow squats while legs are warm because they have a mild-to-high technical rating, unlike incline dumbbell press {{DQo0DGn_wI4@790}}
- [1v 2020 O] Attaching 10 lb ankle weights for sprints to build hamstrings is terrible advice: it ruins sprint form and strains knee and ankle tendons and risks hamstring tears {2020-12 {"load": "10 lb"}} {{sFn68CtUaXI@1369}}
- [1v 2021 P] Tall lifters who want to keep heavy conventionals should do rack or block pulls from whatever height works; as long as the bar starts below the kneecap you still get hamstring and glute work {{3nyuEtmqL-I@400}}
- [1v 2021 P] Stiff-leg and Romanian deadlifts suit tall lifters: with a long torso you can bring it parallel to the floor and control lumbar rounding by stopping where you need to {{3nyuEtmqL-I@350}}
- [1v 2021 P] Good mornings carry over to both squat and deadlift, mostly by building rigidity through the lumbar and thoracic spine {{sFMQj4Lflk0@188}}
- [1v 2021 P] Never do RDLs and good mornings on the same day: both are hinges loading the lower back, a recipe for injury; rotate them week to week instead {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@2265}}
- [1v 2021 P] Do the deadlift first in the session, then 3x3 back-off sets {2021-05 {"reps": 3, "sets": 3}} {{HH7kCVkRyJc@563}}
- [1v 2021 O] Four sets of RDLs beat four sets of hamstring curls or Nordic curls; isolation can complement the heavy lifts but never replace them {2021-09 {"sets": 4}} {{yvhW-zyhpsM@504}}
- [1v 2022 P] For judo and sumo avoid pure knee-flexion lifts like front squats; pick squats with forward lean, because the posterior chain and hip hinge are king in every martial art {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2849}}
- [1v 2022 O] Good mornings may suit in-fighters (lifting the opponent's body with the torso) and deficit deadlifts out-boxers, but both are hip hinges so preference is fine {2022-01 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@602}}
- [1v 2022 P] GHRs or hyperextensions train the posterior chain without leg drive from the ground, useful when an opponent takes away your base {2022-01 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1258}}
- [1v 2022 P] Split-stance landmine RDLs mirror the bladed boxing stance and build hamstrings and explosiveness {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@792}}
- [1v 2022 P] Staggered-stance RDLs are specific because wrestlers fight from a staggered stance {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1793}}
- [1v 2022 P] Deadlift 5-7 reps max, 5 is enough, for 1-3 sets; for hamstring and glute volume use another hip hinge for 3x6-10 or 3x8-12 {2022-02 {"reps": "5-7", "sets": "1-3"}} {{wLx5BRx8AaU@1407}}
- [1v 2022 A] Snap-grip hyperextensions hammer his hamstrings, have never injured him and recover easily, yet he rarely did them as an optional exercise; now they are programmed on every lower-body day {{BW-32tYoNxw@886}}
- [1v 2022 P] Lying hamstring curls on leg day stretch the hamstrings and help prevent injury, but drop-setting to 30 reps is unnecessary for novices {{uYRIWAKlA0c@1316}}
- [1v 2022 P] Wednesday starts with RDLs 3x8-12, only 3 sets because it is peak posterior-chain fatigue; squats go first on Monday since they are riskier when fatigued {2022-06 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@562}}
- [1v 2022 P] Stiff-leg deadlifts are more strength-oriented accessories; Romanian deadlifts are more hypertrophy-oriented due to more stretch {2022-09 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": "3-4"}} {{JnWWva91QUM@1190}}
- [1v 2022 O] Opening a leg day with leg curls works for Israetel because his knee-flexion machines have almost no hip hinge component; for normal squatters, hamstring work first limits the squat {{gizylGr0ebo@877}}
- [1v 2023 P] RDLs 4x8-12 or stiff-legged deadlifts 4x6-10 open the pull day {2023-01 {"sets": 4}} {{_P0-kYyVMn4@1199}}
- [1v 2023 O] Good mornings or Romanian deadlifts are preferable to back extensions after a hack squat, since the machine leaves the lower back fresh {2023-04 {"sets": 3}} {{aJ1e41y8Na4@379}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lying leg curl is S tier and his one desired machine: full stretch, every rep is hamstring, no lower-back tax; seated curl is A tier {{9L90ktKGPLw@2013}}
- [1v 2023 P] Single-leg RDL is S tier and underrated: more hamstring and glute stretch than the bilateral version; use a band round the rack or a belt squat machine for balance {{9L90ktKGPLw@691}}
- [1v 2023 O] Nordic curls are A tier, not S, because they are not beginner friendly and injury prone; control the negative and don't fall and rebound {{9L90ktKGPLw@1069}}
- [1v 2023 O] Back hyperextension is S tier: teaches hip hinge, easy on the lower back, progresses from bodyweight to dumbbell to barbell for years {{9L90ktKGPLw@1636}}
- [1v 2023 P] Quads and posterior chain get separate tier lists because a movement great for quads is usually poor for hamstrings and vice versa {{9L90ktKGPLw@0}}
- [1v 2023 D] Studies he read show the glute-ham raise gives better hamstring activation than Nordics because it is more stable; GHR is S tier {{9L90ktKGPLw@1132}}
- [1v 2023 O] Razor curl (bent-knee Nordic/GHR) is a beginner-friendly B-tier hamstring movement {{9L90ktKGPLw@1069}}
- [1v 2023 O] Stiff-leg deadlift (bar pushed away from midfoot, pulling with hamstrings) is A tier but lacks an emphasized negative {{9L90ktKGPLw@440}}
- [1v 2023 O] The Romanian deadlift is S tier in every style, bar close to shins or floated away from them {{9L90ktKGPLw@503}}
- [1v 2023 P] Tiers: D polio (avoid), C mediocre, B decent, A excellent, S horse; pick from S and A {{9L90ktKGPLw@126}}
- [1v 2023 P] Legs need fewer exercises than upper body; get good at a couple of hip hinges {{9L90ktKGPLw@2452}}
- [1v 2023 P] If a lift lacks the stretch component it cannot rank above decent {{9L90ktKGPLw@251}}
- [1v 2023 P] Program both deadlifts and RDLs; deadlifts at low volume, high intensity: 1x5 or 3x3, or block pulls 3x6-12 {2023-05 {"reps": "3-5", "sets": "1-3"}} {{d1eLAd5WvIA@3375}}
- [1v 2023 O] Single-leg RDLs are his favorite hamstring exercise: they barely tax the lower back, work hip external rotation and are rehab-friendly {{6KA80BQ9W3k@755}}
- [1v 2023 O?] Stiff-legged variation gives unmatched hamstring stretch and likely trains middle and lower traps {{zuvTDBbAWN8@1259}}
- [1v 2023 O] Flexibility, like toe-touching, mostly exists for its own sake unless your sport or job needs it; heavy deadlifts gave him bigger hamstrings and glutes {{ZN5ohmPPfjM@943}}
- [1v 2024 P] Balance squat days with a hip hinge instead of only knee-flexion or extension work {{Ft039H8jzXU@754}}
- [1v 2024 P] Deadlifts, deficit pulls and Pendlay rows have no eccentric; RDLs are better because you get the stretch {{Ft039H8jzXU@1384}}
- [1v 2024 A] He dropped Zercher good mornings because holding the bar, not the posterior chain, became the limiting factor {{JW73oft8Q60@790}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do not do RDLs right after heavy deadlifts taken to failure; rotate them on different weeks {{okm6bZHKHI4@5808}}
- [1v 2024 P] Doing deadlifts twice a week is redundant; swap one for another hip hinge {2024-09 {"frequency": "2x/wk"}} {{okm6bZHKHI4@12646}}
- [1v 2024 O] Leg curls with a dumbbell are not recommended because the strength curve is all over the place {{okm6bZHKHI4@14138}}
- [1v 2024 P] Back extensions count as a hip hinge; if you progress them that's enough {{c7hl9u3h_s0@2094}}
- [1v 2024 P] Stimulus-to-fatigue must be judged per target muscle: hamstring curls beat deadlifts on SFR for hamstrings, though deadlifts give more total stimulus; preacher curls beat barbell cheat curls for biceps {{6yW3GSskl8g@382}}
- [1v 2024 P] Swap deadlifts for Romanian deadlifts: better stretch, eccentric, constant tension on hamstrings and traps {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@444}}
- [1v 2025 P] Snatch-grip hyperextensions holding 25-lb plates rather than 45s give more range and a hamstring bias; elevate the bench to bias the lower back {{waeSP7DBmig@790}}

## [hamstrings] hing / leg / hip / hamstring (12)
- [2v 2020-2021 O] Leg extensions give no carryover and can be removed; leg curls are worth keeping because they hit the hamstrings in a way hinges cannot also: 2020-11: Prefer a hip hinge over leg curls: leg curls only grow the hamstrings, hinges give much more hypertrophy {{9jAjNyxeiXc@689}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@810}}
- [1v 2020 P] 3x8-12 is an excellent rep range for hip hinges like the Romanian deadlift. {2020-06 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{hi-kNbZnXz0@688}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do good mornings at the end of a day whose earlier knee flexion and hinges warmed the hamstrings and glutes without loading the lower back; do not pick them after heavy deadlifts {{oLm1CDuY9zg@650}}
- [1v 2020 O] Lying leg curls are one of the few good machine exercises; they take the hamstrings past the ROM of a hip hinge {2020-09 {"reps": 12, "sets": 3}} {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@1384}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hip hinges should be included whenever you can tolerate them; they also give free upper-back hypertrophy {{9jAjNyxeiXc@689}}
- [1v 2021 P] Programs need a dedicated static hip hinge such as the Romanian deadlift; leg curls do not make up for missing hip hinges {{8mGHG5C-EPE@812}}
- [1v 2021 P] Leg curls without a hip hinge waste time; after deadlifts they complement the hinge by working the stretch and preventing injury {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@2380}}
- [1v 2022 P] Weighted hip hinges and knee flexions are necessary for real leg development {{QfHfxLYRu88@379}}
- [1v 2023 P] Only isolate muscles that don't get much work from compounds; glutes and lower back grow from hinges and squats, but hamstrings often stay small in strong natural lifters {{9L90ktKGPLw@1950}}
- [1v 2023 O] Wide, high-foot stance leg press is C tier; keep the leg press for quads {{9L90ktKGPLw@2203}}
- [1v 2024 A] He trains legs only with free weights and hip hinges despite evidence for leg curls and isolation {{vVQmYGw7Kj8@1954}}
- [1v 2024 O] Hinges suffice for hamstrings but leg curls are free volume if available {{c7hl9u3h_s0@8592}}

## [hamstrings] hamstr / development / pain / hinge (8)
- [1v 2020 O] Hamstring curls are good for hamstring development, but some people get knee pain from them. {{4aThavRpYLw@1136}}
- [1v 2020 P] Replace hamstring curls with Romanian deadlifts or good mornings for 10-12 reps; the program lacks posterior chain work {2020-05 {"reps": "10-12"}} {{GClcGq2tIDU@1515}}
- [1v 2020 P] Hamstring curls mostly hit the lower hamstring and back of the knee, causing knee pain with poor form; RDLs hit the glute-hamstring tie-in and carry over to the deadlift, so he prefers RDLs {{jSUmn33lmlI@1132}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pick either seated or standing hamstring curls and push progression on it; he prefers seated {{eobG2o3Ww4M@186}}
- [1v 2020 P] Paused deadlifts, stiff-leg deadlifts and rack pulls are great but still do not train hamstring lengthening {{YtNUZjBxdtE@170}}
- [1v 2020 O] Hamstring curls beat leg extensions for injury prevention and development because they provide a stretch {{niD0UuwTUvg@505}}
- [1v 2021 P] Start hamstring day with a hip hinge, not leg curls, then use curls for the stretch {{qq459xqmq5A@692}}
- [1v 2023 O] The squat is only C tier for the posterior chain: minimal hip hinge means little hamstring stimulus for the fatigue {{9L90ktKGPLw@251}}

## [hamstrings] kettlebell / hyperextension / hamstr / rdls (4)
- [2v 2020-2022 P] If using kettlebells, do swings; RDLs are a safe hip hinge you should load heavily, which kettlebells rarely allow also: 2022-08: Hamstring builders: leg curls, hyperextensions, RDLs and kettlebell swings {{eobG2o3Ww4M@1060}} {{yoROLGGUPXU@1110}}
- [2v 2020-2022 P] The limiting factor of every hinge is the concentric; train the positive with heavy deadlifts and the negative with RDLs and hyperextensions {{6sFaB3rAY1A@2323}} {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@317}}
- [2v 2020 P] Good mornings are not a substitute for hyperextensions: good mornings are a squat accessory with a better stretch, hyperextensions a deadlift accessory that is healthier on the lower back with a better hamstring contraction also: 2020-11: Best hamstring lengthening lifts are Romanian deadlifts, back hyperextensions and good mornings {{MCUpClPnPkQ@1126}} {{YtNUZjBxdtE@441}}
- [1v 2023 P] Kettlebell swings are S tier; science shows more hamstring activation than kickbacks; go heavy for 6-8 reps, not CrossFit style {2023-04 {"reps": "6-8"}} {{9L90ktKGPLw@1383}}

## [hamstrings] rdl / month / once / reps (6)
- [2v 2023-2024 P] He deadlifts only about once a month, purely to keep his Romanian deadlift progressing; the RDL is the better posterior-chain lift for bodybuilding {2023-09 {"frequency": "1x/month", "reps": 3} | 2024-02 {"frequency": "1x/month"}} {{43F9unmSc_I@944}} {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@317}}
- [1v 2020 P] Rotating lifts should be close in nature: leg curls vs RDL is better than bench vs OHP but still mixes isolation and compound; RDL and stiff-leg deadlift is a much better rotation. {{hi-kNbZnXz0@625}}
- [1v 2020 A] Keep a natural 'joker' lift used about once a month; for him it's good mornings, which trash his hamstrings {2020-08 {"frequency": "~1x/month"}} {{RgGkGiaGqks@1197}}
- [1v 2021 P] Squat 3x4-6 then RDL 3x8 is a great pairing; he would widen the RDL to 3x8-12 {2021-01 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@700}}
- [1v 2021 P] Deadlift and Romanian deadlift do not work as a rotation: RDL at ~10 reps per set is not strength work, so they serve different purposes {{8mGHG5C-EPE@440}}
- [1v 2023 P] The RDL is his king of posterior-chain growth; so stimulating he only does it 2-3 times a month, for 6-12 reps {2023-09 {"frequency": "2-3x/month", "reps": "6-12"}} {{43F9unmSc_I@1007}}

## [hamstrings] rdls / hinge / snatch-grip / concentric (6)
- [2v 2023 OP] He regrets neglecting Romanian deadlifts; doing 80% of hinge volume with deadlifts and 20% with RDLs is wrong if hypertrophy is the goal {2023-02 {"volume": "80/20 deadlift/RDL criticised"} | 2023-04 {"volume": "80% RDL / 20% deadlift"}} {{ABTeMrXdgI4@126}} {{9L90ktKGPLw@691}}
- [1v 2020 P] Splitting hip hinge volume between RDLs and glute-ham raises, which may hit different parts of the hamstrings, is a valid alternative to four sets of RDLs {{4ORCb_i7MxY@371}}
- [1v 2020 P] Plate-loaded deadlift machines with side handles limit the hip hinge and hamstring stretch; do RDLs with a barbell {{TD9SB9Bt45o@420}}
- [1v 2022 P] RDLs 3-4x6-12 are the program's only heavy hinge and can go to failure; if you do not deadlift, do RDLs {2022-08 {"reps": "6-12", "sets": "3-4"}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@540}}
- [1v 2022 P] Bodybuilders should treat the deadlift as an accessory to the Romanian deadlift: RDLs give far better stimulus-to-fatigue with a stretch, but stall on the concentric, which the deadlift trains {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@256}}
- [1v 2023 P] Snatch-grip RDLs also stretch the traps and upper back {{d1eLAd5WvIA@2503}}

## [hamstrings] morning / stiff-leg / 6-10 / 8-12 (6)
- [1v 2020 P] Good mornings and stiff-leg deadlifts should always be done in higher rep ranges, like 6-10. {2020-03 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{Tw8qA93Gf58@629}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep good mornings in a 6-10 or 8-12 rep range; heavy 5-rep sets or 1RMs are useless and dangerous for hypertrophy. {2020-06 {"reps": "6-10 or 8-12"}} {{2hwj-NEebj8@316}}
- [1v 2020 P] Follow with a hip hinge such as Romanian or stiff-leg deadlift (or deficit deadlift) for 3-4 sets of 6-10 or 8-12 reps; avoid good mornings that day (redundant lumbar loading), regular deadlifts, block pulls, and very low reps. {2020-06 {"reps": "6-10 or 8-12", "sets": "3-4"}} {{o3RTXWAia4A@570}}
- [1v 2021 P] If conventional doesn't suit you, still train it as submaximal variations for higher reps such as Romanian or stiff-leg deadlifts instead of chasing a 3RM {{lG5SZbRdOjA@564}}
- [1v 2023 P] Good mornings 6-10 never hurt his back and thicken the hamstrings and upper back; hyperextensions 8-12 are a safe, deep-stretch alternative {2023-09 {"reps": "6-10 / 8-12"}} {{43F9unmSc_I@944}}
- [1v 2024 O] Bodybuilders do not need deadlifts; RDLs, stiff-leg deadlifts, good mornings or hyperextensions work better, and some people are not built for conventional pulls {{Ft039H8jzXU@1194}}

## [hamstrings] morn / non-squat / way / ssb (5)
- [1v 2020 O] The SSB is the only way he does good mornings; a straight-bar good morning is really a 'squat morning', not a true good morning. {{utLw7UeXCOo@312}}
- [1v 2020 O] The SSB is the best way to do good mornings; straight bars sit either dangerously high or too far back {{I7vk4Kk5neA@628}}
- [1v 2021 O] The good morning may be the best non-squat lift for carryover, but it gives no quad volume {{pvpf7Yuayco@305}}
- [1v 2022 O] Rules like never putting more than two plates on a good morning are arbitrary nonsense; it is a great lift for hamstrings, glutes, lower back and upper back {2022-09 {"load": "225 lb cap rejected"}} {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@504}}
- [1v 2023 O] Good morning is A tier: like an RDL with the load nearer the head, so more stretch; worse risk/reward than RDLs {{9L90ktKGPLw@2264}}

## [hamstrings] pair / same / follow / rdls (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Don't pair good mornings with back squats or other posterior-chain-heavy squats on the same day; front squats pair well with them {{xQQJUXf58ZE@740}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pairing 3x3-5 heavy deadlifts with RDLs for 8-12 on the same day is wasteful because it is the same movement {2020-11 {"reps": "3-5 / 8-12", "sets": 3}} {{mbtAKsbDz9I@745}}
- [1v 2021 P] Squats followed by RDLs is an excellent pairing for big legs {{cNUwR9TmVRs@439}}
- [1v 2021 P] Template hip hinge slot: 4x8-12 (manage load if good mornings follow squats) or a floor pull 3x6-10, never high-intensity deadlifts, paired with a vertical pull (AMRAP or 3x5 weighted) or a horizontal pull but no barbell rows {2021-09 {"reps": "6-12", "sets": "3-4"}} {{yvhW-zyhpsM@0}}

## [hamstrings] stimulus-to / ratio / fatigue / rdls (4)
- [1v 2022 O] Deadlifts have a poor stimulus-to-fatigue ratio only when overdone; if they stop you training legs for 5-6 days, switch to RDLs {2022-08 {"days": "5-6"}} {{yoROLGGUPXU@1590}}
- [1v 2022 P] Deadlifts for 5x12 are far too much; deadlifts belong at 3-5 reps, use RDLs for higher reps since their stimulus-to-fatigue ratio is better {2022-12 {"reps": "3-5"}} {{0pKnDucWcDc@1189}}
- [1v 2024 P] Leg curls have a better stimulus-to-fatigue ratio than RDLs, but RDLs develop the upper back, traps, glutes and lower back too; someone doing only leg curls ends up weaker and smaller {{jUMmu583w34@1314}}
- [1v 2024 P] The deadlift is poor for hypertrophy: no real eccentric, heavy loads that limit you to 3-5 reps, and fatigue that drags down the rest of the session. The RDL has a better stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {2024-09 {"reps": "3-5"}} {{okm6bZHKHI4@8896}}

## [hamstrings] intensity / 8-12 / high / unforgiv (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Back hyperextensions: 4 sets of 8-12, never for high intensity, except when used strictly as a deadlift accessory where 3 reps can apply {2020-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1196}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep high intensity for the deadlift and do unforgiving hamstring movements in lower intensity windows {{YtNUZjBxdtE@567}}
- [1v 2020 P] Replace the leg press with 6 sets of lying leg curls at 8-12 reps to balance the quads {2020-12 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 6}} {{hYFO2eKn-m4@840}}

## [hamstrings] glute-ham / rais / hyperextension / nordic (3)
- [1v 2021 P] With full body, split hinges and knee flexions across days; use low-impact work like glute-ham raises, hyperextensions and leg curls, which can be supersetted {{G7rVTseabFU@1280}}
- [1v 2022 P] The hamstring slot can be glute-ham raises, Nordic curls or machine leg curls {{SPicD3F686s@1248}}
- [1v 2024 P] To replace RDLs and deadlifts, use hyperextensions, glute-ham raises and good mornings {{ZLheQPgEfoA@5011}}

## [hamstrings] sumo / posterior-chain / romanian / main (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Use the sumo as your main strength pull from the floor, but do not use sumo variations as accessories the way powerlifters do; take most posterior-chain volume and tonnage from other lifts {{3nyuEtmqL-I@317}}
- [1v 2023 O] You would grow more from Romanian deadlifts than from sumo deadlifts {{x9GNS5HBZ_4@600}}
- [1v 2024 O] A loaded back extension can be the main posterior-chain builder for legs, glutes and back {{6p2x4kDDjzs@8529}}

## [hamstrings] glut / hamstring / deload / block (3)
- [1v 2023 P] Pulling from blocks deloads hamstrings, glutes and lower back {{-sf34ScWLK8@2590}}
- [1v 2023 O] Conventional and sumo deadlifts are B tier: no stretch for hamstrings and glutes, and too much lower-back fatigue for what they give {{9L90ktKGPLw@251}}
- [1v 2023 O] T-bar row done heavy with knee flexion and hip hinge is a B-tier posterior chain movement and leaves hamstrings and glutes very sore {{9L90ktKGPLw@377}}

## [lats] lats / lat / grip / row (78)
- [2v 2020-2023 OP] Add pullovers in step 3, ideally dumbbell or machine (not cable); they stretch the lats differently from any pull-up also: 2020-10: Pull-ups, ideally weighted, are all you need for lats and vertical pulling {{MIHPylz3f7Q@1650}} {{7uBT8vp0lNw@1261}}
- [1v 2020 P] Many cable machines seat you far from the pull point, so you pull in front of you and never reach the lat stretch you get hanging under a pull-up bar with the bar in line with the collarbones. {{J9Y9VZhnZ5M@130}}
- [1v 2020 O] With machines, a strong middle back takes over and you pull with it rather than the lats, given the machine's moment arm. {{J9Y9VZhnZ5M@66}}
- [1v 2020 O] He considers supinated-grip rows unsafe and is looking for an overhand Yates row angle that still hits the lats. {{J9Y9VZhnZ5M@447}}
- [1v 2020 A] He is too weak to do weighted chin-ups himself but thinks they are amazing. {{fSOiGuUPqBc@1132}}
- [1v 2020 A] Vary pull-up grips; it's underrated. He feels wide grip more in his lats and close grip more in his arms, so he does mostly wide since his arms are already big. {{87CMjYjX2jY@1189}}
- [1v 2020 P] Nucleus overload is boring, so rotate variations (band, dumbbell, new angles) to bring back the pump; for lats he alternates pullovers and rows {{4rjywMLXt98@1201}}
- [1v 2020 O] People wrongly treat calisthenics as either only for novices or the only thing advanced lifters need; they should be applied properly, e.g. for lats {{GClcGq2tIDU@1892}}
- [1v 2020 O] Weighted pull-ups are a very good home addition to gym training. {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@62}}
- [1v 2020 P] After a deadlift day, vertical pulls like pull-ups suit the next day: they hit the lats and decompress the spine and sacrum {{NiV6lE0XUH8@253}}
- [1v 2020 O] You can never do too many pull-ups if you protect your tendons; they are very safe, decompress the spine and pair well with overhead press, which loads the lumbar spine {{PFQ30yaL1t0@250}}
- [1v 2020 P] Yates rows at 8-12 are perfect: heavy enough but controllable to stretch the lats {2020-07 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@686}}
- [1v 2020 O] Rather than doubling the top set of barbell rows he would have preferred vertical pulls in the program {{YcHkXwsIOI8@689}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pullovers are excellent but should not be trained at high intensity; the weak link is the shoulders and elbows, not the lats, so multiply sets instead {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1038}}
- [1v 2020 P] On a vertical press day, pair a vertical pull such as weighted pull-ups for 3 sets of 4-7 instead of 5x10 generic lat work {2020-11 {"reps": "4-7", "sets": 3}} {{hT4zgNcMOBY@479}}
- [1v 2020 P] Superset OHP with bodyweight AMRAP pull-ups to maintain pull-up fitness; rows already cover upper back intensity {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@648}}
- [1v 2021 O] Front levers are not comparable to pullovers: the lats stabilize rather than move, without the stretch {{-anA4CV2SVU@1952}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pull-ups grow the lats like nothing else with proper form; switching grips lets you do them more often, and accumulating volume on them is key {{1MOLCv6ojgw@235}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pull-ups: bodyweight 3 sets AMRAP daily or 5 sets AMRAP every two days; weighted 3x3 every 3 days {2021-03 {"reps": "AMRAP or 3", "sets": "3-5"} | 2021-03 {"reps": "8-12 or 4-8", "sets": 4}} {{1MOLCv6ojgw@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] The pull-up is the best mass builder for the upper back, especially the lats; since doing them religiously the difference has been night and day {{qDH088UUiMA@204}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pull-ups barely tax the cardiovascular system, so superset them with forearm, ab or other small-muscle work {{qDH088UUiMA@239}}
- [1v 2021 P] Weighted chins at 2x6-8 are a good addition, and loading only after 6-8 clean bodyweight chins is the right rule {2021-05 {"reps": "6-8", "sets": 2}} {{HH7kCVkRyJc@438}}
- [1v 2021 O] Jeff's programs obsess over lat isolation; three lat exercises back to back is too much, spread them out {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@1076}}
- [1v 2021 P] Dumbbell rows are the best row variation: no lower back tax, and the arc path lets the lat fully stretch {{Q5y4wM8zcWY@1701}}
- [1v 2021 O] Bodyweight chin-ups should not look agonising after two reps; visible suffering signals very low pain tolerance {{eaypnfdz3J0@631}}
- [1v 2021 P] Pull-ups are one of the most essential movement patterns for a big, aesthetic natural back {{C4CnYbHCCGo@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Giving yourself grip variety keeps pull-ups exciting if you get bored of them {{C4CnYbHCCGo@756}}
- [1v 2022 P] Every program includes weighted chin-ups because they apply to every combat sport; do not skip them {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@3388}}
- [1v 2022 O] Unilateral back work doubles training time; better to learn to pull with the back bilaterally, and pull-ups beat unilateral lat moves {{wLx5BRx8AaU@1913}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rather than several lat exercises for a pump, do one heavy lat movement taken to exhaustion for more growth {{kKX0AUrXzIw@2073}}
- [1v 2022 P] For lat stretch use weighted pull-ups with proper form, seal rows or dumbbell rows {{QfHfxLYRu88@2135}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pull-ups are among the least functional lifts: almost nobody uses a vertical pull in daily life, and firefighters need about one rep to climb in a window {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@379}}
- [1v 2022 O] Weighted pull-ups with a belt and plates have no real-life equivalent, so by functional logic adding load makes the lift non-functional {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@565}}
- [1v 2022 O] The pull-up is the best vertical pull in existence; start unloaded then add weight {{Mt7cGMJZjkw@1321}}
- [1v 2022 O] For certain lat development the chin-up is superior to the pronated pull-up {{Mt7cGMJZjkw@500}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pull-ups are essential for a complete back as a natural lifter; NH went from poor lats to decent lats with pull-ups only {{EGCKBEWh8ng@0}}
- [1v 2022 O] Nearly all natural bodybuilders with big lats and upper backs built them with pull-ups and vertical pulls, from the bronze era to today {{EGCKBEWh8ng@999}}
- [1v 2022 P] Cable rows are fine to finish the lats, not as the main stimulus; naturals need heavy, more destructive movements {{EGCKBEWh8ng@835}}
- [1v 2022 O] Almost anyone who did weighted pull-ups would see massive lat growth {{EGCKBEWh8ng@1340}}
- [1v 2022 P] Dead hangs build the mobility for a full bottom position and decompress the lower back {{yoROLGGUPXU@2698}}
- [1v 2022 P] Wide-grip pull-ups should use a false grip or fat grip so you palm the bar; with the week's back volume they need no added weight {2022-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{g-pqgciOSgE@1259}}
- [1v 2022 O] The wide pronated lat pulldown is only decent: sitting on a seat removes the full bodyweight hang and stretch of a pull-up, and most machines make you pull in front instead of straight down; use it as a complement or until you can do pull-ups {{Cbq-vxVba4k@442}}
- [1v 2022 O] A wide supinated pulldown beats the pronated version for lats because it is easier to pull low to the sternum with the elbows tucked; a pronated grip flares the elbows and shifts work to the upper back {{Cbq-vxVba4k@505}}
- [1v 2022 O] The front lever is mediocre for hypertrophy: huge technical demand for little lat stimulus; calisthenics athletes built their lats with pull-ups, not levers {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2700}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Meadows row is excellent: single arm, easy to find your angle and follow the arc that benefits the lats; it falls short of top tier because the staggered stance still loads the lower back, glutes and hamstrings {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2136}}
- [1v 2022 P] Grip flavours: chin-ups bias the lower lat and work the core; pronated pull-ups add more upper and mid back; neutral grip allows the best stretch with tucked elbows; wide grip is the best lower-lat builder among pull-ups {{Cbq-vxVba4k@378}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Kroc row is slightly inferior to the dumbbell row: it is grip-heavy, invites explosive reps without negative control, and the more open elbow recruits more upper back; he uses it slowly as a rotation and likes its pendulum swing at the bottom {{Cbq-vxVba4k@882}}
- [1v 2022 O] The sternum (Gironda) pull-up is the best of the best because it mixes vertical and horizontal pulling, but you need about 10 chest-to-bar pull-ups before attempting it; he can barely manage it himself {2022-11 {"reps": 10}} {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1757}}
- [1v 2022 O] The body row between parallel dip bars is top tier and underrated: big stretch, tucked elbows, a pendulum motion, neutral grip that forces lat stretch, and it is easy to load with plates or a backpack {{Cbq-vxVba4k@3013}}
- [1v 2022 O] The close-grip neutral lat pulldown is top tier: elbows tuck easily, full overhead stretch, comfortable on the shoulders, and the close grip lets you stay tight and move more weight with better form {{Cbq-vxVba4k@567}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Yates row is excellent for lower lat and mid back: supinated grip, very vertical torso, row to the belly button. The supinated grip carries biceps-tear risk; Dorian Yates tore his biceps on it {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1194}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pronated ring rows are only decent because the grip biases the upper back; supinated ring rows are excellent for the lats {{Cbq-vxVba4k@630}}
- [1v 2022 O] The EZ-bar incline chest-supported row is top tier and better than the seal row: the bench height guarantees a full bottom stretch and the EZ bar allows a comfortable closer grip {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2574}}
- [1v 2022 O] Chest-supported dumbbell rows on an incline bench are excellent, just as good as a seal row if your gym lacks one {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2763}}
- [1v 2022 O] Behind-the-neck pulldowns and pull-ups are only decent: easy to impinge the shoulder, you end up pulling in front, and they hit the upper back and rhomboids more than the lats {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2951}}
- [1v 2022 O] The one-arm pull-up is mediocre: nobody controls the negative, so there is no stretch; it is a show-off skill built by people who already have strong backs {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2763}}
- [1v 2022 O] Bodyweight ring rows tend to fail for reasons outside the back, such as grip giving out in heavier lifters; on a neutral pulldown the grip is a non-factor {{Cbq-vxVba4k@757}}
- [1v 2022 P] The diagonal (iliac) pulldown is top tier, but only if you go heavy for 8-10 hard reps to failure with progressive overload, not baby weights for 15 reps {2022-11 {"reps": "8-10"}} {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2825}}
- [1v 2022 P] Machine rows are excellent and as good as dumbbell rows; if the machine blocks your stretch, go one arm at a time with your body shifted to the side {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1695}}
- [1v 2022 O] The dumbbell row is top tier and underrated just because it is old news; you can build a monster back with it {{Cbq-vxVba4k@757}}
- [1v 2022 O] The muscle-up is only decent for back hypertrophy because it is half a pull-up and too technical {{Cbq-vxVba4k@3076}}
- [1v 2023 P] Weighted chin-ups 3-5x4-10: never under 4 reps; any added weight that challenges you counts {2023-01 {"reps": "4-10", "sets": "3-5"}} {{_P0-kYyVMn4@757}}
- [1v 2023 P] Prioritize weighted pull-ups over the RDL line; if you skip something skip the second line {{upmJaBt1WHk@694}}
- [1v 2023 P] Cupped fat-grip neutral pull-ups, with hands on top rather than wrapped, are his favourite pull-up for forearms and back; for advanced lifters with strong wrists {2023-04 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{kQMrIszr_Bw@1197}}
- [1v 2023 A] His previous novice program had too many pull-ups; now they are fewer, weighted when possible, not max reps {{gpFdh0eBMGc@500}}
- [1v 2023 P] An arm day can and should include some upper-back work; the pullover hits the triceps long head, lats and upper back {{awwJ5sbyz6A@761}}
- [1v 2023 P] Grip changes the elbow angle and therefore how the back is hit: neutral-grip dumbbell rows favour the lats, pronated croc rows the traps {{B5eAFxfON3A@569}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pull-ups are S-tier for the upper back, especially lats {{7uBT8vp0lNw@1071}}
- [1v 2023 P] The belt squat machine row hits the lower lats better than any row he's tried, with no lower-back involvement; 6-10 reps with a pause in the stretch {2023-11 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{6YC_gmRNhPo@313}}
- [1v 2024 A] He once relied on deadlifts for his back; for lats do pull-ups and vertical pulls {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@254}}
- [1v 2024 P] The pullover's active lat range is small (lats engage as arms go behind the head and peak at the stretch) but powerful enough to justify the lift {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@2019}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lesson three: do not dismiss calisthenics; the pec and lat stretch from bodyweight dips and pull-ups is hard to match {{tbY5VMSmlP8@955}}
- [1v 2024 O] Pull-ups are an amazing hypertrophy movement, not a bad one, when done properly; they largely explain Baki's back {{2LoP6-KdEx4@376}}
- [1v 2024 O] The iliac pulldown should be taken to failure and done hard; it is hated because of the light, slow, high-rep way it was promoted, not because of the movement {{jUMmu583w34@1580}}
- [1v 2024 O] The iliac pulldown is a noob trap: it is easy and needs no technical adaptation, so beginners choose it over harder, more productive lifts like dumbbell rows and pull-ups {{jUMmu583w34@400}}
- [1v 2024 P] Pull-ups give more overall back and arm stimulus but stress the elbow, biceps and shoulders more; the iliac pulldown targets lats with little wear {{jUMmu583w34@1390}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dumbbell pullovers on the floor limit the stretch, and on a bed the shoulder blades sink which is unsafe {{bRKQIOYo2u4@4013}}
- [1v 2024 O] He considers the pullover a lats or triceps lift, not a chest lift; its resistance profile is debated {{6yW3GSskl8g@507}}

## [lats] pulldown / lat / vertical / use (30)
- [2v 2020-2023 O] Close-grip neutral lat pulldowns are inferior to pull-ups; the pull-up stretch is unmatched also: 2020-02: Dumbbell pullovers give unmatched lat isolation and stretch. {{3oZD6Hk525A@162}} {{_P0-kYyVMn4@1323}}
- [2v 2020-2021 OP] Prefer pull-ups, then weighted pull-ups, over lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine but should never be the main strength lift or open the workout also: 2020-08: He prefers chin-ups or pull-ups to lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine only on a machine where the bar comes straight down over your head {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@430}}
- [2v 2020 OP] Replace lat pulldowns with chin-ups for better results; pulldowns are inferior for hypertrophy, use light ones only as an accessory also: 2020-12: Pull-ups are always better than lat pulldowns; use chin-ups to also train the biceps {{eobG2o3Ww4M@500}} {{DQo0DGn_wI4@1000}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pull-ups and chin-ups aren't just a vertical pull, they are THE vertical pull; pulldowns are not an equivalent substitute. {{OATtf3kPtdI@1001}}
- [1v 2020 A] He uses no machines or pulldowns for lats. {{J9Y9VZhnZ5M@383}}
- [1v 2020 O] On heavy pulldowns the thigh pad holds you down so the spine gets stretched, and many machines put the handle in front, turning it into a rear-delt pull rather than a lat stretch. {{eFhwWirR70o@379}}
- [1v 2020 O] Weighted pull-ups beat lat pulldowns: you move your body through space, the stretch isn't limited by machine hinges, and machine resistance curves are inconsistent. {{eFhwWirR70o@379}}
- [1v 2020 P] He advises replacing lat pulldowns with pull-ups, especially for martial artists, using variations such as fat grips, towel grips, slow negatives or explosive reps for athletic carryover; keep pulldowns if you like their different load and stretch. {{rucwL-SIefg@625}}
- [1v 2020 P] Replace lat pulldowns with pull-ups or weighted pull-ups: better for hypertrophy, athleticism and injury prevention {{GClcGq2tIDU@879}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do pull-ups instead of lat pulldowns; if you can pulldown a lot but can't match the reps with bodyweight pull-ups, there's an imbalance. {{0yIieHQjEiA@567}}
- [1v 2020 O] Instead of lat pulldowns, do pull-ups: more athletic, more lat stretch, less spinal loading, and faster progression; use the machine only if you like the pump. {{hi-kNbZnXz0@379}}
- [1v 2020 O] NH dislikes lat pulldowns; pull-ups are better for hypertrophy, especially weighted {{TD9SB9Bt45o@160}}
- [1v 2020 O] Straight-bar pulldowns are the most expendable exercise; just do more pull-ups {{dHXPhjaWBWk@1068}}
- [1v 2021 P] Always prefer pull-ups over pulldowns, for athleticism and hypertrophy; vertical pulls tolerate high volume, so 3-5 sets is fine {2021-01 {"sets": "3-5"}} {{cNUwR9TmVRs@188}}
- [1v 2021 P] A lat pulldown should not be the strength lift; start pull day with a hinge, then a vertical pull or row {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1347}}
- [1v 2021 P] Use lat pulldowns only if you cannot do pull-ups yet or as a supplementary variation to change torso angle; never open a back day with pulldowns, they are not intense enough {{qDH088UUiMA@278}}
- [1v 2022 P] Use one isolation movement per muscle for NO, or two at most if experienced and closely related (e.g. lat pulldown and straight-arm pulldown or pullover) {{LNggSj0WBd8@1762}}
- [1v 2022 P] Include at least one heavy vertical pull; use lat pulldowns if you can't do weighted pull-ups {{SPicD3F686s@878}}
- [1v 2022 O] Opening a back day with 4x20 lat pulldowns is aerobics with weights; machine/cable isolation belongs at the end after heavy compounds {2022-06 {"reps": 20, "sets": 4}} {{uYRIWAKlA0c@439}}
- [1v 2022 P] Enhanced bodybuilders use pulldowns because they are too heavy for pull-ups; if you can, do pull-ups {{gizylGr0ebo@1403}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rotate pulldown grips (chin-up, neutral) and favour pullovers to stretch the ribcage {{gizylGr0ebo@1600}}
- [1v 2022 P] Lat pulldowns in this program use pronated or neutral grip, not supinated {2022-10 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{CSwbuwl33eA@560}}
- [1v 2022 O] The standing cable pulldown is Peewee tier: it becomes a momentum-driven unbalanced row; sit down and do a real pulldown {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2322}}
- [1v 2023 P] If you do two similar back exercises in a day (pull-ups and pulldowns), separate them rather than doing them back to back {{BiVfpZEchws@251}}
- [1v 2023 O] The narrow neutral-grip pulldown is the best pulldown for the back {2023-04 {"reps": "8-12"}} {{aJ1e41y8Na4@693}}
- [1v 2023 P] For carryover pick a 'soft' variation close to the main lift (pull-up -> lat pulldown); bodybuilders can also pick 'hard' variations for variety since specificity matters less {{7uBT8vp0lNw@635}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pull-ups are preferable as the vertical pull, but a lat pulldown is fine if you cannot do them yet; aim to get to pull-ups for later weighted work {{7uBT8vp0lNw@194}}
- [1v 2023 P] A second vertical pull alongside weighted pull-ups can be bodyweight chest-to-bar pull-ups, a new grip, or lat pulldowns 8-12 to rest tendons {2023-09 {"reps": "8-12"}} {{7uBT8vp0lNw@1071}}
- [1v 2024 P] The lat pulldown is the best pull-up substitute and is worth rotating in even if you can do pull-ups {{bRKQIOYo2u4@1729}}
- [1v 2024 P] Never open a day with an isolation movement, and never put two lat movements back to back {{6p2x4kDDjzs@3770}}

## [lats] pullover / best / cable / lats (16)
- [2v 2021-2023 O] Pullovers are the best lat exercise after pull-ups and help people who can't feel their back on pulls also: 2023-02: Cable pullovers are fine if you feel them better, though dumbbell pullovers are the best {{1MOLCv6ojgw@290}} {{K2h1QhDo4qU@670}}
- [1v 2020 A] Pronated pull-ups on the Smith frame feel terrible on his biceps and forearms; only supinated chin-ups feel great there. {{TrU2m-4yZT0@566}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pullovers can be done with cables, bands, dumbbells or an EZ bar; NH prefers dumbbells. {{M9QpHgTUago@63}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pullovers are godlike for the lats {{NiV6lE0XUH8@1047}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pullovers are a very important lat exercise; with pull-ups they are the two best lat exercises and high-volume pullovers transformed his lats {{VXwYO2qG9ZY@128}}
- [1v 2021 P] Nearly everyone can do pullovers, with many variations: dumbbell, EZ bar, barbell, supinated, incline, cable {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@127}}
- [1v 2021 O] The pullover is one of the best upper-body movements and the best complement to the bench press {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@127}}
- [1v 2022 P] If dumbbell pullovers hit the triceps more than the lats, try barbell or EZ-bar pullovers with different grips {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2821}}
- [1v 2022 O] The idea that muscles need a week or two of inactivity to resensitize is nonsense; he resensitizes via rotations such as switching dumbbell pullovers to cable pullovers or changing the angle {2022-02 {"weeks": "1-2"}} {{BJXFeQWGUuY@2016}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pullovers are the most underrated exercise; 4x12 is the right way to do them {2022-06 {"reps": 12, "sets": 4}} {{uYRIWAKlA0c@2073}}
- [1v 2022 P] Cable pullovers give a tremendous stretch but are a nightmare to set up, so prefer dumbbell pullovers if you can do them {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2510}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pullovers (dumbbell, machine, or cable on a bench with a low pulley, even on a decline) are his favourite lift and pair best with push days {{4t72r8Q0vXQ@1255}}
- [1v 2024 O] Contrary to Carter, the pullover is a lat exercise; Steve Reeves and many others built big lats with it {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@1830}}
- [1v 2024 O] Machine pullovers are easier to set up than dumbbell pullovers, may recruit the abs more and suit rest-pause better, and are the closest substitute; the cable version is far removed {{okm6bZHKHI4@1094}}
- [1v 2024 P] Pullovers should be in every program; they fit any push or upper body day {{okm6bZHKHI4@1157}}
- [1v 2024 P] Dumbbell pullovers supplement but cannot replace pull-ups or pulldowns {{c7hl9u3h_s0@5349}}

## [lats] lats / pullover / expand / rib (5)
- [2v 2020-2022 AP] He does dumbbell pullovers every day or two at 5-12 reps with 60 lb to build lats and expand the rib cage {2020-12 {"frequency": "daily/every 2 days", "load": "60 lb", "reps": "5-12"} | 2022-01 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{SbPaZ5giUNs@746}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2688}}
- [1v 2021 O] Pullovers made the biggest difference to his physique: they build the lats and triceps long head and expand the ribcage, making a narrow lifter look wider and thicker {{Ug_FoOIpwxY@66}}
- [1v 2022 P] Deadlifts work the lats only a little; you will never have developed lats from deadlifts alone {{dVssxcEh1UQ@251}}
- [1v 2022 A] The same exercise can serve as both a hard set and NO with different parameters: he does heavy pullovers with 90-95 lb for 12 and NO pullovers with 60 lb for 7-10 reps far from failure {2022-03 {"load": "90-95 lb hard / 60 lb NO", "reps": "12 / 7-10"}} {{LNggSj0WBd8@1262}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pullovers are top tier: unmatched lat stretch and rib cage expansion. They transformed his physique and gave him lat flare he never had, visible even relaxed {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1132}}

## [lats] rep / rang / 8-10 / unweight (4)
- [2v 2020 OP] Weighted chin-ups work well in low rep ranges while unweighted chin-ups suit higher reps, so rows and chins rep ranges should be swapped in this program. {{fSOiGuUPqBc@312}} {{87CMjYjX2jY@251}}
- [1v 2020 P] Keep weighted pull-ups in low rep ranges; 3x8 is good as it sandbags a lift where form breakdown is harmful and shifts the target muscles. {2020-06 {"reps": 8, "sets": 3}} {{ZKgM-tX_ODU@735}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you cannot do weighted pull-ups, rep ranges matter little: do them unweighted for as many reps as possible, ideally keeping reps in the tank {{cGzlMRCxvxo@691}}
- [1v 2020 P] 8-10 is a good rep range for pull-ups {2020-10 {"reps": "8-10", "sets": 3}} {{dHXPhjaWBWk@126}}

## [lats] chin-up / lat / arm-dominant / bicep (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Heavy barbell rows before weighted chin-ups pre-fatigue the lats, biceps and forearms and sandbag the chin-ups {{Lj0m82JUtkM@480}}
- [1v 2022 P] The lat is the main muscle in chin-ups even when done arm-dominant, so chin-ups alone will not build big biceps {{Mt7cGMJZjkw@500}}
- [1v 2022 A] He has almost stopped doing normal chin-ups in favor of cupped chin-ups {{Mt7cGMJZjkw@750}}
- [1v 2024 O] Chin-ups feel like more front lat for him; wide pull-ups bring in a lot of traps {{ZLheQPgEfoA@5701}}

## [lats] lats / v-taper / lat / close (4)
- [1v 2022 O] The deadlift is only mediocre for the lats: the lat just isometrically holds the bar close, so it builds a thick 'fridge back' with no V-taper {{Cbq-vxVba4k@190}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Jefferson deadlift is also mediocre for lats; staggering the stance and loading one lat more is still not enough lat work {{Cbq-vxVba4k@253}}
- [1v 2023 O] Super-wide pull-ups are tremendous for lats and V-taper {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@378}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lats don't grow much from deadlifts just because they keep the bar close, any more than biceps grow from bench pressing {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@190}}

## [lats] decompression / spinal / purely / lat (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Pull-ups are better even purely for hypertrophy because of the lat stretch and spinal decompression. {{0yIieHQjEiA@629}}
- [1v 2020 P] Naturals should do pull-ups over pulldowns: better for hypertrophy, spinal decompression and shoulder health; pros avoid them because of their poor strength-to-size ratio {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1101}}
- [1v 2022 O] Dead hangs are Peewee tier for lats because the lats are disengaged; people who did weighted hangs reported zero lat growth. They are good for forearms and spinal decompression {{Cbq-vxVba4k@2637}}

## [lats] vertical / supersett / lack / add (3)
- [1v 2020 P] The program lacks vertical pulling: add pull-ups at the end, supersetted with any non-strength work. {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@1568}}
- [1v 2020 P] Add chin-ups to a chest and arms day, supersetted with incline bench, for more vertical pulling which you can never do too much of {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@305}}
- [1v 2020 O] It lacks a stretch-focused hip hinge for the hamstrings and any vertical pull; pull-ups are key for building lats {{YcHkXwsIOI8@1220}}

## [lats] bodyweight / programm / 3x3 / 20 (3)
- [1v 2020 P] More bodyweight vertical pulls should be programmed {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1390}}
- [1v 2020 P] Weighted pull-ups suit 3x3 strength work; bodyweight pull-ups should just be done for as many reps as possible {2020-12 {"reps": 3, "sets": 3}} {{hYFO2eKn-m4@210}}
- [1v 2024 P] Once you can do 20+ bodyweight pull-ups, move to weighted pull-ups; weighted calisthenics is the way {2024-12 {"reps": "21"}} {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@6664}}

## [lats] finisher / pump / one-arm / cable (3)
- [1v 2021 A] He used the one-arm cable row only as a pump finisher on deadlift days for the horizontal stretch; pull-ups and rows build lats better {{-anA4CV2SVU@1638}}
- [1v 2022 O] The one-arm seated cable row is excellent and better than the two-arm version: you can wrap around for a full lat stretch and re-tuck for a better contraction without hitting yourself {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1883}}
- [1v 2022 O] The straight-arm pulldown is only decent: the cable takes away the loaded stretch and its real benefit is the squeeze, so use it as a pump finisher at the end of a workout {{Cbq-vxVba4k@1069}}

## [lats] beat / 4-8 / 6-10 / prioritise (3)
- [1v 2021 P] In the hierarchy of vertical pulls, pull-ups beat machines and weighted pull-ups beat pull-ups; prioritise them {{8mGHG5C-EPE@1125}}
- [1v 2023 P] Lifting beats swimming for aesthetics on time-efficiency; even swimmers lift for mass, and 10 reps of weighted pull-ups beat 100 backstrokes {2023-10 {"reps": 10}} {{oytzIPcqih4@758}}
- [1v 2024 P] For pull-ups, 4-8 reps beats 6-10 {2024-09 {"reps": "4-8"}} {{bRKQIOYo2u4@10829}}

## [lats] pullover / physique / made / help (3)
- [1v 2021 A] The pullover changed his physique more than anything: it made him look wider, flared his lats and also helps arms and chest {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@64}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pullovers are so good he expects to put them in every program: they build lats, chest and long-head triceps and help thoracic mobility and shoulder health {{IYobXlRVLd0@746}}
- [1v 2022 A] Pullovers made the biggest change to his physique; part of the lat gain is structural {{QfHfxLYRu88@1760}}

## [lats] athlet / calisthenic / seen / climber (3)
- [1v 2022 O] Claiming pull-ups build rear delts and traps more than lats is nonsense; calisthenics athletes are not known for huge upper traps {{EGCKBEWh8ng@184}}
- [1v 2022 D] Calisthenics athletes and climbers have some of the best backs of any athletes because they constantly do pull-up motions {{EGCKBEWh8ng@1030}}
- [1v 2022 O] Pull-ups of every grip are top tier for lats; the best backs he has seen belong to people who do pull-ups, such as calisthenics athletes {{Cbq-vxVba4k@316}}

## [neck] neck / bridg / rowing / extension (3)
- [2v 2020-2023 OP] Neck curls instead of neck bridges build the neck without the risk {{EnmFDF2DxAo@530}} {{rFfLUloDHeg@844}}
- [1v 2020 P] Plan: keep deadlifting and rowing but add neck isolation work, neck extensions with plates. {{PfqgZ3_2EcM@128}}
- [1v 2024 P] Bent-over neck harness extension while holding a bar with arms open makes it a compound upper-back and neck movement that hits mid/lower traps {{qvWxIzhT1QU@885}}

## [neck] driven / head / resist / safer (1)
- [1v 2023 P] Band neck extensions driven by a hip hinge with the head resisting feel more natural and safer than lying with a plate on the head {{6KA80BQ9W3k@632}}

## [quads] quad / leg / legs / front (126)
- [2v 2020-2023 O] Jump squats after heavy lower work train you to stay explosive while tired, which fighting demands {2020-06 {"reps": 12, "sets": 3}} {{PFQ30yaL1t0@566}} {{_IBSNO56zuE@1437}}
- [2v 2020 O] Heavy back squats followed by RDLs is a match made in heaven; a light squat day plus leg press wastes time {{niD0UuwTUvg@442}} {{DQo0DGn_wI4@840}}
- [1v 2020 P] His leg training philosophy is simple: squats for the quads, and a wide variety of exercises for the hamstrings. {{m6zOEA6DjCg@5}}
- [1v 2020 O] The SSB is not an easier squat but a different movement: its camber shifts the load so you cannot squat your straight-bar weight with it. {{utLw7UeXCOo@249}}
- [1v 2020 A?] He uses the SSB for block squats, good mornings and movements where you wrap your arms tightly around the padded bar and yank on it. {{utLw7UeXCOo@124}}
- [1v 2020 P] Take each main lift and use its variations: for the squat, back squat plus paused back squat, front squat and other variants, rotating them to reach your goals. {{IR-YS-TGGx0@62}}
- [1v 2020 O] Sissy squats on the Smith machine are fine, but these lifts aren't for beginners anyway. {{TrU2m-4yZT0@505}}
- [1v 2020 P] He does pause squats ass-to-grass for 4-8 reps at about 80% of his max (around 300 lbs), supersetted with grippers, strength restoration and towel grip. {2020-02 {"load": "~300 lbs", "percent": 80, "reps": "4-8"}} {{j5PP3gx7DC4@191}}
- [1v 2020 P] Front squats are his favorite squat variation even though they don't suit his build; he does 3 sets of 4-5 relatively heavy reps. {2020-02 {"reps": "4-5", "sets": 3}} {{j5PP3gx7DC4@127}}
- [1v 2020 O] Deep squats are the best leg hypertrophy exercise for the vast majority of people, though not every individual. {{a02c10s3Pjk@130}}
- [1v 2020 P] Front squats are mostly beneficial for the back squat: they force you upright, create core rigidity, teach leg drive and keep the thoracic spine from rounding. {{_iaU9_rM1QQ@249}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats carry over to the deadlift because you push from roughly hip-height of the pull, but quad and glute development won't be maximised; full ROM squats are better, and box-only squatters are surprised how little carries to free squats. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@443}}
- [1v 2020 P] 'Squat' should mean a knee-flexion pattern; let lifters choose the variation (front, SSB, etc.) so they learn they needn't follow orthodox rules. {{4aThavRpYLw@502}}
- [1v 2020 A] He never does lunges: even with proper form they inflame his knees. If squats hurt you, play with stance and foot position. {{awroGsPwNPU@382}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg extensions are a subpar lift; do more squats instead, and if the lower back limits you add hyperextensions or more hamstring work {{GClcGq2tIDU@1450}}
- [1v 2020 P] Rotate lunges and frog squats week to week rather than doing both after squats and stiff-leg deadlifts {2020-06 {"sets": 4}} {{NiV6lE0XUH8@860}}
- [1v 2020 O] Dumbbell lunges suit fighters by mimicking fight positions, and are safer heavy than barbell lunges because you can drop the dumbbells {2020-06 {"reps": 5, "sets": "3-5"}} {{PFQ30yaL1t0@541}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pause squats done alone every 10 days may progress, but not optimally; paired with heavy regular squats they feed each other through similar patterns with different difficulty curves, loads and volumes. {2020-07 {"frequency": "every 10 days"}} {{8I6IPBr2_z0@190}}
- [1v 2020 P] There are no expiration dates on lifts; you can squat for 10 years and still progress {2020-07 {"years": 10}} {{Zkhc-j-6ptU@360}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats are not the best for hypertrophy: people rock back into a good morning and take super wide stances; they are acceptable if posterior chain is the priority {{du7JYCA2pa4@682}}
- [1v 2020 O] Smolov is more extreme than Squat Every Day or Widowmakers, which are more reasonable routes to bigger quads {{N8Dp4inzHog@1074}}
- [1v 2020 P] Roofers may need less leg training, but strength work still helps by covering movement patterns the job misses {{mXLMkXmIeW8@313}}
- [1v 2020 O] Refusing to squat means small legs for life {{Of7VDakRks4@818}}
- [1v 2020 A] 4x10 front squats are acceptable since you can reset the rack between sets; he always does 5 reps on front squats {2020-09 {"reps": 10, "sets": 4}} {{i4YmJhlw7cs@812}}
- [1v 2020 O] The more specific the accessory the better; the best squat accessory is the pause squat, which builds strength in the hole and confidence {{9M7QVlvFdQY@1130}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg days made of leg extensions without squats and deadlifts will not build big natural legs {{A4KNy_uMCRM@655}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg press on a deadlift day is a valid knee-flexion addition {{oLm1CDuY9zg@1140}}
- [1v 2020 O] Deficit deadlifts use more quad drive and are a good lift {{oLm1CDuY9zg@900}}
- [1v 2020 O] Legs are simple to program: squats and deadlift variations are all you need; use machines only if you can't back squat {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@1318}}
- [1v 2020 P] Replace box squats with paused squats for 7-10 reps, then deadlift one set of five, slowly ramping up {2020-10 {"reps": "7-10; 1x5"}} {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1260}}
- [1v 2020 O] Kettlebell lunges are limited by the heaviest kettlebells available and turn into a grip exercise {{eobG2o3Ww4M@125}}
- [1v 2020 P] Variation back-offs should stay as close in specificity as possible; a front squat after back squats is a new exercise needing a new warm-up, not a back-off {{Rz4uzjM068M@253}}
- [1v 2020 O] Band good mornings tend to be harder than band squats; big quads are very hard to build with bodyweight {{dfnn-YdXerg@400}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg minimalism works as long as you squat and deadlift with smart rep ranges and never get injured; legs will keep growing {{Lj0m82JUtkM@970}}
- [1v 2020 P] The pause squat is meant as a harder variation to help the back squat progress; if it is your only squat you lack a main mover and will plateau {{6sFaB3rAY1A@127}}
- [1v 2020 P] Rotating three lunge variations is fine because they're far enough from the squat; you want stimulus there, not carryover {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@838}}
- [1v 2020 P] A hip hinge followed by a knee-flexion movement (deadlift then walking lunges) is how you get big legs {{DQo0DGn_wI4@1210}}
- [1v 2020 P] Swap heavy back squats every other session for a variation such as front squat, belt squat or good morning to keep stress and tonnage while getting distance from the lift {{7ZS-mGePFXw@249}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't program strength squats, back-off squats, front squats and paused squats in one day; repeating one pattern is worse than a bro split {{ehxjCB_MdKc@877}}
- [1v 2021 P] Lower days need fewer exercises than upper days because the tonnage is so high; legs do not need isolation, just squat {{cNUwR9TmVRs@501}}
- [1v 2021 O] Every leg day he reviews is a squat, a hinge and machines, and that is fine; the squat and the hinge are 90 percent of the gains {{pey1Ni6HPlY@781}}
- [1v 2021 P] Keep at least one squat or deadlift variation (knee flexion or hip hinge) as a permanent staple done for high reps, 6-12, never below 6 {2021-02 {"reps": "6-12"}} {{BBqiphYfqMA@400}}
- [1v 2021 P] Paused squats are NH's favorite quad variation: safe when done upright, no stretch reflex, great quad tension and carryover to the squat {{pvpf7Yuayco@229}}
- [1v 2021 O] Front squats are great but too hard to master; there's no in-between, and unmastered they're useless for hypertrophy {{pvpf7Yuayco@215}}
- [1v 2021 P] Open a full body day with the back squat because it needs the most thoracic extension and freshness {{8mGHG5C-EPE@248}}
- [1v 2021 P] Squat and deadlift variations plus a smidgen of isolation is enough to build massive legs {{8mGHG5C-EPE@1253}}
- [1v 2021 P] A variation of the squat has more carryover than adding hack squats afterwards {{8mGHG5C-EPE@1441}}
- [1v 2021 P] Open with the back squat rather than bench because lower body lifts take more energy {{_L5wLHlFfI0@378}}
- [1v 2021 P] If you want occasional front squats, do them high volume with a cross-arm grip or on a Smith machine, which removes the stability demand {{pHNNbwju0GU@302}}
- [1v 2021 O] Looking back he wouldn't take up the front squat again; he would pick another variation or spend the time elsewhere {{pHNNbwju0GU@391}}
- [1v 2021 P] Start front squats light, as a back squat variation, and assess whether the investment is worth it {{pHNNbwju0GU@740}}
- [1v 2021 O] You don't have to squat to call yourself a lifter; that belief comes from powerlifting-centric YouTube fitness {{0RoULYC_tao@252}}
- [1v 2021 O] For big legs, bodyweight options (pistols, shrimp squats, banded good mornings) are technical or inefficient; deadlifts and squats are better {{HrUPp573ki0@559}}
- [1v 2021 P] Example rotation: alternate pause squats one week with Zercher squats the next while keeping back squats three times a week {{7n8RMhsxEB4@1129}}
- [1v 2021 P] Any lift can be a variation; the back squat is normally a main lift simply because it does that job so well {{7n8RMhsxEB4@186}}
- [1v 2021 O] Trying to isolate every muscle head with machines is a pro bodybuilder complication; for naturals a compound knee flexion covers most of the leg {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@2257}}
- [1v 2021 P] At equal load and reps a deeper squat activates more muscle, but you usually move more weight to parallel; squat as deep as your leverages allow without limiting progression {{GH8tqzGTgH8@1620}}
- [1v 2021 O] A 350 lb parallel squat may give better muscle activation than a 300 lb ATG squat {2021-07 {"load": "300 vs 350 lb"}} {{GH8tqzGTgH8@1684}}
- [1v 2021 P] Never open a leg session with leg extensions or hamstring curls; if you want a warm-up, do the main lift with less weight {{yvhW-zyhpsM@133}}
- [1v 2021 P] Always open with a heavy lift, either a heavy knee flexion or a heavy hip hinge, with heavy relative to your strength and goals {{yvhW-zyhpsM@189}}
- [1v 2021 O] Hack squats are good for hypertrophy, with big range of motion and less lower back strain, and could even replace back squats, though variations are hard to find and you can still get hurt {{P89o8FBSWDI@1203}}
- [1v 2021 O] Belt squats carry over to the squat, better to high bar because of the upright torso, with little lower back impact; they will lead to bigger legs for many people {{P89o8FBSWDI@2860}}
- [1v 2022 P] If poor internal rotation forces a wide, duck-footed back squat, make front squats your main squat; front squat variations alone can build big quads {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2191}}
- [1v 2022 P] Zercher or SSB squats push the torso forward like an opponent pulling or pushing you and recruit the upper back, unlike orthodox back squats {2022-01 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 4}} {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1684}}
- [1v 2022 P] Muay thai and kickboxing involve more strike volume than karate, so the lower body is trained for endurance legs and hips; getting flat-footed is dangerous {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@3189}}
- [1v 2022 A] Reviewing it live, he noticed the karate program had zero knee-flexion work and added squat and Bulgarian split squat options {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2747}}
- [1v 2022 P] Punching power starts at the floor, so boxers still need leg press, hack squats or squats to avoid imbalances {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@421}}
- [1v 2022 O] You can't build maximal leg size without weights; calisthenics athletes have average legs, around 24-25 inches, and weak posterior chains {2022-06 {"thigh": "24-25 in"}} {{QfHfxLYRu88@316}}
- [1v 2022 O] Rugby players' big legs come from crouched hinging and knee flexion on the field plus body fat; sled work grows legs if the hips are low {{QfHfxLYRu88@2891}}
- [1v 2022 O] Barbell hack squats and behind-the-back deadlifts are hybrids; behind-the-back deadlifts are mostly a hinge with little quad work {{QfHfxLYRu88@1945}}
- [1v 2022 P] Friday is lighter on the legs: lunges 4x10-15 or squats 3x6-8, ideally a different squat variation than Monday {2022-06 {"reps": "6-15", "sets": "3-4"}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@876}}
- [1v 2022 P] Bulgarian split squats 3-4x12-15 replace squats because Kinobody thinks squats overbuild the outer quad; unilateral work biases the VMO {2022-08 {"reps": "12-15", "sets": "3-4"}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@480}}
- [1v 2022 O] Minimalism is always limiting for lower body; branch out from one squat {{XAcv427WvMw@563}}
- [1v 2022 O] Low-bar squats look silly to some, but if they suit your build they are the right choice; nobody sees you squat, only your legs {{XAcv427WvMw@100}}
- [1v 2022 P] Tall lifters with long femurs (around 6'4) are usually better off low-bar squatting to parallel {{XAcv427WvMw@780}}
- [1v 2022 P] The Smith's fixed path lets you put the feet forward, which raises quad activation, lowers back strain and for some raises glute activation; useful for back injuries or extra leg volume without a hack or belt squat {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@756}}
- [1v 2022 P] Any squat pattern (high bar, front, hack) is the king of lower-body exercises; there is no better overall stimulus for the legs and posterior chain {{gYyqqbs2SLI@190}}
- [1v 2022 O] Avoiding squats because they are too hard is a mistake; there are safe ways to squat {{gYyqqbs2SLI@1198}}
- [1v 2023 O] Opening leg day with a hack squat (or Smith hack squat) for 5-10 reps then 10-15 rep backup sets is a good quad-biased start {2023-04 {"reps": "5-10, then 10-15"}} {{aJ1e41y8Na4@316}}
- [1v 2023 P] Lunges and split squats are energy and motivation vampires; never open or close leg day with them, third is ideal {{aJ1e41y8Na4@441}}
- [1v 2023 O] The SSB squat may be the best quad variation; Alex does it 3x10 at 2 RIR {2023-05 {"reps": 10, "rir": 2, "sets": 3}} {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@1072}}
- [1v 2023 O] Low-bar squats let you lift more due to leverage, not more muscle work: more fatigue, less stimulus for bodybuilding {{Z-hGdKz28RY@1257}}
- [1v 2023 O] The SSB is the best implement for pause squats because the bar cannot shift and form does not degrade: Platz tier {{_IBSNO56zuE@2766}}
- [1v 2023 O] Front squats are his favorite squat, A tier, but the front rack position is technically demanding and needs wrist flexibility {{_IBSNO56zuE@625}}
- [1v 2023 P] Knee flexions and hip hinges should be separated: the best quad exercise can never be the best hamstring exercise {{_IBSNO56zuE@63}}
- [1v 2023 O] Shrimp squats are C tier; pistol squats are B tier with good range but a hard-to-control negative that can strain the knee tendons {{_IBSNO56zuE@1500}}
- [1v 2023 O] Hybrid movements that try to be both a posterior chain and a quad exercise fail at both {{_IBSNO56zuE@188}}
- [1v 2023 O] Smith machine squats with feet forward are Platz tier for intermediates and advanced lifters: the knees can travel far forward for a big quad stretch without falling backward {{_IBSNO56zuE@500}}
- [1v 2023 O] High-bar squats are excellent (A tier), time-tested and how he built his legs, but for many people ankle mobility limits knee travel and they hip-hinge too much {{_IBSNO56zuE@250}}
- [1v 2023 O] The seated horizontal leg press recruits more quads and less glute and is A tier, but most machines max out around 200 lb, which he can do for 25 reps {2023-07 {"load": "200 lb", "reps": 25}} {{_IBSNO56zuE@998}}
- [1v 2023 O] Improvised belt squats standing on two platforms are dangerous at failure and the wide stance makes them more glute and adductor work: B tier {{_IBSNO56zuE@749}}
- [1v 2023 O] The adductor machine is A tier pure isolation for the adductors without taxing the lower back {{_IBSNO56zuE@2891}}
- [1v 2023 O] The regular hack squat is A tier, but its strength curve has an unbeatable sticking point where hip and knee align, making true failure hard {{_IBSNO56zuE@1879}}
- [1v 2023 O] Sissy squats are A tier, not knee destroyers, if done strictly with a slow negative; start band-assisted; best bodyweight quad isolation {{_IBSNO56zuE@1563}}
- [1v 2023 O] Low-bar squats are B tier for bodybuilders, only for those who cannot high-bar; more weight does not mean more quad recruitment {{_IBSNO56zuE@2640}}
- [1v 2023 O] Single-leg leg press is C tier: dedicated machines rarely allow a full stretch and on a normal press you must shift off-center {{_IBSNO56zuE@936}}
- [1v 2023 O] Barbell hack squats are A tier; Silver and Golden Era bodybuilders credited them for big quads and the spine is not loaded {{_IBSNO56zuE@2194}}
- [1v 2023 O] The machine belt squat is Platz tier: it destroys the quads without loading the spine and needs zero technical skill {{_IBSNO56zuE@749}}
- [1v 2023 P] Isolation movements are valuable for legs because they target specific quad areas without fatiguing the whole body {{_IBSNO56zuE@125}}
- [1v 2023 O] A pendulum-style leg press (e.g. Technogym) fixes the strength curve and feel: Platz tier, but rare and expensive {{_IBSNO56zuE@1125}}
- [1v 2023 O] Reverse hack squats defeat the purpose by letting the hips travel back into a hinge: B tier {{_IBSNO56zuE@1815}}
- [1v 2023 O] The king squat, a good morning/squat hybrid, is C tier: no load suits both patterns {{_IBSNO56zuE@2067}}
- [1v 2023 O] Split squats are A tier; avoid bumping the back knee on the floor to cheat the rep {{_IBSNO56zuE@1251}}
- [1v 2023 O] Sumo shiko stomping is C tier for size: bodyweight only and not scalable {{_IBSNO56zuE@2577}}
- [1v 2023 O] The SSB keeps the weight over midfoot so you can safely push pause squats close to failure {{6KA80BQ9W3k@507}}
- [1v 2023 P] On leg day compounds are 80-90% of the work; isolation is secondary {{6KA80BQ9W3k@818}}
- [1v 2023 P] He high-bar squats for 3 reps only about once a month to gauge strength; the squat is an accessory to his split squat, not the reverse {2023-09 {"frequency": "1x/month", "reps": 3}} {{43F9unmSc_I@758}}
- [1v 2023 P] Paused safety-bar squat 4-8 is his hardest squat, yet very safe {2023-09 {"reps": "4-8"}} {{43F9unmSc_I@820}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pre-fatigue (e.g. leg extensions before squats) is D tier; bias a muscle through exercise selection such as heel-elevated squats {{SxqjF2oPasg@3024}}
- [1v 2023 D] About 80% of NH's squat volume is now heel-elevated at 20-45 degrees {2023-12 {"elevation": "20-45 deg"}} {{DAZRYoex8_w@759}}
- [1v 2024 A] He now back squats about once a month and PRs pain-free each time; most quad volume comes from belt squats, heel-elevated SSB squats and split squats {2024-04 {"frequency": "back squat ~1x/month"}} {{Ft039H8jzXU@1695}}
- [1v 2024 O] Front squats are not worth it for most bodybuilders: they take mobility work, are limited by thoracic extension, and gave him wrist pain that hurt his upper-body training; a hack squat does the job better {{Ft039H8jzXU@691}}
- [1v 2024 P] Weighted Cossack squats train groin health, mobility and multi-directional joint patterns often ignored {{2LoP6-KdEx4@1067}}
- [1v 2024 O] Split squats beat shrimp and pistol squats for building mass {{2LoP6-KdEx4@1130}}
- [1v 2024 O] Goblet squats would be an ideal quad builder if they could be loaded to 200-300 lb, but 140 lb for 20 reps is not a challenge for him {2024-09 {"load": "140 lb", "reps": 20}} {{JW73oft8Q60@620}}
- [1v 2024 O] Front squats are limited by holding the rack position; for front-loaded quad work he prefers hack squats or belt squats, and he finds the strap trick awkward {{okm6bZHKHI4@1598}}
- [1v 2024 O] Trap bar deadlifts are useless for quads: little knee bend and no loaded eccentric {{okm6bZHKHI4@5545}}
- [1v 2024 P] Front squats suit low reps (4-6) because heavier loads keep the front rack from limiting {2024-10 {"reps": "4-6"}} {{c7hl9u3h_s0@4715}}
- [1v 2024 O] His big three for massive legs: split squats, RDLs and heel-elevated squats; RDLs put huge mass on the posterior chain {{c7hl9u3h_s0@385}}
- [1v 2024 O] Leg press is a perfectly fine leg builder despite squat supremacists {{c7hl9u3h_s0@2094}}
- [1v 2024 O] Low-bar squat is hard to justify for a bodybuilder unless no hack squat or high-bar squat is possible; low-bar squat plus RDL loads the lower back heavily {{6p2x4kDDjzs@4422}}
- [1v 2024 O] Heel-elevated squats are good for rectus femoris and he feels them more in the VMO than normal squats, but they may not develop all quad heads equally, so also include split squats {{Plr3f3LXO_o@8943}}
- [1v 2024 P] Heavy compounds are king for lower-body hypertrophy, but after a point plain squats and deadlifts give poor returns for the energy spent {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@63}}
- [1v 2024 D] Heel elevation costs about 15-30% of your squat load, which is good: more stimulus for less fatigue {2024-12 {"load": "-15-30%"}} {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@253}}
- [1v 2024 O] He is not a fan of the Sendback squat {{hXx-KDH2p28@452}}
- [1v 2024 O] Smith machine split squats are a better option than the free-weight version {{ZLheQPgEfoA@7529}}

## [quads] box / front / ssb / parallel (15)
- [2v 2020 OP] If the squat is your main knee flexion, use front squats, box squats and SSB squats rather than leg press, single-leg squats and lunges also: 2020-12: Box squats are inferior; he uses his box for box front squats and SSB box squats for deadlift carryover {{KTuKLbl0TOE@311}} {{SbPaZ5giUNs@434}}
- [1v 2020 P] His second squat day starts with front squats to a parallel box, then front rack holds, an Olympic accessory for core, upper back and thoracic extension strength to stay upright. {{j5PP3gx7DC4@314}}
- [1v 2020 P] Following squats with leg press is pointless; pick one knee-flexion family (squat, front, SSB, box squat) or leg press and its variations, for specificity {{GClcGq2tIDU@1386}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats as an accessory for high-bar squatting are not smart: with a wide stance and the box tight behind the knees they become a hip hinge, not knee flexion (fine only for low bar). {{vHf2ZoHWHQs@565}}
- [1v 2020 P] The SSB squat is not like a front squat: a front squat feels like being pulled forward at the chest via thoracic extension, the SSB like being pushed forward at the mid-back; neither replaces the other {{4ORCb_i7MxY@371}}
- [1v 2020 P] Use lifts that can't fail mechanically for high-volume leg work: Romanian deadlifts, pause squats, box squats {{pUpQypTV3Ck@439}}
- [1v 2020 O] Most people don't know how to box squat; for hypertrophy prefer pause high-bar squats for time under tension and specificity {{RgGkGiaGqks@817}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats don't recruit more quads, whatever studies say; most people turn them into a hip hinge without stretching the quads {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1099}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats are not great for hypertrophy nor for carryover to the regular squat {{OtoSjIZudIA@1860}}
- [1v 2021 O] The box front squat is the one version he likes: the front rack already enforces an upright torso, and the box helps with the front squat's real limiter, losing the rack {{bTin_tBGoVo@759}}
- [1v 2021 A] He does and recommends box front squats at or below parallel, preferring parallel; he never programs box back squats, especially for novices {{bTin_tBGoVo@937}}
- [1v 2021 O] Bodybuilders can use a box but get less than from normal squats, and most of their variations will be without one {{bTin_tBGoVo@728}}
- [1v 2021 P] Second favorite: parallel box squat with a close stance, carrying over to both squat and deadlift {{pvpf7Yuayco@292}}
- [1v 2022 P] Once the main squat is mastered, add variations: hack squat, front squat, SSB squat; spamming one squat style is not the way {{XAcv427WvMw@360}}
- [1v 2023 O] Box squats for novices make no sense; done with a low box, vertical torso and no rocking or resting, they are A tier for quads {{_IBSNO56zuE@2703}}

## [quads] quad / leg / extension / glut (11)
- [1v 2020 O] No leg extensions are needed; squats take care of the quads and glutes. {{b4rMVrWFVEg@754}}
- [1v 2020 O] NH doesn't like leg extensions but accepts them if you like them for quads. {{hi-kNbZnXz0@688}}
- [1v 2020 P] Leg curls and leg extensions are finishers, not mass builders; you need a squat-type knee flexion, or leg press if you dislike squats {{2ag0uM9zhs4@721}}
- [1v 2020 P] To build the legs you need knee flexion and hip hinging; lunges on an incline cover quads, hamstrings and glutes while staying athletic {{ZdcKQXMmmPY@580}}
- [1v 2020 P] Train quads, glutes and hamstrings together as a package on lower days {{ehxjCB_MdKc@940}}
- [1v 2021 O] A quad-only day makes little sense; never open leg day with leg extensions {{qq459xqmq5A@993}}
- [1v 2022 P] Leg extensions 4x15-20 add quad and VMO volume with little fatigue {2022-08 {"reps": "15-20", "sets": 4}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@580}}
- [1v 2023 O] Leg extensions are Platz tier; you only hurt your knees on them by using idiotic loads, and they are a rare true quad isolation {{_IBSNO56zuE@2954}}
- [1v 2024 P] The program's leg days are the best: squat, stiff-leg deadlift, lunges, leg extensions; one big quad move, one posterior-chain move, 1-2 isolations {{-OYtq2AHGX8@1393}}
- [1v 2024 O] The reverse lunge as main leg lift is on paper a quad movement but in practice mostly glutes; do not bank on it to grow quads {{bRKQIOYo2u4@3564}}
- [1v 2024 O] You can build great quads and glutes with only split squats and no squats {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@694}}

## [quads] front / technical / love / investment (8)
- [2v 2020 O] He loves front squats but doesn't think they're a good time investment for most because they're so technical {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@505}} {{i4YmJhlw7cs@561}}
- [1v 2020 O] Box squats are a poor choice for novices: not the same motion, stance or muscles as a squat, more lumbar and hip-structure load, less quad, and more technical than people think. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@191}}
