- [1v 2020 O] He loves front squats because they deload the lumbar spine, emphasise the quads and help thoracic extension. {{4aThavRpYLw@1011}}
- [1v 2020 A] He now must front squat plus a front squat variation weekly, sometimes twice, a prisoner of his own investment {2020-12 {"frequency": "1-2x/wk"}} {{6z43GClpmK8@570}}
- [1v 2021 O] Front squats as the main quad lift only make sense for Olympic weightlifters or people who love them {{P89o8FBSWDI@1203}}
- [1v 2021 O] Front squats may not be the best option for bodybuilders because they are too technical {{_Ak93cVwd1c@1400}}
- [1v 2022 O] The front squat is fine as a main lift but it is highly technical and takes heavy investment to learn {{XAcv427WvMw@140}}
- [1v 2023 O] Zercher squats are B tier; zombie squats are A tier, less technical but less stable than front squats {{_IBSNO56zuE@3080}}

## [quads] safety / amaz / bar / front (6)
- [2v 2020 OP] In weeks without back squats he still trains legs with safety squat bar squats and front squats. {{cHYOYTgiRlM@312}} {{TD9SB9Bt45o@420}}
- [1v 2020 P] If you need a narrow grip but it hurts, replace about 50% of your squat work with belt squats, hack squats or, best of all, safety bar squats to let the shoulders recover. {2020-03 {"percent": "50%"}} {{WXMc7tSB6jk@501}}
- [1v 2020 O] The front squat is an amazing leg and quad builder, which is why NH does it. {{wiuVyCccWC4@187}}
- [1v 2024 O] He dislikes cross-arm front squats as a safety matter because one shoulder must sit higher than the other {{okm6bZHKHI4@1219}}
- [1v 2024 P] Split squats are his favourite leg movement because their stimulus-to-fatigue ratio is excellent; load them with a safety squat bar or similar rather than dumbbells {{c7hl9u3h_s0@300}}
- [1v 2024 A] Moving split squats from dumbbells to a safety squat bar let him add 80 lb; the balance demand had made him unknowingly go light {2024-10 {"load": "+80 lb"}} {{c7hl9u3h_s0@324}}

## [quads] 4-8 / bar / sets / low (6)
- [1v 2020 P] His big squat day opens with high-bar squats (he dislikes low bar and thinks high bar looks better) for 1-3 heavy sets of 4-8 reps. {2020-02 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": "1-3"}} {{j5PP3gx7DC4@127}}
- [1v 2020 A] He never squats for 2 reps; his squat window is 4-8 with a minimum of 4 {2020-08 {"reps": "4-8"}} {{pUpQypTV3Ck@375}}
- [1v 2020 P] Back-off set or variation after squats must be a pure knee flexion with a bar on the back (paused, front, SSB squat), not a good morning: 3 sets of 4-8 {2020-10 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 3}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1015}}
- [1v 2020 P] Paused squats 3 sets of 4-8 superset with pull-ups finish the posterior chain on deadlift day {2020-10 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 3}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1937}}
- [1v 2021 P] Use low bar as the main squat, then add high-bar pause squats to parallel, front squats or safety-bar squats instead of getting all the volume from the main lift {{3nyuEtmqL-I@770}}
- [1v 2022 P] Consciously pick your squat style (high bar, low bar or front) by what feels natural for your build, not by what looks good; years on an unsuited style are wasted {{XAcv427WvMw@64}}

## [quads] unilateral / leg / call / fan (4)
- [2v 2021-2024 P] Athletes should place unilateral movements after the knee flexion and hip hinge, done fatigued with less weight and higher reps, instead of leg extensions {{yvhW-zyhpsM@881}} {{Ft039H8jzXU@1695}}
- [1v 2020 A] Almost all his lower body gains came from squat and deadlift variations; he never did hack squats, leg press, leg extensions or leg curls. {{awNEfmfGkmY@0}}
- [1v 2020 O] He is not a fan of unilateral leg movements but does not call them bad {{dHXPhjaWBWk@689}}
- [1v 2022 P] Use unilateral movements, leg press or Smith machine squats to add leg volume without taxing the lower back, as many tall lifters do {{XAcv427WvMw@1188}}

## [quads] elevat / platz / heel / torso (5)
- [1v 2023 O] The Platz squat (high bar, very close stance, toes forward, heels elevated, vertical torso) is the most quad-dominant squat; expect to halve your back-squat weight: Platz tier {2023-07 {"load": "-50%"}} {{_IBSNO56zuE@1625}}
- [1v 2023 P] Elevating the heels with squat shoes or small plates fixes high-bar squats, keeping the torso vertical and knees travelling forward: Platz tier {{_IBSNO56zuE@374}}
- [1v 2023 O] The deficit sumo squat is to knee flexions what the RDL is to hip hinges: Platz tier; initiate with the knees and keep a vertical torso {{_IBSNO56zuE@2323}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bulgarian split squats are Platz tier; elevating the back leg removes it from the effort so you get more out of less weight {{_IBSNO56zuE@1313}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do all squats with elevated heels: more quads, less lumbar involvement, easier recovery {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@126}}

## [quads] split / balance / belt-squat / 75-80 (5)
- [1v 2024 A] He plateaued on split squats because balance with dumbbells was the limit; belt-squat split squats with hands free fixed it {{l6L6HlGM4F0@251}}
- [1v 2024 P] Belt-squat split squats keep you vertical and are almost pure quad, good for VMO but not for glutes {{l6L6HlGM4F0@315}}
- [1v 2024 P] Eliminating balance demands improves split squats and similar lifts {{sZntx8mqaH8@629}}
- [1v 2024 P] He banned dumbbell split squats because at 75-80 lb for 12 he failed from balance, not leg fatigue; belt squat and safety-bar split squats let him load almost double {2024-12 {"load": "75-80 lb DB vs 205-210 lb", "reps": 12}} {{TvKzeUKgZcY@1959}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dumbbell split squats are the worst variation: balance limits you around 75-80 lb per hand rather than the muscle {2024-12 {"load": "75-80 lb/hand"}} {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@694}}

## [quads] aggravate / deload / substitute / quad (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Front squats deload the back, so they are a good substitute when back squats aggravate the lower back. {{1R42snjAX4I@126}}
- [1v 2020 O] He doesn't like leg curls or leg extensions much and would rather see squats, but they're fine if they don't aggravate your knees. {{eFhwWirR70o@1053}}
- [1v 2020 O] Using the SSB as a front squat substitute is terrible advice: front squats are quad dominant, the SSB is posterior chain and lower back {{I7vk4Kk5neA@315}}
- [1v 2022 P] Extra quad work should come from movements that deload the lower back and stretch the quads, like single-leg press or split squats {2022-10 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{CSwbuwl33eA@560}}

## [quads] front / recommend / people / legs (4)
- [1v 2020 O] For most people he does not recommend front squats. {{OATtf3kPtdI@750}}
- [1v 2020 P] Front squats after back squats work well: for most people the front squat is limited by thoracic extension and front rack, not legs, so pre-fatiguing the legs equalizes them and works the legs harder. {2020-05 {"reps": 5, "sets": 3}} {{0yIieHQjEiA@1192}}
- [1v 2020 O] Holding SSB handles with crossed arms can make a more comfortable front squat for people who cannot front rack {{I7vk4Kk5neA@501}}
- [1v 2020 P] Paused squats are possibly the best pure knee-flexion variation for big legs and a bigger squat, but 8-12 reps to failure is masochistic; he recommends 4-8 reps for quads {2020-12 {"reps": "4-8"}} {{hYFO2eKn-m4@687}}

## [quads] goblet / range / motion / reverse (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Dumbbell squats are inferior to barbell and goblet squats because of range of motion; they become a reverse dumbbell deadlift {{NiV6lE0XUH8@1236}}
- [1v 2020 O] Hack squats are good because their angle allows deep range of motion {{eobG2o3Ww4M@998}}
- [1v 2021 P] If squats do not suit your build, lunges, reverse lunges and goblet squats can still build good legs {{P89o8FBSWDI@1684}}
- [1v 2021 P] Goblet squats 4x8-12 for quad volume; paused squats work in the same range but are torture {2021-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@960}}

## [quads] legs / small / development / calisthenic (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Calisthenics is not the best for leg development; pistol squats aren't the same and small legs are an advantage for bodyweight performance {{RgGkGiaGqks@62}}
- [1v 2021 O] Small legs in calisthenics athletes are a feature: a lighter lower body improves performance {{HrUPp573ki0@507}}
- [1v 2022 O] The squat is the king of lower-body development; if you want big legs you should squat {{XAcv427WvMw@0}}
- [1v 2023 O] Calisthenics athletes claiming pistols build big legs still have small legs; split squats are more effective with less skill and balance {{_IBSNO56zuE@1563}}

## [quads] zercher / ssb / replace / complement (4)
- [1v 2020 P] On the second lower day replace leg extensions with a quad-focused squat (Zercher or SSB front squat) for 3x6-10, complementing lumbar-focused deadlifts {2020-11 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 3}} {{xQQJUXf58ZE@1678}}
- [1v 2021 P] You don't need front squats to grow legs; zercher, reverse SSB squats or unilateral work can replace them {{pHNNbwju0GU@758}}
- [1v 2021 O] Leg extensions have no carryover to the squat; they can complement squats but never replace them, and he usually cuts them from programs as junk volume {{ANvvVrMqF7Y@2319}}
- [1v 2021 P] Alternate deadlifts with Zercher/SSB squats weekly; only deadlifting creates imbalances because you never train knee flexion {{bqcRtC3bbK8@771}}

## [quads] chicken / knee / step-up / leg (4)
- [1v 2023 O] The vertical leg press is chicken tier: awkward position, pelvis rounding that endangers the lower back and knees into the chest {{_IBSNO56zuE@874}}
- [1v 2023 O] Banded lying leg extensions are chicken tier: impossible to overload, only for warm-ups or knee rehab {{_IBSNO56zuE@1188}}
- [1v 2023 O] Dumbbell leg extensions are chicken tier: bad strength curve and maximal knee shear at the top {{_IBSNO56zuE@2004}}
- [1v 2023 O] Step-ups are chicken tier for hypertrophy: limited knee flexion and no negative {{_IBSNO56zuE@1437}}

## [quads] smith / machine / ssb / split (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Smith machine front squats work well: you can use the bodybuilder crossed-arm rack without fear of dropping the bar, keep a very vertical torso and focus on the quads, replacing a hack squat machine. {{TrU2m-4yZT0@442}}
- [1v 2024 P] Split squats with an SSB or Smith machine are better for bodybuilders than holding dumbbells: you get stronger with less cardiovascular strain {{6yW3GSskl8g@949}}
- [1v 2024 O] He abandoned dumbbell Bulgarian split squats as inferior for hypertrophy; do them on a Smith machine, with SSB, or on a belt squat {{qvWxIzhT1QU@759}}

## [quads] pain / imbalanc / dislik / stance (3)
- [1v 2020 O] He disputes the claimed box-squat benefits (better recovery, less knee pain, explosiveness): people rock and good-morning the weight up, the wide stance barely uses quads, and the stressed range may create imbalances leading to knee pain. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@317}}
- [1v 2021 O] He dislikes the leg press; many people get lower back pain from it {{8mGHG5C-EPE@497}}
- [1v 2023 O] Jefferson squats are B tier: limited range, staggered stance and possible imbalances {{_IBSNO56zuE@2130}}

## [quads] extension / leg / knee / duplicate (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Leg extensions are fairly dangerous for the knee joint for many people {{4rjywMLXt98@250}}
- [1v 2020 O] Leg extensions are the cherry on top; legs grow from squats and deadlifts, and one heavy set of extensions just hurts the knee {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1978}}
- [1v 2020 P] Leg press and leg extensions duplicate each other; drop one and do more knee flexion work {{dHXPhjaWBWk@1446}}

## [quads] leg / hack / pick / same (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Do not do leg press and hack squat on the same day; pick one, progress it, rotate if bored {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1978}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hack squat is for those who don't want squat technicality; leg press for those with lower back problems {{SPicD3F686s@1123}}
- [1v 2024 O] Smith squat plus leg press on the same day is redundant; pick one {{6p2x4kDDjzs@1763}}

## [quads] leg / save / compound / quad-dominant (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Single-leg press, leg extensions and leg curls have zero specificity to compounds; a compound saves time and feeds the squat and deadlift {{dHXPhjaWBWk@689}}
- [1v 2021 P] Legs work as a group and cannot really be isolated; going beyond the idea of an isolation leg day gets more results, saves time and prevents injuries {{yvhW-zyhpsM@127}}
- [1v 2023 P] A good leg day: heavy quad-dominant compound (e.g. squat 6-10) then something for the posterior chain like leg curls, and you're done {2023-12 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{hEuy__sCbZA@507}}

## [quads] never / superset / 10-15 / 8-12 (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Program belt squats for 4 sets of 10-15 or 8-12, never low reps; use them on hinge day after deadlifts (superset with pull-ups) or after back squats when the lower back is fried {2021-05 {"reps": "8-15", "sets": 4}} {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@2014}}
- [1v 2023 P] Do split squats for 8-12 or 10-15 reps, never 30, or the fatigue is excessive {2023-04 {"reps": "8-15"}} {{aJ1e41y8Na4@504}}
- [1v 2024 P] A Bulgarian split squat should never close a superset, and never superset two lower-body compounds {{6p2x4kDDjzs@5872}}

## [quads] spar / hack / 3x10-12 / knee (3)
- [1v 2021 P] After deadlifts, use a knee flexion that spares the lower back, such as hack squats at 3x10-12 {2021-06 {"reps": "10-12", "sets": 3}} {{Mq2TaHm_ySI@823}}
- [1v 2023 P] Heel-elevated squats (3-5x6-12) or hack squats (3-5x8-15) give nearly pure quad work while sparing the posterior chain {2023-01 {"reps": "6-12 / 8-15", "sets": "3-5"}} {{_P0-kYyVMn4@631}}
- [1v 2023 O] The pendulum hack squat is Platz tier: spares the lower back, huge range of motion, can grind and get partner assistance on the positive, easy on the knees {{_IBSNO56zuE@1941}}

## [quads] knee-flexion / pattern / monday / 4x6-10 (3)
- [1v 2021 P] For someone adapted to it, the squat is the best way to grow the legs and should be the main knee-flexion pattern, with variations built around it {{3bEkmyM8NBQ@0}}
- [1v 2022 P] Monday: squats (any proper knee-flexion pattern) 4x6-10 {2022-06 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@251}}
- [1v 2023 P] You cannot build legs as big as possible without a squat or heavy knee-flexion pattern; any variant (low bar, SSB, hack, belt squat) works {{ABTeMrXdgI4@313}}

## [quads] heel-elevat / quad / glut / pre-ignit (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Heel-elevated squats are better for quad growth than regular squats for someone whose glutes dominate, even with less weight {{9y2s6kyFkPc@1188}}
- [1v 2023 P] Leg press before a heel-elevated SSB squat 'pre-ignites' the quads for lifters with long legs who can't reach quad failure on squats {2023-06 {"reps": "8-15 then 5-8"}} {{Z-hGdKz28RY@375}}
- [1v 2023 D] Expect to cut the load by about 20-30% on heel-elevated squats because hamstrings, glutes and lower back contribute less; less weight with more quad stimulus is a win {2023-07 {"load": "-20-30%"}} {{_IBSNO56zuE@374}}

## [quads] lung / finisher / walk / regard (3)
- [1v 2022 O] Bodyweight walking lunges as finishers are mostly pump; to cause damage, weight them {{gizylGr0ebo@1063}}
- [1v 2023 O] Walking lunges are the worst single-leg option (C tier): limited by cardio and grip, inconsistent reps; static lunges are better (B tier) {{_IBSNO56zuE@1188}}
- [1v 2024 P] Treating split squats or lunges as finishers is poor programming; regard them as heavy main compounds equal to squats {{-OjJEzZTXtQ@631}}

## [quads] high-bar / hack / failure / move (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Platz was built to squat; long-legged lifters who turn high-bar squats into good mornings should pick a variation that suits their leverages (e.g. hack squat) {{gYyqqbs2SLI@1072}}
- [1v 2023 P] Low-bar squats move more weight by biasing glutes and lower back; high-bar or hack squats move less weight but grow quads better {{Zqv7KVsmu78@1010}}
- [1v 2023 O] Technical failure blocks muscular failure, which is why hack squats can beat high-bar squats for quads {{Zqv7KVsmu78@821}}

## [quads] gap / leg / minor / legs (3)
- [1v 2022 P] The gap between leg press and squat is minor; the leg press still builds big legs {{gYyqqbs2SLI@252}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pick one main squat style and one variation, get stronger at them, then fill the volume gap with less fatiguing isolation movements {{_IBSNO56zuE@3142}}
- [1v 2023 O] The classic 45-degree leg press is only B tier because better variants exist, though you can build big legs with it {{_IBSNO56zuE@998}}

## [quads] chicken / stimulus-to / ratio / bosu (3)
- [1v 2023 O] Sumo deadlifts are chicken tier for legs: mostly a hip hinge with poor range and stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {{_IBSNO56zuE@2451}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bosu or unstable-surface squats are chicken tier; overhead squats are C tier for bodybuilders {{_IBSNO56zuE@3017}}
- [1v 2023 P] Heel-elevated squats give more effective range, less lower-back strain and a better stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {{DAZRYoex8_w@822}}

## [shoulders] overhead / lateral / incline / rais (62)
- [1v 2020 O] The overhead press is very important but taxing on lower back and shoulders for many, so it gets little focus in this novice-friendly program. {{xFVva4-swKc@627}}
- [1v 2020 P] Bench day 2 finishes with ring pull-ups and push-ups, light high-volume side raises (shoulders are fresh with no heavy overhead press), a push-up/pull-up superset, band work, neck, calves and abs. {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@445}}
- [1v 2020 P] At least one vertical press per week is non-negotiable; the overhead press needs no accessory at first, and just doing it puts you ahead of most novices. {2020-02 {"frequency": "1x/week minimum"}} {{_4wWgin1dVY@630}}
- [1v 2020 O] The 'you never do that in real life' argument against front raises is poor; you never lie on your back and press in real life either. {{1P-3YblXBRI@124}}
- [1v 2020 O] Pairing close-grip bench (arms/chest) with Z-press (shoulders) at 4x8-12 gives freedom to manipulate volume and intensity. {2020-05 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 4}} {{rucwL-SIefg@1480}}
- [1v 2020 O] The push press is poor for shoulder hypertrophy because you cannot tell how much the legs help; for size, touch-and-go overhead press beats paused because of the stretch and elastic response {{PYmr91aELnE@192}}
- [1v 2020 O] Wide-grip barbell upright rows are a good shoulder superset with rows as long as the form is correct {{PYmr91aELnE@726}}
- [1v 2020 P] High-rep band face pulls keep the rotator cuff healthy and build the upper back {{u10c43TRDb0@691}}
- [1v 2020 O] There are few truly bad positions besides universal faults like flared shoulders or a rounded lumbar spine; lifts like behind-the-neck presses suit some people if approached with respect {{rsbqqfM6ZFM@441}}
- [1v 2020 O] Dumbbell overhead press for 3x10-12 is excellent; the hardest part with dumbbells is getting them into position {2020-07 {"reps": "10-12", "sets": 3}} {{du7JYCA2pa4@1063}}
- [1v 2020 O] Incline bench and overhead press in the same day are somewhat redundant; overhead press is my pick {{-banYSTnkq4@625}}
- [1v 2020 O] One high-intensity set of lateral raises is unwise for the delicate shoulder; dumbbell plus cable laterals are redundant and exist only to honor the program's philosophy {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1415}}
- [1v 2020 P] Pull-ups and chin-ups are excellent for the shoulders; they become dangerous only with poor shoulder mobility {{EElvKR4K_HE@312}}
- [1v 2020 P] Variations close in specificity (e.g. barbell to axle overhead press) feed the main lift; seated or kneeling is further away, and lateral raises carry almost nothing over {{xQQJUXf58ZE@330}}
- [1v 2020 O] He's a big fan of barbell overhead press after bench, strength focused, 3x6-8 {2020-11 {"reps": "6-8", "sets": 3}} {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@648}}
- [1v 2020 O] He would never treat an incline press as strength work; if you want shoulder emphasis use overhead press or incline bench {{DQo0DGn_wI4@970}}
- [1v 2021 A] His own tests: opening with a horizontal press leaves him fresher for overhead press than the reverse order {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1060}}
- [1v 2021 O] Separating the bench and the dumbbell press with the incline fly is smart because it rests the shoulders {{pey1Ni6HPlY@295}}
- [1v 2021 O] The overhead press tends to give tall lifters the fewest problems, and tall lifters often do well on pull-ups despite the extra range {{3nyuEtmqL-I@905}}
- [1v 2021 A] Two exercises between presses is plenty of recovery; more spacing doesn't change pressing performance much {{_L5wLHlFfI0@440}}
- [1v 2021 O] The Arnold press is a gimmicky hybrid lift: fine at the end of an arm or press day but too random to bank progress on; he would not program it {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@258}}
- [1v 2021 P] If you want shoulder or wrist rotation work, do it separately with light weight as mobility rather than mixing it into a press {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@320}}
- [1v 2021 O] Behind-the-neck presses are not dangerous per se; the load makes them dangerous {{3bEkmyM8NBQ@250}}
- [1v 2021 P] Using leg drive (jerk/push press) to lift more overhead doesn't build bigger shoulders; it unloads the shoulders by sharing work with the legs {{_Ak93cVwd1c@840}}
- [1v 2021 O] The overhead press and the bench are the lifts he most supports supersetting because the paired exercise won't damage their performance {{jAnfGqpsK38@313}}
- [1v 2021 P] Push press 4x4-8 after front squats; this combo is advanced and wrecks abs and cardio {2021-10 {"reps": "4-8", "sets": 4}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@1756}}
- [1v 2022 O] Landmine press is often called more MMA-specific, while dumbbell overhead press may be better for shoulder endurance; choose by preference {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@2258}}
- [1v 2022 O] Front raises copy a common daily motion, while pistols, shrimp squats and single-arm or single-leg calisthenics look cool but aren't functional {{kAVZsNLPNQ4@691}}
- [1v 2022 O] Gada (mace) swings strengthen the upper body and are still used in wrestling gyms; Chris Duffin's Shoulder Rock sells the same idea at ten times the price {{4W_D07eE29I@753}}
- [1v 2022 P] Incline dumbbell flies 4x12-15 superset with cable lateral raises 4x10-15; do the chest move first, because fatiguing the shoulder first destabilises the flies and risks rotator cuff tendonitis {2022-08 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{o_x3Ij2GBXM@377}}
- [1v 2022 P] Compounds give more fatigue and more stimulus, so they are the priority; you will not get big round natural shoulders from lateral raises alone; if you must skip, skip an isolation set {{BhOuuyriBDw@1632}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pick 1-2 vertical presses and 2-3 shoulder isolations: minimum 3, maximum 5 shoulder movements, to stay specific while varying planes to spare tendons {2022-10 {"exercises": "3-5"}} {{BhOuuyriBDw@315}}
- [1v 2022 O] For isolations any movement that hits the three delt heads and spares the joints works, even upright rows {{BhOuuyriBDw@442}}
- [1v 2022 P] True isolation is a myth, but you can bias a muscle by removing helper muscles, e.g. upright rows for shoulders {{FC8x-cwd4DQ@649}}
- [1v 2023 O] Gada swings are B tier: not for hypertrophy but good for shoulder mobility and health {{k3yRgcmiCls@3201}}
- [1v 2023 O] Push press is mediocre: legs move the supramaximal load and you skip the negative {{k3yRgcmiCls@882}}
- [1v 2023 P] Tiers: Boulder (S), excellent (A), decent (B), mediocre (C), Pebble (D); ranking considers hypertrophy, shoulder health, mobility and stimulus-to-fatigue, comparing lifts within the same family {{k3yRgcmiCls@63}}
- [1v 2023 P] Incline presses beyond a slight incline are more shoulder than chest (A tier); close-grip incline press is S tier for deeper shoulder flexion but riskier, so build up gradually {{k3yRgcmiCls@2385}}
- [1v 2023 O] Dips are S tier and could build massive chest and shoulders alone, but are riskier than presses because there is no safety net at the bottom {{k3yRgcmiCls@3014}}
- [1v 2023 P] Reverse flies (seated or on an incline bench) are A tier: they counter internal rotation, hit mid and lower traps and help press stability; use much less weight than laterals {{k3yRgcmiCls@1070}}
- [1v 2023 P] Behind-the-neck press is A tier if controlled: he stopped at the bump behind his head and never had shoulder issues; use a Smith machine if worried {{k3yRgcmiCls@1322}}
- [1v 2023 P] Lean-away (Egyptian) unilateral lateral raises are excellent: leaning prevents shrugging and keeps tension through the useful range {{k3yRgcmiCls@504}}
- [1v 2023 P] Decline ring push-ups with a vertical torso are S tier: safe on wrists and elbows with a perfect stimulus-to-fatigue ratio {{k3yRgcmiCls@3140}}
- [1v 2023 P] Strict barbell overhead press done from a rack is Boulder tier; cleaning it from the floor wastes energy and stability {{k3yRgcmiCls@251}}
- [1v 2023 O] A plain machine shoulder press is S tier and could be your only press; whatever puts the muscle under tension is good {{k3yRgcmiCls@1447}}
- [1v 2023 O] The Z press is mediocre: being less stable and lifting less weight does not recruit the shoulders more {{k3yRgcmiCls@379}}
- [1v 2023 P] Dumbbell external rotations are useless because tension vanishes at the top; use cables or face pulls {{k3yRgcmiCls@1885}}
- [1v 2023 P] Combination lifts like the Cuban press fail because each shoulder function needs a different load {{k3yRgcmiCls@1384}}
- [1v 2023 O] Bench press trains the shoulders but delt activation competes with pecs, so B tier {{k3yRgcmiCls@2385}}
- [1v 2023 P] Prioritise the V-taper (shoulders and lats) and abs; train arms moderately and possibly skip triceps long head work, which adds the most arm bulk {{HOgwZO70z_M@438}}
- [1v 2023 P] Partial-range behind-the-neck press (stopping behind the head) trades range of motion for load safely, avoiding impingement {2023-04 {"load": "90 kg", "reps": 5, "sets": 5}} {{-sf34ScWLK8@380}}
- [1v 2023 P] Muscle built by pressing carries over to lateral raises, so the two lifts feed each other in a virtuous circle; a single movement hits diminishing returns {{yRRXCYcgzJs@573}}
- [1v 2024 P] The overhead swing people copy is mostly a front raise followed by a pullover pattern, so it mainly hits shoulders, traps and upper back; most people don't step in, so legs are left out {{29XrRmOrCMs@380}}
- [1v 2024 P] He chose front raises because cheating let him use more weight; a bodybuilder should pick variations that work the muscle with less weight {{Ft039H8jzXU@879}}
- [1v 2024 P] Upright rows with a wrist roller handle let the wrists move freely and helped his shoulder pain; use about 30 lb for 10-20 reps {2024-05 {"load": "30 lb", "reps": "10-20"}} {{l6L6HlGM4F0@1065}}
- [1v 2024 A] He dropped Arnold presses not because they are suboptimal but because they no longer fit in the program {{bRKQIOYo2u4@8172}}
- [1v 2024 O] Military press is a plateau machine because it is unstable; Smith press, single-arm or seated dumbbell press progress better {{c7hl9u3h_s0@2219}}
- [1v 2024 O] Cables are for people who train hard: cuffed cable lateral raises allow more weight and reps than dumbbells because the line of pull stays fixed and the stack controls the negative {{6yW3GSskl8g@1139}}
- [1v 2024 O] He replaced the overhead press with an 80-degree incline press on the Smith machine because he can progress on it; a lift you can't progress on is a bad lift {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@5510}}
- [1v 2024 P] Lateral delt isolation before or after pressing makes little difference, and doing it before dips makes dips feel better; never isolate triceps before dips or presses because they become the limiting factor {{ZLheQPgEfoA@945}}
- [1v 2024 P] His favourite shoulder movements: Smith overhead press on a near-vertical bench, shallow incline press, cable lateral raises; upright rows are a valid shoulder isolation that was wrongly demonised {{ZLheQPgEfoA@6478}}
- [1v 2025 O] Low reps like 4 on a cable lateral raise are impractical: too close to your 1RM and clunky {2025-03 {"reps": 4}} {{ACFKXT44BzM@310}}

## [shoulders] rais / lateral / lu / delt (12)
- [2v 2020-2023 OP] Front raises are not useless since every delt head works in any raise, but they are redundant with presses; prioritize lateral raises to avoid reinforcing an already strong front delt {{KMcJAERb9Dk@441}} {{k3yRgcmiCls@1133}}
- [2v 2021-2022 P] Opening a shoulder day with 10x10 lateral raises pre-fatigues for nothing; start with a heavy compound press when you are freshest {2022-06 {"reps": 10, "sets": 10}} {{qq459xqmq5A@866}} {{uYRIWAKlA0c@1630}}
- [2v 2021-2022 OP] Lu raises (Lu Xiaojun lateral raise variation) 3x8-12, start very light because of shoulder mobility demands {2021-10 {"reps": "8-12", "sets": 3}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@1495}} {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2885}}
- [1v 2020 P] Lateral raises and face pulls cannot replace each other; one loads the medial delt, the other the rear delt, so do both {{MCUpClPnPkQ@813}}
- [1v 2020 P] Lateral raises at 4x20 are a good range to control the weight instead of swinging it {2020-06 {"reps": 20, "sets": 4}} {{PFQ30yaL1t0@375}}
- [1v 2021 O] Front raises are fine if pain-free {{qq459xqmq5A@866}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lu raises are only A tier: overhyped, limit load and wrist/grip comfort, though great for mobility and small rear shoulder muscles {{k3yRgcmiCls@630}}
- [1v 2023 P] Cable lateral raises are the best lateral raise (S tier) due to constant tension; do them however you best feel the delt {{k3yRgcmiCls@567}}
- [1v 2023 P] Spamming lateral raises usually forces you to drop horizontal presses, removing the lift that would give the biggest shoulder gains and support lateral raise progress {{yRRXCYcgzJs@447}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do both overhead press and lateral raises: the side delt is only an accessory mover in the press but the prime mover in raises, so they complement rather than cannibalize each other {{bRKQIOYo2u4@2559}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do overhead pressing before lateral raises because it is the compound {{okm6bZHKHI4@10267}}
- [1v 2024 O] Lu raises are fine for shoulder health or rehab but poor for hypertrophy because light plates limit loading {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1587}}

## [shoulders] press / vertical / horizontal / arms (7)
- [2v 2022-2023 P] No law says horizontal pressing must outnumber vertical; if shoulders are the goal, do more vertical pressing {{QfHfxLYRu88@1007}} {{k3yRgcmiCls@2073}}
- [1v 2020 P] It does little for vertical presses like the overhead press because of the biomechanics; it is meant for horizontal presses. {{Pr6to3cPR1g@184}}
- [1v 2022 A] He will bring back vertical presses, which he had stopped because they grew his shoulders too much, now that his arms outgrow them {{O9txIDHBBIk@1660}}
- [1v 2022 O] The landmine press 'instability' argument is invalid; it is a hybrid between vertical and horizontal pressing, best used as an accessory to the overhead press {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1074}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pick presses you enjoy and can do comfortably long-term, not 'optimal' ones; the only requirement is that it is a vertical press {{BhOuuyriBDw@378}}
- [1v 2024 A] Shoulder-dominant lifters can skip vertical presses entirely; a natural with huge shoulders at his gym does {{okm6bZHKHI4@10330}}
- [1v 2024 P] When the shoulders dominate the arms, cut back on vertical pressing and favor horizontal pressing and vertical pulling {{pt4mbNb7NJ0@5384}}

## [shoulders] face / push / combination / posture (5)
- [2v 2021 OP] Face pulls belong at the end of a pushing session {2021-01 {"reps": 20, "sets": 4} | 2021-03 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@215}} {{_L5wLHlFfI0@440}}
- [1v 2020 P] Banded face pulls 4x25 are ideal; high reps let you sense mechanical failure, and face pulls done with postural failure reinforce bad posture {2020-06 {"reps": 25, "sets": 4}} {{PFQ30yaL1t0@312}}
- [1v 2021 O] Overhead press and close-grip bench on push 2 is a great combination {2021-01 {"reps": "6-8 and 8-10", "sets": "4 and 3"}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@990}}
- [1v 2021 P] Use the time saved for lateral raises, face pulls, forearms and biceps; pressing-heavy sessions need balancing {{62OBPecQPnM@688}}
- [1v 2023 O] Ring face pulls beat cable face pulls (S tier) because bodyweight keeps loading the eccentric while a cable stack unloads {{k3yRgcmiCls@1696}}

## [shoulders] handstand / push-up / pike / deficit (3)
- [2v 2023 O] Deficit handstand push-ups are probably the best bodyweight shoulder exercise {2023-04 {"reps": "4-6", "sets": 10}} {{-sf34ScWLK8@1453}} {{mMA3KF3qmlQ@567}}
- [1v 2020 P] If overhead pressing hurts the elbow, try incline presses, pike or handstand push-ups which involve more shoulder flexion {{gAmsgcm-8Q8@1380}}
- [1v 2023 P] Push-ups rank higher for shoulders the more the torso is vertical and the range is increased: pike C, deficit B, decline A, handstand push-up S {{k3yRgcmiCls@693}}

## [shoulders] seat / stand / athletic / populariz (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Standing dumbbell press is more athletic and works the core more than seated {{NiV6lE0XUH8@698}}
- [1v 2021 O] Standing dumbbell press can be done seated; it makes little difference and 4x10-12 complements the bench well {2021-01 {"reps": "10-12", "sets": 4}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@164}}
- [1v 2024 P] Stability means how easily you hold the body and joints at the right angle to hit the target muscle; a seated dumbbell press beats a standing overhead press because hips, knees and torso lean are removed {{6yW3GSskl8g@319}}
- [1v 2024 P] Face pulls done seated in the lat pulldown machine are good; people do them standing only because the person who popularized them did {{6p2x4kDDjzs@378}}

## [shoulders] delt / rais / rear / mind-muscle (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Arnold raises, from his Road to Bigger Shoulders series, stretch the rear delt at the bottom and fully contract it at the top; no lift beats them for mind-muscle connection {{NJd_zD9OeQ4@254}}
- [1v 2020 O] Needle raises target side delts more but still build the rear delts {{NJd_zD9OeQ4@254}}
- [1v 2020 O] NH prefers Arnold raises to bent-over lateral raises because the latter recruit too much trap, which overpowers his rear delts {{01jOBsrQCPg@62}}
- [1v 2024 A] Switching to cuffs for lateral raises improved his mind-muscle connection and let him use more weight and reps; refusing to even try something new is stupid {{xqjfYhZ-CvQ@828}}

## [shoulders] viking / neutral / overhead / grip (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Overhead press is preferred for shoulders; Viking press is fine if you like the neutral grip {2020-10 {"reps": "8-10", "sets": 4}} {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1330}}
- [1v 2022 P] No straight horizontal press on upper 2 because Zodd's shoulders dwarf his chest; use incline or Viking press (Viking press only if you need no chest work) {2022-10 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 4}} {{CSwbuwl33eA@622}}
- [1v 2023 P] Trap bar overhead press is S tier for those who dislike straight-bar pressing: neutral grip and more range; grip the frame, not the handles {{k3yRgcmiCls@1008}}
- [1v 2023 P] Pick whatever overhead press grip or implement is comfortable (narrow, snap, behind the neck, dumbbells, neutral) as long as the arm moves overhead {{ABTeMrXdgI4@225}}

## [shoulders] lateral / rais / bent-over / overuse (4)
- [1v 2021 P] Lateral and bent-over raises often overuse the traps and can hurt the shoulder as load rises {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@378}}
- [1v 2022 P] Bent-over lateral raises 4x12-15 instead of standing laterals, since side delts matter little for most women but upper-back shape does {2022-06 {"reps": "12-15", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@687}}
- [1v 2023 P] Lying lateral power raises with momentum give a great side-delt stretch but are for advanced lifters only; beginners can hurt themselves {{awwJ5sbyz6A@253}}
- [1v 2024 P] Rather than repeating lateral raises on several days, rotate in upright rows or Y-raises to cut overuse injuries and keep performance progressing {{okm6bZHKHI4@2809}}

## [shoulders] front / delt / rais / contentiou (3)
- [1v 2020 P] His shoulder work: lots of overhead pressing, high-rep front raises for the front delt, and rear-delt accessories with relatively high weight. {{TJmOP7UCoQw@64}}
- [1v 2020 O] Front raises are contentious; he agrees most people can drop them since front delts get enough work, but he does them heavy because he has never had issues with them. {{IahVc0kbGOU@438}}
- [1v 2020 P] The only real front delt isolation is raises; lateral raises involve the front delt little when done properly and cause impingement when done badly. {{1P-3YblXBRI@62}}

## [shoulders] behind-the / neck / posture / bradford (3)
- [1v 2020 D] The Bradford press is a behind-the-neck press alternated with a front overhead press. {{zMCZ0vGzo6c@381}}
- [1v 2021 O] The behind-the-neck press uses the same shoulder position as gripping a low-bar squat; it's fine within your own pain-free range {{sFMQj4Lflk0@1508}}
- [1v 2024 O] Behind-the-neck presses force an exaggerated upright posture and so reinforce good posture {{okm6bZHKHI4@3555}}

## [shoulders] rear / delt / snatch / grip (3)
- [1v 2020 P] For heavy rear delt work choose a compound that involves them and modify it to accentuate them, such as snatch grip pulls from the floor or blocks, rather than heavy face pulls {{NJd_zD9OeQ4@130}}
- [1v 2020 P] Snatch grip lifts put the rear delts under a loaded stretch {{NJd_zD9OeQ4@65}}
- [1v 2023 P] Rear delt swings (heavy dumbbells, chest supported, small motions) are A tier for advanced rear delt work, learned from John Meadows; straps may be needed {{k3yRgcmiCls@3262}}

## [shoulders] delt / lateral / bias / side (3)
- [1v 2020 O] One-arm lateral raises leaning away from a pole are the best version because they remove the traps and focus on side and rear delts {{9jAjNyxeiXc@440}}
- [1v 2021 O] Band reverse laterals work the rear delt but are hard to load and tend to be junk volume {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@1760}}
- [1v 2022 P] Upright rows bias traps; lateral raises bias side delts {{SPicD3F686s@1065}}

## [shoulders] twice / allow / cheat / week (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Arnold raises on a bench allow lighter weight, strict form and no cheating, and are easy to recover from; they can be done twice a week for big shoulders {2021-07 {"frequency": "2x/wk"}} {{AFK9x4EZh2Q@441}}
- [1v 2024 P] Power raises can be dangerous so build up to them; they allow safe cheating and lengthened partials at the end of the set {{-OYtq2AHGX8@951}}
- [1v 2024 P] Do not repeat the exact same lateral raise twice a week; use a different variation {{6p2x4kDDjzs@3940}}

## [shoulders] 10-15 / raise / upright / manageable (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Treat the upright row like a lateral raise, 10-15 reps; few people ever need more than about 130 lb {2022-09 {"load": "<=130 lb", "reps": "10-15"}} {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@883}}
- [1v 2023 P] Upright rows are safe and A tier if done for 10-15 reps with a manageable weight {2023-02 {"reps": "10-15"}} {{k3yRgcmiCls@1948}}
- [1v 2024 P] Rotate lateral raise variations (cable, dumbbell) to keep progressing {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1839}}

## [shoulders] side-delt / tension / properly / upright (3)
- [1v 2024 P] Power raises are as close to pure side-delt isolation as possible, with peak tension at the bottom stretch, unlike cable laterals where the arm hits the body and tension dies {{-OYtq2AHGX8@888}}
- [1v 2024 P] Cable lateral raises with a cuff beat dumbbells: no grip limit, controlled negatives that don't speed up with fatigue, tension at the bottom, and less trap takeover, which matters if traps limit side delt growth {{xqjfYhZ-CvQ@577}}
- [1v 2024 O] Properly done upright rows are a good side-delt isolation {{c7hl9u3h_s0@5099}}

## [traps] trap / shrug / mediocre / shrimp (22)
- [1v 2020 A] He also uses trap-focused Yates row variations for heavy trap work. {{IgxJijW3MSQ@252}}
- [1v 2020 P] The deadlift's hardest part, getting past the knee, is posterior chain; a below-knee rack pull shifts emphasis to the upper back and traps {{de4yF1k6kzQ@502}}
- [1v 2021 A] He learned snatch-grip shrugs from Jeff Nippard and loves them {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1430}}
- [1v 2021 P] Shrugs: 4 sets of 10-15 every 3 days, or 4 light sets of 6-10 every two days {2021-03 {"reps": "10-15 or 6-10", "sets": 4}} {{1MOLCv6ojgw@0}}
- [1v 2021 P] Block pulls reduce lower-back demand and allow supramaximal loads that hit traps and middle back {{lG5SZbRdOjA@2076}}
- [1v 2021 P] 'Toshiki raises', copied from Toshiki Yamamoto, are a snatch-grip hang pull finished with a calf raise, an explosive trap/shoulder/calf movement supersetted with deadlifts {{jAnfGqpsK38@1540}}
- [1v 2022 O] Upright rows are safe for the shoulder if you know how to do them {{IYobXlRVLd0@809}}
- [1v 2022 P] Rack pull from below the knee, touch-and-go with a controlled negative, and hold the last rep until you can't; uses less weight than his deadlift and carries over to all pulls {{bPUrJ3CSqEA@1074}}
- [1v 2022 O] Snatch-grip shrugs are incredible for the yoke and worth trying {{g-pqgciOSgE@1574}}
- [1v 2023 O] Pendlay row is decent for traps because there is no controlled negative and no dangling stretch {{hRmjyBa64oE@1142}}
- [1v 2023 O] Dumbbell shrugs are only decent because dumbbells cap the load, even if they put the shoulder in a comfortable neutral position {{hRmjyBa64oE@564}}
- [1v 2023 O] Reeves incline row is Shrimp, Reeves deadlift mediocre, Reeves shrug decent; snatch-grip shrugs with straps beat them {{hRmjyBa64oE@1513}}
- [1v 2023 O] Overhead press is Shrimp tier for traps; shrugging at the top is a gimmick; overhead shrugs are mediocre {{hRmjyBa64oE@439}}
- [1v 2023 O] Above-the-knee rack pull is Leonidas tier: nearly double the deadlift load, so roughly double the trap work {2023-01 {"load": "~2x deadlift"}} {{hRmjyBa64oE@375}}
- [1v 2023 O] Band pull-aparts are Shrimp tier for traps but good for shoulder health; kettlebell swings are mediocre {{hRmjyBa64oE@1827}}
- [1v 2023 O] Lateral raises and bent-over lateral raises are mediocre for traps; they are shoulder exercises {{hRmjyBa64oE@1451}}
- [1v 2023 O] Rear-delt ring rows with a rope are decent; reverse ring flies are mediocre {{hRmjyBa64oE@1890}}
- [1v 2023 O] Reverse snow angels are Shrimp tier {{hRmjyBa64oE@1640}}
- [1v 2023 P] Cheat-style T-bar rows are mostly a hip hinge and work legs, posterior chain and yoke more than lats {2023-04 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 5}} {{-sf34ScWLK8@696}}
- [1v 2024 O] Kelso shrugs, especially on the Smith machine, are very effective; Jefferson curls cost a lot of technique but build the posterior chain {{bRKQIOYo2u4@11087}}
- [1v 2024 P] That more upright rowing variation hits the traps more than lats; a good builder for the whole upper back {{qvWxIzhT1QU@445}}
- [1v 2024 O] Tiny isolation movements for the upper thoracic back are not very effective; train it in the way that feels most effective {{qvWxIzhT1QU@885}}

## [traps] trap / carri / excellent / decent (8)
- [1v 2020 P] Loaded carries are excellent for traps, shoulders, upper back and forearms because they are a weighted stretch {{6sFaB3rAY1A@1131}}
- [1v 2023 O] Farmer's carries with a frame and straps are the best trap exercise: heavy load, and every step forces the traps to stretch and contract quickly to stabilize the shoulders {{hRmjyBa64oE@821}}
- [1v 2023 O] Deadlift is only decent for traps: lower back, glutes and hamstrings limit it, though years of heavy pulls do build big traps and upper backs {{hRmjyBa64oE@252}}
- [1v 2023 O] Face pulls can be modified to load the traps with more weight; decent tier, better stimulus-to-fatigue than the deadlift {{hRmjyBa64oE@375}}
- [1v 2023 O] Snatch-grip shrugs are excellent: more mid traps, upright so no lower-back fatigue, though the wide grip limits load {{hRmjyBa64oE@564}}
- [1v 2023 O] Dumbbell farmer's carries are only decent because grip and dumbbell weight limit them; frame holds are excellent {{hRmjyBa64oE@757}}
- [1v 2023 O] Behind-the-back barbell shrugs/curls are excellent for mid and lower traps, but some shoulders dislike them {{hRmjyBa64oE@1265}}
- [1v 2023 O] Overhead carries are Shrimp tier: the traps only stabilize passively {{hRmjyBa64oE@693}}

## [traps] hang / clean / power / snatch (6)
- [2v 2021-2022 OP] Hang cleans or hang power cleans 3-5x6-10 replace shrugs to add trap stretch and posterior chain work {2021-10 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": "3-5"} | 2022-10 {"reps": "8-10", "sets": 4}} also: 2022-10: Hang or power cleans are not that hard to learn; pick the one you enjoy most {{jAnfGqpsK38@1430}} {{CSwbuwl33eA@812}}
- [1v 2022 P] If knee power shrugs stress the lower back, switch to normal, seated, or chest-supported incline shrugs {{9y2s6kyFkPc@2128}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hang clean pulls are easy to learn, low impact, need little mobility, don't wreck recovery, teach exploding from the floor and build calves and traps {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@421}}
- [1v 2022 P] Hang clean pulls mimic the up-and-down movement of a takedown and build traps and explosiveness {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1890}}
- [1v 2023 O] Dumbbell/kettlebell snatch is mediocre; power clean is decent; hang clean pull is excellent; hang snatch pull is Leonidas tier (personal preference) {{hRmjyBa64oE@948}}
- [1v 2023 O] Upright row is mediocre for traps: essentially a strict hang snatch pull with less weight and more shoulders {{hRmjyBa64oE@1142}}

## [traps] shrug / superset / trap / bar (7)
- [1v 2020 P] Shrugs are easy to superset with anything if you want bigger traps {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@1500}}
- [1v 2020 P] Superset shrugs with hammer curls; there is no point resting after shrugs {{Lj0m82JUtkM@700}}
- [1v 2020 P] Superset stiff-legged deadlifts with shrugs using the same bar: finish the set, stand, squeeze glutes and shrug {{D3mJh0ZA5nc@1152}}
- [1v 2021 P] Superset OHP with shrugs 3-5x10-15: after pressing, lower the bar, add plates and shrug, since your shrug strength far exceeds your press strength {2021-10 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": "3-5"}} {{jAnfGqpsK38@240}}
- [1v 2023 P] Shrugs are a trap exercise, not shoulders, and do not belong on a shoulder list {{k3yRgcmiCls@2951}}
- [1v 2024 P] Don't superset RDLs with heavy shrugs: a fatigued upper back limits the posterior chain {{c7hl9u3h_s0@4778}}
- [1v 2024 O] Trap bar shrugs are an underrated movement, good for rugby {{6p2x4kDDjzs@3940}}

## [traps] trap / snatch-grip / add / compound (4)
- [1v 2020 P] Compounds like deadlifts, snatch-grip deadlifts, overhead press and pin press add trap volume without being trap isolation. {{IgxJijW3MSQ@188}}
- [1v 2023 P] Snatch-grip block pulls add upper back and yoke stimulus within leg work {2023-04 {"reps": 10, "sets": 4}} {{-sf34ScWLK8@1328}}
- [1v 2024 O] Big traps on top deadlifters come from the sheer volume of heavy pulling; the deadlift is only a C-tier trap builder {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@190}}
- [1v 2024 O] Trap isolation is not necessary since back compounds train traps, though trap-biased compounds can help {{ZLheQPgEfoA@8040}}

## [traps] catch / shrug / leonida / power (4)
- [1v 2023 O] Power shrugs are Leonidas tier: explode up and catch heavy weight; slow strict tempo shrugs are detrimental for trap growth {{hRmjyBa64oE@629}}
- [1v 2023 O] Kroc rows are Leonidas tier: body English and catching a dumbbell in front of the body hit the whole upper back {{hRmjyBa64oE@1203}}
- [1v 2023 O] Cable shrugs are Shrimp tier because the cable removes the catch and stretch {{hRmjyBa64oE@1265}}
- [1v 2024 O] Versus a barbell high pull, the power shrug is always the better trap choice {{TvKzeUKgZcY@827}}

## [traps] farmer' / walk / gpp / wish (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Weighted carries like farmer's walks fit an arms/GPP day because everything is warmed up {{2ag0uM9zhs4@1410}}
- [1v 2022 P] Farmer's walks help handle a wrestler's pinning pressure and build traps, grip and upper back; they pair well with kettlebell swings {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@1952}}
- [1v 2022 A] He wishes he could do farmer's walks but cannot; if squats leave you too tired, swap them for shrugs or farmer's holds {{IYobXlRVLd0@621}}

## [traps] strap / trap / target / shrug (3)
- [1v 2021 P] Shrugs finish the demon back; change torso angle to target upper, middle or lower traps; use straps for back work except pull-ups {{1MOLCv6ojgw@350}}
- [1v 2023 O] Chest-supported incline shrugs are Leonidas tier: the stationary body maximizes trap stimulus, heavy dumbbells with straps, and adjusting the incline targets each area {{hRmjyBa64oE@1764}}
- [1v 2024 O] Farmer walks without straps build grip and with straps build massive traps {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1269}}

## [triceps] tricep / elbow / close-grip / extension (52)
- [2v 2020 P] The football bar puts the emphasis of the bench press on the triceps {{wTisNk9Av6Y@63}} {{4ORCb_i7MxY@497}}
- [2v 2023 O] SSB without handles and the Smith machine are ideal for JM presses; the Smith version is the triceps isolation he can push hardest to true failure also: 2023-11: The Smith machine is great for JM presses, and the SSB bar seems built for them {{DA7QvIAyptw@1452}} {{DAZRYoex8_w@444}}
- [1v 2020 P] Use it mainly for bench press, but also push-ups and dips; with push-ups it allows tons of reps while sparing the shoulders and loading the triceps. {{Pr6to3cPR1g@157}}
- [1v 2020 P] Overhead and behind-the-neck extensions are very important for arm development, but a straight barbell demands elbow and wrist mobility novices lack; use an EZ bar or dumbbells. {{Tw8qA93Gf58@441}}
- [1v 2020 P] You can pick from a short list of triceps exercises, but choosing randomly on the day is too much freedom. {{z9MDwMO8K4Y@572}}
- [1v 2020 O] Dead hangs may be underrated for arm growth because of the stretch they put on the arms, e.g. the triceps, not just the lats. {{2edX06SQDnU@506}}
- [1v 2020 A] He does bench strength work with a slingshot to make the lockout relatively harder and protect his damaged shoulder, accepting some lost chest stimulus {{wTisNk9Av6Y@63}}
- [1v 2020 O] Standing barbell triceps extensions stress the wrists and give little stretch {{PFQ30yaL1t0@754}}
- [1v 2020 P] After the giant sets, do a less taxing press variation relevant to your main press (close-grip bench, incline press, Viking press) for 4 sets of 6-10 reps, supersetted with an arm isolation of choice. {2020-06 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": 4}} {{o3RTXWAia4A@886}}
- [1v 2020 P] Close-grip bench works the chest through a longer range; open arm days with a compound like close-grip, pin or board press {2020-07 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{eaXDVkHjsZQ@876}}
- [1v 2020 O] He prefers 6-10 reps on close grip bench, but bench is the compound where he accepts low reps most, especially close grip: it is shoulder-safe and if the triceps fail the bar just comes down {2020-07 {"reps": "6-10"}} {{YcHkXwsIOI8@382}}
- [1v 2020 P] The epidemic of torso-dominant lifters comes from powerlifting-style programs: bench hits chest, front delts and the shorter triceps heads, deadlifts grow the back, neither grows the arms {{Tfa-eM5LUV0@378}}
- [1v 2020 P] The floor press shortens range unless your leverages are good, making it more triceps-specific {{i4YmJhlw7cs@124}}
- [1v 2020 O] Real dips are probably better than a dip machine {{eobG2o3Ww4M@936}}
- [1v 2020 O] Close-grip bench is a low-impact press you can hardly overdo {{niD0UuwTUvg@192}}
- [1v 2020 P] Find pairings that balance endurance, performance, progression and injury prevention, e.g. a press followed by a long-head triceps exercise, or a press followed by face pulls {{ZScs2L9rero@790}}
- [1v 2021 O] Close grip bench mostly works the chest, so picking it as a triceps exercise alongside flat bench doubles chest work {{A55c7v1yMn4@1308}}
- [1v 2021 P] An arm day is a good place for bench, horizontal press or vertical press variations, because it is not taxing and everything can be supersetted {{RSkzA1l5jlk@780}}
- [1v 2021 P] Two kinds of pushdowns back to back hit the same area; pick one {{BnzVYV9_IQc@441}}
- [1v 2021 O] EZ-bar close-grip press with a monkey grip is a good backup movement and pairs well after skullcrushers, but it is not strength-work material and doesn't beat a standard barbell close-grip bench; watch elbow comfort {{D1_tXh-Kp9Y@195}}
- [1v 2021 O] A paused skullcrusher maximizes the long-head stretch behind the head; paused pull-ups only work if the scapulae stay retracted at the bottom {{MP01uQbcoLc@1366}}
- [1v 2022 P] Sphinx push-ups (pushing up from the elbows onto the hands) remove shoulder flexion and work the triceps nearly in isolation with bodyweight {{vyKzL85QwY4@574}}
- [1v 2022 A] Deficit diamond push-ups increase range of motion, are harder on the triceps and give NH just the right amount of shoulder work {{vyKzL85QwY4@129}}
- [1v 2022 O] Diamond push-ups to AMRAP will give heavy shoulder DOMS but help shoulder mobility without risk of injury {{3JNwlqx3Z6w@969}}
- [1v 2022 O] Putting presses and close-grip bench at the end, after all the isolation, is backwards scheduling {{uYRIWAKlA0c@1949}}
- [1v 2022 P] Triceps pushdowns or chest flies 4x10-15; women will not get bulky arms, it just firms the back of the arm {2022-06 {"reps": "10-15", "sets": 4}} {{CVHNXyR5yCY@751}}
- [1v 2022 O] Swiss/football bar bench lets the elbows travel further back for more triceps; close-grip on it could be excellent tier {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1193}}
- [1v 2022 O] Machine triceps extensions on chest machines, assisted dip machines and seated dip machines are mediocre; assisted dips are at best a finisher {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2382}}
- [1v 2022 P] Any setup that forces acrobatic positions (like the standing rope push-away fighting the stack) loses points; the kneeling cable extension gives the same angle comfortably {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1631}}
- [1v 2022 P] Finding your ideal kneeling cable extension setup takes several workouts of experimenting, but once found you can keep it for life {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1758}}
- [1v 2022 O] Bodyweight triceps extensions build the long head but rely on balance, which is risky for a fragile, tendinitis-prone elbow; fine for skilled calisthenics athletes {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1380}}
- [1v 2022 P] Toothpick-tier exercises are dangerous for the elbow, tendinitis-prone, or barely work the triceps {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@65}}
- [1v 2022 P] Cables suit how the triceps function and spare the tendon, but give less overall stimulus and get boring; mix excellent free-weight and mythical cable movements {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2320}}
- [1v 2022 O] The Tate press combines everything wrong with presses and extensions: limited ROM, bad for wrist, stress on tendon; historically used for shoulders not triceps {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1946}}
- [1v 2022 O] Dumbbell kickbacks are garbage and a noob trap: unbalanced, tension only at the top; if you like the motion, use cables which provide tension throughout {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1820}}
- [1v 2022 P] Tier criteria: mythical-tier triceps exercises work all three heads properly, are low fatigue for the elbow, can be spammed, are fun and easy to set up {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@127}}
- [1v 2022 P] Lying triceps extension with the shoulder in full flexion (bar behind the head) is his favorite and the only free-weight mythical-tier exercise {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2508}}
- [1v 2022 P] Reverse-grip pushdowns are underrated; letting the arm rise as you extend brings in plenty of long head, but the wrist can become the limiting factor {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@438}}
- [1v 2022 P] If grip can become a limiting factor, a triceps movement loses points {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1318}}
- [1v 2022 P] Cross-body cable extension is good (not the best): works all three heads, very elbow friendly; use a proper handle, not the bare carabiner {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2071}}
- [1v 2022 O] The standing EZ-bar overhead extension is mostly ego lifting: you can almost double the weight because shoulders turn it into a press {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@690}}
- [1v 2022 P] Pick triceps exercises that are tendon-friendly, comfortable and progressable long term, and that together develop all three heads {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2762}}
- [1v 2022 O] Bench dips are a noob exercise: awkward wrists, internal rotation, no good stretch or squeeze; intermediates have all moved on {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1443}}
- [1v 2022 O] The bench press is not a triceps builder; failing a lockout is due to poor chest velocity, not weak triceps {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2698}}
- [1v 2022 O] Floor press trains lockout but lockout does not grow triceps as much as people think {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2446}}
- [1v 2023 A] The JM press, picked up from Basement Bodybuilding, Alex Leonidas and Bald Omni-Man, has helped his elbow pain {{43F9unmSc_I@567}}
- [1v 2024 P] For maximally challenging the triceps, a pushdown beats the bench press; isolation is strictly superior for growing specific arm muscles {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@440}}
- [1v 2024 P] Very few people are triceps-dominant on bench; leverage favors chest and shoulders, so bench strength carries over little to triceps size {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@504}}
- [1v 2024 P] If you only bench and overhead press, 100% of your triceps gains come from them, which proves nothing about their effectiveness {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@189}}
- [1v 2024 O] JM presses are polarising, but many report they rescued their elbows {{c7hl9u3h_s0@5224}}
- [1v 2024 P] Soreness is a practical tool to find which muscle a lift hits, e.g. some people get sore triceps from pullovers {{6yW3GSskl8g@697}}
- [1v 2024 O] The conjugate system is decent for hypertrophy and Louis Simmons was one of the best non-bodybuilding coaches; Westside lifters had great triceps, so bodybuilders should copy good methods even from non-bodybuilders {{Plr3f3LXO_o@197}}

## [triceps] skull / crusher / head / long (13)
- [2v 2020-2021 OP] Close grip bench and skull crushers are not interchangeable: close grip is a horizontal press hitting lateral and medial triceps heads plus chest and shoulders, skull crushers with shoulder flexion target the long head also: 2020-10: NH dislikes the JM press as a hybrid lift that's hard on the elbows; use skull crushers for long head and close-grip bench for lateral/medial heads {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1351}} {{-anA4CV2SVU@440}}
- [2v 2022-2023 OP] The JM press combines the best of skull crusher and close-grip bench, building long head with carryover to pressing; start with very light weight to learn the form {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@879}} {{DAZRYoex8_w@319}}
- [1v 2020 P] Add long-head triceps work: swap day A extensions for skull crushers and day B cable crunches for behind-the-head cable extensions. {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@1568}}
- [1v 2020 P] For the long head, do skull crushers letting the shoulders flex back past vertical so the lats engage; he considers this the superior version {{wTisNk9Av6Y@189}}
- [1v 2020 P] Don't rotate a compound with an isolation in the same slot (close-grip bench vs skull crushers); they aren't equivalent and wreck tonnage tracking {{niD0UuwTUvg@129}}
- [1v 2020 P] Do both close-grip bench and skull crushers as a zero-rest superset; it won't hurt strength or recovery {{niD0UuwTUvg@192}}
- [1v 2021 A] Pairing a horizontal press with an elbow extension like skull crushers improves his performance on both {{XncJr9uMpWo@494}}
- [1v 2022 O] The skull crusher was meant as a long-head isolation exercise, but powerlifting coaches turned it into a compound press {{traEzBUl8aU@439}}
- [1v 2022 O] The conventional skull crusher with upper arms vertical is still fine; his version is for maximising long-head stretch {{traEzBUl8aU@691}}
- [1v 2022 P] V-bar pushdowns remain a standard because they let you load heavy safely; best for the lateral head, they still recruit some long head {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@941}}
- [1v 2022 P] Dumbbell skull crushers beat EZ-bar ones because the dumbbells can travel past the head for a deeper stretch {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2071}}
- [1v 2023 P] The bench works chest, front delts and mostly the lateral triceps head; the long head is largely ignored {{elk4uYvjXos@253}}
- [1v 2023 P] Close-hand push-ups with fingers forward hammer the triceps long and lateral heads, with failure in the triceps rather than chest or shoulders {{P7ADeDVghxg@676}}

## [triceps] close-grip / adds / volume / mid-way (3)
- [2v 2020 OP] Close-grip bench is a good bench-builder: it adds horizontal pressing volume, spares the shoulders, and carries over to the regular bench in both directions. also: 2020-06: Close-grip bench adds pressing volume without taxing the shoulders and adds triceps work {{mViZdjZIv6s@767}} {{PYmr91aELnE@913}}
- [1v 2020 P] Close-grip bench doesn't tax the shoulder much; both close-grip bench and mid-way paused dips shift work to the triceps and are done with less weight. {2020-03 {"reps": "6-10", "sets": "4"}} {{b4rMVrWFVEg@314}}
- [1v 2020 O] Close-grip bench 3x8 on day B is good: extra horizontal press volume without smoking the shoulders, synergising with day A bench. {2020-06 {"reps": 8, "sets": 3}} {{5Wz0xTfWOOs@1004}}

## [triceps] dips / tricep / shift / removable (4)
- [1v 2020 O] Dips after bench, incline press and OHP are the most removable exercise; very vertical dips shift more work to the triceps {{dHXPhjaWBWk@314}}
- [1v 2022 P] Triceps dips with a vertical torso, only doing the top portion, shift load to the triceps; do not go deep or you risk the shoulders {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2258}}
- [1v 2024 P] Dips late in a day after pressing and triceps are fine as bodyweight AMRAP, but weighted dips belong earlier {{c7hl9u3h_s0@4338}}
- [1v 2024 O] Dips and pull-ups are S-tier; overloading weighted dips changed his mind and grew his triceps {{c7hl9u3h_s0@1776}}

## [triceps] head / long / overhead / extension (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Superset bench press with overhead dumbbell extensions: the triceps long head is not recruited in pressing, and supersetting long-head work with presses has made him stronger {{GClcGq2tIDU@504}}
- [1v 2021 O] Close-grip push-ups followed by overhead extensions is a good superset: lateral head then long head of the triceps {{BnzVYV9_IQc@441}}
- [1v 2022 P] The overhead cable extension can be progressed for two years to build a huge long head without problems: no rebound, no head collision, freer shoulder path {2022-10 {"weeks": 104}} {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@252}}

## [triceps] dips / slingshot / tricep / lean (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Triceps dips: slingshot, half reps, torso as vertical as possible, then lean forward as triceps tire to bring in chest and shoulders; 4 sets of 30, progressing toward 50 reps {2020-06 {"reps": "30 -> 50", "sets": 4}} {{wTisNk9Av6Y@314}}
- [1v 2022 O] Properly done dips with forward lean are mostly chest and shoulders; only doing dips for triceps will disappoint {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1257}}
- [1v 2023 P] Slingshot weighted dips deload the bottom and shoulder, skewing the work to triceps {2023-04 {"load": "+80 kg", "reps": "5-8", "sets": 4}} {{-sf34ScWLK8@571}}

## [triceps] kickback / useles / importance / tricep (3)
- [1v 2020 O] Ditch triceps kickbacks: gravity makes them poor for progression, they may hurt tendons and don't fit any movement pattern {{aaQr-Av0x9Y@610}}
- [1v 2021 O] Incline dumbbell kickbacks are useless {{_L5wLHlFfI0@689}}
- [1v 2021 O] Triceps kickbacks are at the bottom of programming importance {{qq459xqmq5A@930}}

## [triceps] french / 8-12 / elbow / adjust (3)
- [1v 2020 P] Dumbbell overhead press 8-12 for 4 sets superset with French press 6-10 (8-12 or 10-15 also work); never go below 5 reps on French press {2020-10 {"reps": "8-12/6-10", "sets": 4}} {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1462}}
- [1v 2022 O] The French press is easy on the elbows for most people; adjust torso angle or hand position to fit your build {{traEzBUl8aU@439}}
- [1v 2022 P] The dumbbell French press is excellent but run heavy for too long it inevitably causes elbow tendinitis; done with the back against an incline bench it becomes mythical {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@189}}

## [triceps] alone / tricep / grow / close-grip (3)
- [1v 2020 P] If a body part does not grow from compounds you must isolate it; those who grow from compounds alone, e.g. triceps from close-grip bench, are anomalies {{3J-zADEbtqU@125}}
- [1v 2022 P] Close-grip bench alone gives disappointing triceps for most people; it is the best horizontal press for triceps but compounds prioritize the strongest muscles (chest, shoulders) {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@315}}
- [1v 2024 P] Everyone who gets stronger on pushdowns gets bigger triceps; the minority who grow huge triceps from bench alone are outliers {{GyK1Y6WTR-g@504}}

## [triceps] tendiniti / elbow / near / pushdown (3)
- [1v 2022 P] Rotate V-bar and rope pushdowns: near one-to-one carryover while the slight differences in elbow angle prevent tendinitis {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@1131}}
- [1v 2022 O] Floor triceps extension keeps elbows pointing up at the bottom: tendinitis risk and poor stretch {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@2197}}
- [1v 2023 D] A band's ascending resistance puts most tension near lockout, making band pushdowns easy on the elbows while hitting the long head {{awwJ5sbyz6A@506}}

## Possible contradictions / changes over time (25 flagged by script; verify)
- (stance flips) 2022-02: Always open a back day with a heavy hip hinge or heavy pull (e.g. heavy row from the floor or heavy pull-up variation), never a light pulldown or machine, since you are freshest at the start {{wLx5BRx8AaU@1071}} {{bJ7gfILR6RA@628}} ⟷ 2023-01: You can open a back and biceps day with a heavy row/pull instead for more stimulus {{BiVfpZEchws@187}}
- (stance flips) 2020-10: Close grip bench and skull crushers are not interchangeable: close grip is a horizontal press hitting lateral and medial triceps heads plus chest and shoulders, skull crushers with shoulder flexion target the long head {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1351}} {{-anA4CV2SVU@440}} ⟷ 2023-12: The JM press combines the best of skull crusher and close-grip bench, building long head with carryover to pressing; start with very light weight to learn the form {{ylmTf9Qb6jg@879}} {{DAZRYoex8_w@319}}
- (stance flips) 2020-08: Prefer pull-ups, then weighted pull-ups, over lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine but should never be the main strength lift or open the workout {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@430}} ⟷ 2020-12: Replace lat pulldowns with chin-ups for better results; pulldowns are inferior for hypertrophy, use light ones only as an accessory {{eobG2o3Ww4M@500}} {{DQo0DGn_wI4@1000}}
- (stance flips) 2020-09: Bodybuilding pull-ups are the only vertical pull you need for upper back development {{uyTgjl3miAE@0}} ⟷ 2023-12: Do not treat the back as one muscle; it needs several movement patterns: hip hinges, vertical pulls and horizontal pulls {{qDH088UUiMA@37}} {{hEuy__sCbZA@569}}
- (stance flips) 2020-08: Prefer pull-ups, then weighted pull-ups, over lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine but should never be the main strength lift or open the workout {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@430}} ⟷ 2020-10: NH dislikes lat pulldowns; pull-ups are better for hypertrophy, especially weighted {{TD9SB9Bt45o@160}}
- (stance flips) 2020-11: The lifts are the skeleton of a program; volume, intensity and frequency are built around them, not the other way round {{gWdAfpJIXbc@373}} ⟷ 2021-06: Build any good program around the lifts first, then set volume, intensity and frequency; choosing a 5x5 and then a lift is backwards {{7n8RMhsxEB4@313}}
- (stance flips) 2020-05: Superset bench press with overhead dumbbell extensions: the triceps long head is not recruited in pressing, and supersetting long-head work with presses has made him stronger {{GClcGq2tIDU@504}} ⟷ 2021-03: Close-grip push-ups followed by overhead extensions is a good superset: lateral head then long head of the triceps {{BnzVYV9_IQc@441}}
- (stance flips) 2020-12: Always open the training day with your strength work {{DQo0DGn_wI4@150}} ⟷ 2022-12: Always open with the compound and put isolation after, not before {{0pKnDucWcDc@1065}}
- (stance flips) 2020-04: Never squat in the Smith machine. {{T_DyRlu3Y7I@505}} ⟷ 2022-01: Advanced lifters can use the Smith machine for rows and front squats because balance is taken care of {{O5xVdqgt8Uo@2056}}
- (stance flips) 2021-05: Do not treat the back as one muscle; it needs several movement patterns: hip hinges, vertical pulls and horizontal pulls {{qDH088UUiMA@37}} {{hEuy__sCbZA@569}} ⟷ 2023-11: A proper back day is one vertical pull, one horizontal pull and one shrug pattern; 6-7 back exercises is bro-split garbage {{DA7QvIAyptw@1261}}
- (stance flips) 2020-10: Do not do leg press and hack squat on the same day; pick one, progress it, rotate if bored {{6kRRzKx7lKs@1978}} ⟷ 2024-11: Smith squat plus leg press on the same day is redundant; pick one {{6p2x4kDDjzs@1763}}
- (stance flips) 2021-02: Arm days built only from cable isolation are not how you grow {{K1GVimJowoM@445}} ⟷ 2024-10: An upper body built only from compounds with no arm isolation leads to imbalance {{c7hl9u3h_s0@3387}}
- (stance flips) 2020-02: Pull-ups and chin-ups aren't just a vertical pull, they are THE vertical pull; pulldowns are not an equivalent substitute. {{OATtf3kPtdI@1001}} ⟷ 2024-09: The lat pulldown is the best pull-up substitute and is worth rotating in even if you can do pull-ups {{bRKQIOYo2u4@1729}}
- (stance flips) 2023-07: Low-bar squats are B tier for bodybuilders, only for those who cannot high-bar; more weight does not mean more quad recruitment {{_IBSNO56zuE@2640}} ⟷ 2024-11: Low-bar squat is hard to justify for a bodybuilder unless no hack squat or high-bar squat is possible; low-bar squat plus RDL loads the lower back heavily {{6p2x4kDDjzs@4422}}
- (stance flips) 2020-10: Hammer curls after wrist curls at 6-10 for 3 sets so mechanical failure does not limit him; replace with pinwheel curls if shoulders dislike the position {{cGzlMRCxvxo@1640}} ⟷ 2022-08: He prefers pinwheel curls to hammer curls because hammer curls in front of the body over-recruit the front delt {{_KypKq9Hbe0@590}}
- (stance flips) 2020-11: Opening with a horizontal press then following with a vertical press is NH's favorite way to program a bench day and good for shoulder health {{hT4zgNcMOBY@752}} {{62OBPecQPnM@251}} ⟷ 2022-05: The bench press grew his chest, but it is not the only horizontal press {{k9_dDbGLFZQ@434}}
- (numbers differ: reps 8-10 vs 6-10) 2020-10: Overhead press is preferred for shoulders; Viking press is fine if you like the neutral grip {{TD9SB9Bt45o@1330}} ⟷ 2022-10: No straight horizontal press on upper 2 because Zodd's shoulders dwarf his chest; use incline or Viking press (Viking press only if you need no chest work) {{CSwbuwl33eA@622}}
- (stance flips) 2020-02: Pull-ups and chin-ups are non-negotiable: they don't tax the CNS, decompress the spine and teach beginners to pull with the back for rows and deadlifts. {{OATtf3kPtdI@875}} ⟷ 2021-01: Weighted pull-ups after deadlifts help decompress the spine {{pey1Ni6HPlY@510}}
- (stance flips) 2021-01: With 'row of choice', pick one row type and stay in one tree of specificity {{cNUwR9TmVRs@63}} ⟷ 2024-11: He is not a fan of the seated cable row; a dumbbell row or a pullover is a better choice {{6p2x4kDDjzs@1511}}
- (stance flips) 2020-08: Prefer pull-ups, then weighted pull-ups, over lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine but should never be the main strength lift or open the workout {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@430}} ⟷ 2024-09: The lat pulldown is the best pull-up substitute and is worth rotating in even if you can do pull-ups {{bRKQIOYo2u4@1729}}
- (stance flips) 2021-01: A lat pulldown should not be the strength lift; start pull day with a hinge, then a vertical pull or row {{pey1Ni6HPlY@1347}} ⟷ 2022-05: Include at least one heavy vertical pull; use lat pulldowns if you can't do weighted pull-ups {{SPicD3F686s@878}}
- (stance flips) 2020-08: Prefer pull-ups, then weighted pull-ups, over lat pulldowns; pulldowns are fine but should never be the main strength lift or open the workout {{4ORCb_i7MxY@122}} {{pey1Ni6HPlY@430}} ⟷ 2023-09: Pull-ups are preferable as the vertical pull, but a lat pulldown is fine if you cannot do them yet; aim to get to pull-ups for later weighted work {{7uBT8vp0lNw@194}}
- (stance flips) 2020-11: Boring But Big is a powerlifting program, not a hypertrophy program; it will make you look like a powerlifter {{hT4zgNcMOBY@942}} ⟷ 2021-03: Cutting ~20% of the Big 3 volume to add diversity would make programs better and less boring {{S0qBQppUtTQ@1379}}
- (stance flips) 2020-10: He is not a fan of unilateral leg movements but does not call them bad {{dHXPhjaWBWk@689}} ⟷ 2024-04: Athletes should place unilateral movements after the knee flexion and hip hinge, done fatigued with less weight and higher reps, instead of leg extensions {{yvhW-zyhpsM@881}} {{Ft039H8jzXU@1695}}
- (stance flips) 2021-05: Do not treat the back as one muscle; it needs several movement patterns: hip hinges, vertical pulls and horizontal pulls {{qDH088UUiMA@37}} {{hEuy__sCbZA@569}} ⟷ 2024-02: For a bigger back, do heavy vertical and horizontal pulls with a stretch and felt tension {{ZRNSc-xBVnc@380}}
